Summary

Ends are often written before beginnings.

or: Kate and Yelena meet when they need it the most, but sometimes you have to learn to be ready for it.

Notes

i've had these two on my mind since i watched Hawkeye, so here is my first ever attempt at actually publishing something! i've been updating this on my tumblr before getting an AO3 account, so today there will be 3 chapters all at once.

You keep me grounded (I'll keep you still)

by tardexlanoche

https://archiveofourown.org/works/36481771

    Chapter 1

    please keep in mind that I've seen most of the MCU movies pretty casualy, meaning that while i do know most of the characters, i will probably mess around with their stories/characteristics, hence the AU.

    also, i always write with background music so i'll give you a song to each chapter, it doesn't necessarily mean it's related to the content but this way i'll manage to create a playlist to this fic.

    this first one is Algo Contigo - Rita Payés & Elisabeth Roma I hope you enjoy X I - Algo Contigo It’s her first day of class, and she’s already late.

    “Excuse me, ma’am, could you help me? I need to find the lecture hall where Dr. Myriad is giving the International Law master’s introduction, but it doesn’t say where we’re supposed to go in the schedule,” Kate begs, practically shoving her phone on the poor woman’s face, “I’m really late, I can’t keep wandering around”.

    The clerk adjusts her glasses further up her nose and squints at the screen, “Oh dear, that lecture is to be given in the Aularium, see?” She zooms in a small red footnote at the corner of the document, where it clearly states that all the master’s classes will indeed take place in the Central Aularium.

    The Central Aularium, which happens to be at about fifteen minutes away – running - from Law School, where she is currently standing.

    It’s her first day of class and she’s already late. And nowhere near to where she is supposed to be.

    Kate takes off so quickly she almost forgets her phone, barely shouting a “Thanks!” above her shoulder.

    She’s close to the building when she sees a big ass sign with blue, bold letters welcoming the new I.L. master’s students, clearly indicating where her lecture is scheduled.

    Yeah, great help now. Assholes.

    “Wait!” someone yells behind her, making her stop and turn around. A guy around seemingly around her age is running after her almost out of breath. She wonders if she dropped something on her way here, checking her pockets frantically for her phone.

    “You’re also late to Myriad’s introduction, right? I overheard you back there,” says the boy now stopping in front of her, a little out of breath, “We’re classmates. Name’s Peter,” he says extending a hand.

    Kate shakes it hurriedly, giving him a small smile, “Kate. Nice to meet you, Peter. Ready to be -” she stops to check her watch “twenty-two minutes late on our first day?”

    “It’s totally my thing.”

    She pushes the door with so much force she worries for a minute about denting the wall with it. Mom would love to hear about that, she thinks fleetly to herself, focusing now on finding a way to enter the classroom as discreetly as possible.

    “Okay, any ideas?” whispers Peter to her right from where he is peeking through the window. The professor is already writing on the board, the rest of their classmates taking notes or tipping away on their computers.

    She thinks of the feel of eyes on her, the burning sensation in her cheeks when she is the center of attention, and curses herself. Shit.

    “Yes, I say we confidently leave now and just come to the next class,” Kate answers pitifully, but before she has time to turn around a lithe body crashes into her.

    “Ow, sorry” a sweet, high-pitched voice apologizes while Kate takes a couple of steps back, adding distance between her and her aggressor. It just turns out to be a very cute, redhead aggressor.

    “Fuck me, the class has already started?”

    A very cute, redhead aggressor with a very cute accent who also happens to be late.

    “Yeah, welcome to the club,” answers Peter in camaraderie, “I’m Peter, and the one you just pushed into the wall is Kate,” he says pointing at her. The mention of her name makes her blink, and she forces herself to get her shit together. Stop being a fucking mess when there’s a pretty girl around, Bishop, she berates to herself. She lifts her hand and gives what she hopes is a welcoming smile to the girl, who thankfully looks just as flustered as Kate feels.

    “Oh god, yes. Sorry about that, I was so worried about making a fool of myself in front of our class that I didn’t even see you” she says sheepishly, “I’m Wanda, and I guess I’m now a member of your late club.”

    “Cool, at least we get to know each other before we’re thrown in there” replies Peter tilting his head to the closed door behind them.

    “Nice to meet you, Wanda,” finally, her brain seems to regain its normal functioning, “I was just telling Peter we should ditch introduction and come later to our first class, what do you say?”

    “Introductions are always just nonsense talk, anyways. We can just ask someone later if Myriad said something relevant,” Peter joins her cause, and she can already tell they’ll get along fine alright.

    Wanda bites her lip casting a glance through the door’s window, but the corners of her mouth turn up in a second, “Well… My best friend’s inside, so I guess I can pass whatever notes she takes to you guys. We’re already more than half an hour late, anyways.”

    “Awesome,” Peter does a kind of lame kind of cute fist pump in the air and links both his arms through theirs, “Let’s go then, ladies!”

    They end up sitting on some stairs drinking black, sour coffee from a machine, but it’s nice. She fins out Peter studied law in New York, too, just a couple of years ahead of her, and that Wanda came to the U.S. as a refugee from Sokovia, went to a community college, and was so amazingly bright she was awarded a scholarship here. The conversation flows naturally with Peter’s energy and Kate’s blabbermouth tendencies, and though Wanda seems to be more on the introverted spectrum, she holds her own.

    Despite what her open and friendly behavior may make it seem, Kate hasn’t always had the easiest of times making friends since she left school. There, everybody knew each other since practically kindergarten, but once she got to high school things started getting kinda hard for her.

    So right now, Kate feels kind of glad she didn’t see those stupid red letters on the schedule.

    “Okay, let’s get moving girls. We don’t want to be late again,” Peter says after checking his phone and adjusting his backpack, helping both of them to stand up.

    As they approach the classroom again, Wanda seems to get a bit worried, “Do you guys think we’ll get in trouble for skipping introduction?”

    “I hope not, I didn’t even know there would be one.”

    The three of them turn to the source of the voice, a girl around their age sitting on the floor, legs crossed in front of her and back to the wall. She seems to be fighting with her hair, curly brunette strands escaping her attempt at a ponytail. Kate may not have exactly the same curls, but she totally can relate to the frustrated huff their new companion makes before giving up.

    “Well, we did and we missed it anyways,” Kate shrugs, and remembering her manners adds, “My name’s Kate. They are Wanda and Peter.”

    “M.J.”

    “Nice to meet you, M.J.,” Wanda shakes her hand and wiggles her fingers with a cute smile scrunching her nose, now seemingly more relaxed seeing yet another classmate missing their first period. Power in numbers and all that, Kate guesses, “I’ll ask my friend if there’s anything we need to know, and I’ll let you know.”

    “Cool, Wanda. Thanks,” says M.J. standing up, offering a hand up for the redhead to high-

    five.

    Kate watches the exchange smiling and shaking her head, turning her head to share a wasn’t that cute look with Peter when she suddenly sees the boy almost petrified next to her. His eyes - fixated on their new friend - almost bulge out of their orbits, his hands firm around the straps of his backpack, knuckles white with force. His words seem to have abandoned him, as she now realizes he hasn’t spoken since they introduced themselves, not even to say hi.

    Needless to say, Kate takes this great opportunity to mess with her classmate, “If you keep looking she will notice your little… problem,” she whispers next to his ear, pointing subtly to his pants.

    That seems to snap him out of his trance, quickly letting go of the backpack to cover his groin on instinct only to realize a second later that it didn’t get to be that kind of obvious. He punches her on the arm, “Fuck you, Kate. Not funny,” but even then, he can’t help but let a nervous laugh go.

    The other two girls stop their conversation probably to see what they are bickering about, but just then the classroom door opens and students start getting out. The four of them nod at each other and Wanda, unsurprisingly, ends up being the bravest one entering first.

    It ends up not being a great deal at all. Their professor barely castes them a glance as they go through the door, more focused on turning the class projector on.

    “Well, totally a good decision getting that coffee,” nods Kate with a self-satisfied smile. Peter claps her shoulder and Wanda winks at her, and all the dread that she had been feeling these past days about starting anew in this city has now melted away giving pass to a giddy happiness. She’s managed to score two almost friends in just the first hour without making a fool of herself, yay her.

    “Hey, my friend’s over there. Let’s go sit with her, see what they talked about,” Wanda points to the fourth row, left corner, and Kate loses her breath a bit.

    See, she has met tons of pretty women in her life, she has even had the chance to be with some of them, but this girl… She might quite certainly be the most beautiful woman Kate has ever seen.

    Her blonde hair hangs down her side in a perfect braid, some loose strands of hair framing her heart-shaped face perfectly. Bold, pink plump lips pursed as she mindlessly scrolls through her phone, chin resting on one hand as if she were posing, her high cheekbones resembling those of a renaissance painting. She has an untouchable aura around her, like she is not interested at all in what’s happening around her, like she’s actually gracing them with her presence there.

    She totally is, though.

    And when she looks up, Kate swears those big green eyes steal all the light in the room.

    For a second she worries this magnificent stranger is looking at her - because with this creepy staring she has going on, she as well might - but luckily her eyes trail after Wanda, clearly happy to see her friend as the first sign of emotion crosses her face.

    And oh god, she has dimples when she smiles.

    A chuckle to her right snaps her out of her admiration, “Well, who would be in deep trouble now if she was a dude?” breathes lowly Peter, mockingly hiding his mouth behind his cupped hand. The burning cheeks Kate was worrying about earlier come with vengeance, worsening when her classmate starts almost choking on his laugh.

    “Shut up.”

    “Great comeback, Romeo. Glad to see blondie over there didn’t steal your tongue,” and with that, Kate does give him a good deserved shove. It only makes him laugh more, but well. At least now she has something else to focus on.

    “Come on, Kate. Let’s find out the name of our new friend,” and if it weren’t for his arm around her shoulders, Kate is pretty sure she would have spent the rest of the day firmly planted on the second stair, not moving one bit. The predicament of having to talk to this girl seems quite terrifying.

    When they make it to the fourth row, Wanda is already talking to the girl, M.J. next to them listening politely. And with Peter’s not subtle at all throat clearing, the three girls turn their heads to them.

    Wanda smiles and places a delicate hand on her friend’s arm to get her attention, “And those are Peter and Kate, my other late-to-class club mates,” she says pointing at them.

    Peter lifts his hand and salutes the girls, and Kate would totally make fun of him if she could concentrate on something else than not tripping over her own feet while standing still. She manages what she hopes comes out as a friendly smile and a curt hi, before Wanda officially introduces her, “Guys, this is my friend, first-class-atendant and savior, -”

    “Yelena. Nice to meet you. And don’t worry, you didn’t miss much.”

    Okay, Kate was not expecting that voice at all. A deep, rich tone - can a voice remind you of chocolate?- also subtly marked with a foreign accent. She wonders if perhaps she and Wanda are actually from the same place. Is that how they meet? And is she also the same age as Wanda? How is she not working as a model somewhere? Does she like chocolate? What’s her favorite kind of music?

    Questions start pilling out on her head, and she has to bite her tongue to keep her mouth shut. She wants to know more about this girl, but it would be cool if she could, you know, not scare her off the first time they meet. So she just nods to the girl - who stopped looking her way a few seconds ago already - and sits down next to Peter, who just cocks an eyebrow up at her but abstains from teasing her further.

    Yelena.

    She mouths it quietly to herself, just to see how it feels in her tongue.

    Yelena.

    Kate has a feeling that name is going to be etched on her memory for a really, really long time.

    II - Should Have Known Better Chapter Notes Should Have Known Better - Sufjan Stevens Their first real class ends up being as unproductive as the introduction they missed, and for a moment Kate wonders what made her think that this would be any different from college: professors reading through powerpoints mindlessly, a couple of group projects, some presentations, and finals that will make her hate her life choices for about a month.

    On their break, Peter suggests they make a group chat to keep each other updated about deadlines, notes, and exams, but frankly, Kate knows he’s just doing it to get M.J.’s phone.

    She’s not going to complain, though. Yelena also gave hers, so.

    By the time their last period ends they have met a couple more classmates, James striking up a conversation with Wanda about a question she asked their professor, and Eddie directly asking Kate if she could send him their schedule because he didn’t even know they had it beforehand.

    Kate chuckled at that. There’s always someone worse of than you.

    “Okay, so after these three enlightening hours, who’s down to grab something to eat before going home?” Peter asks rather loudly, almost all of their classmates - and their professor - turning their heads at him. Wanda shushes him while Kate and M.J. laugh shamelessly at him, and Kate swears she can see a shadow of a smile on Yelena’s pursed lips.

    They are not out of the door yet when someone stops them, “Hey, mind if we join?” Eddie’s the one who asks, James following after him.

    “I’m Eddie, and this statue next to me is James,” says the taller of the two as a formal introduction to the ones who hadn’t talked with them, pointing at his scowling friend, “Don’t let him fool you though, you won’t turn into stone if you look directly into his eyes.”

    If looks could kill Eddie would most likely be underground by now, but James seems to be pretty used to his friend’s antics, shaking his head resignedly before sighing, “You can call me Bucky.”

    M.J. claps both their shoulders, “Welcome to the family, guys. Now everybody, if you don’t mind, we could keep getting to know each other while walking, cause I’m fucking starving,” and before she has even finished speaking she’s linking her arm through Wanda’s and pushing through the door.

    “Ha. I like that one,” Bucky says nodding, and Kate can’t help but add under her breath Yeah, you aren’t the only one, because seriously, the dreamy look Peter has going on right now?

    Straight out of a teenage rom-com.

    Eddie seems to notice too, a lopsided grin on his face as he passes his arm around Peter’s shoulders and starts dragging him along, Bucky trailing close behind, “So tell me, buddy, what’s it like to meet your dream girl the first day of class? Must be pretty neat.”

    Kate laughs at Peter’s indignant babbling, but her fun’s cut short when she hears another, throatier laugh close behind her, and she starts to recount in her head. Wanda and M.J. were the first ones out the door, the three boys leaving after them, so that leaves her and…

    Yelena.

    “Hi,” the blonde girl says, and Kate realizes she must have spilled her name out loud without realizing it, “You’re Kate, right?”

    Quick, Kate. Focus. Try and don’t make a fool of yourself. That’s a question you can answer, you’ve got this.

    “Yeah! I’m Kate. Kate Bishop. Well, actually, my full name’s Katherine Elisabeth Bishop, you know? But that’s too much work just to call someone, right? So Kate, yeah. Kate is fine.”

    Yeah, smooth. She doesn’t facepalm herself right now because she doesn’t want to lose the last shreds of dignity she has, but damn, who knew you could make such a fool of yourself just by saying your name?

    For some divine reason, though, Yelena doesn’t take off running after her little outburst. She just looks at her curiously, again a little contained smile turning up the corners of her mouth and a crooked eyebrow pointed her way, “Well, Kate Bishop, nice to meet you. My name’s Yelena. Belova. Yelena Belova.”

    Yelena Belova. Sounds beautiful.

    “Nice to meet you too, Yelena.”

    Yelena hugs her laptop close to her chest and gives Kate a small smile, before nodding towards the door, “Should we too keep this while walking?”

    “Oh, yeah. Yeah, sure! Let’s go, before we lose them,” Kate says, thankfully managing not to trip on the last couple of stairs hurrying to open the door for her blonde companion, “Ladies first.” It may be lame, but Yelena smiles again and Kate totally counts it as a victory.

    “So, are you from Sokovia too?” good job, Bishop. A good, average question to keep the conversation flowing. She might even be able to do this.

    “No, I was born in Russia. Close to Moscow,” the girl answers curtly, voice colder, like her birthplace is not something she likes to discuss with anyone. Kate worries if perhaps in Russia it’s unpolite to ask someone where they’re from when you’ve just met them, so she tries to fix it, “Oh, cool. I bet Russia’s beautiful. I was born in New York, lived there my whole life. You know, pretty boring comparing.”

    Yelena’s only answer is a short nod without taking her eyes off the ground, and before Kate has time to think about something else to say they reach the exit, the other two girls waiting, presumably, for them.

    “Thank god, I was gonna send someone in there to rescue you guys,” M.J.’s voice greets them, “The boys went ahead to grab a table, let’s go before they start eating without us,” she says already walking backwards, not sparing another second without food on her hands.

    “I think I might head home now,” Yelena speaks quietly looking directly at Wanda, descending to stand next to her, “Nat’s coming over in a bit, I don’t want to be late.”

    Kate feels a knot form on her throat, her heart plummeting to the ground.

    “You sure? Want me to come with you?” Wanda asks her friend, not seeming all that surprised about her wanting to drop out of their improvised group lunch. Yelena shakes her head, squeezing the redhead’s hand briefly before turning to the opposite direction of the canteen and saying a short goodbye waving her hand. Her eyes find Kate’s for a moment before dropping them to the ground quickly and taking off.

    “I think I messed up,” Kate can’t help but sigh worriedly a few seconds later, eyes still fixed on blonde hair and a green coat. Wanda eyes her confused, “What? With Yel?”

    Kate had forgotten for a second that Wanda was still there. Wanda, Yelena’s friend. She blushes sheepishly, “Yeah, we were cool. I introduced myself and then she told me her name, everything was fine. She even laughed! But then I asked her where she was from, and she totally closed off. Maybe it was rude of me to ask that, you think? Or maybe she was just being polite but doesn’t have any interest in me knowing where she’s from, or where she lives, or -”

    Thankfully, Wanda takes pity on her and saves her from rambling to death, “Oh, no Kate. No. Don’t worry about her, that was not personal at all,” and truly, with such a sweet voice she would believe Wanda if she told her that gravity didn’t exist. Kate takes a deep breath, nodding to the redhead who’s now looking down the path where Yelena disappeared a while ago, “Yelena is… complex. Some people think she’s mean, at first. But nothing farther from the truth. Trust me, I would know. It takes a little bit of work to get through her but be patient, it’s worth it.”

    Kate smiles at the tender way Wanda talks about her friend - wishes she had more people who talked about her that way - and decides to trust her, to work on it, and be patient.

    “I’m sure it is.”

    So, being patient may not be one of Kate’s strengths.

    Her thoughts have been circling around what she’s decided to call the “Russia Incident”, her discomfort at possibly having upset Yelena earlier making her feel like she needs to say something to the other girl, at least to make sure she knows Kate didn’t mean to make her uncomfortable.

    She knows, objectively, that she did not do anything terrible. But for some reason she feels this pull to the blonde, like she knows her from before, kinda. And that has never happened to Kate, this connection at first sight, but she knows they exist.

    When I first met your father I was attending a volleyball game at college. I was in fact going to see my then-boyfriend play, but then it was your father’s turn to serve, and everyone clapped so I joined. And he looked at me, straight into my eyes. Right then, I knew I had to meet him.

    Kate has always let her instinct guide her, and right now her instinct is telling her that Yelena and her were supposed to meet, too.

    With a self-encouraging nod, she sits down on the couch with her phone in her hands, her fingers hovering over the contact list on the group chat Peter so diligently created.

    I’ll have to remember to thank him for that later.

    It’s easy finding Yelena’s number, she’s the only one who hasn’t texted anything yet, so after saving her number she takes a deep breath and opens a new text for her.

    hey, it’s Kate. just wanted to apologize if i overstepped before, didn’t mean to make you uncomfortable

    Right after she hits send she throws her phone to the other side of the couch as if it were a bomb that could explode on her bare hands. It lands softly on a pillow, and Lucky watches from his bed, head tilted, only needing a short wiggle from Kate’s fingers to go sit next to her. She scratches his ears, partly to comfort herself and partly to stop her need to check if Yelena has answered already.

    She will probably take a good while, she tries to talk her anxiety down, maybe won’t even respond. Maybe we’ll just talk tomorrow.

    Her phone chimes two minutes later - not that she was counting, mind you - and she nearly chokes on her water.

    “Alright, boy. We were not expecting such a rapid answer, but we’ve got this!” she tells her dog, and Lucky whines at her in total support. When the screen lights up, there are two messages waiting for her.

    Hello, Kate Bishop.

    Don’t worry, you didn’t. I really had to get home to talk with my sister, but I’ll try not to miss the next lunch date.

    She knows there’s more to it, but now is a good time to remember Wanda’s words and don’t push. Yelena’s not mad, that’s a good start.

    Kate opens them ready to type a response when she sees the three dots dancing below Yelena’s name, the telltale of her writing something more, so she waits. It seems the blonde writes and deletes time and time again (that, or she’s going to send her a pretty long text), but after a couple more seconds the third text appears.

    Would you like me to send you the notes I took today of the introduction you missed?

So either Yelena really hadn’t checked at all her other messages and she had chosen to answer Kate first, or she knew she already had them, but couldn’t think of anything else to keep the conversation going. Whatever it was, the thought of the other girl willingly making an effort to talk with her made Kate’s heart beat a little faster.

    great! i really didn’t want us to start on the wrong foot. i hope everything went great with your sister.

    and yeah, that’d be awesome, thanks Yelena.

    This time she waits by the phone for the answer, which comes within seconds.

    Well, we’re not on great terms right now, but it could have been worse.

    Kate stops in her tracks. Is that an invitation? Should she pry, ask what’s going on between her and her sister? Or would that be overstepping and messing things up again? Yelena makes the choice for her.

    The dog on your profile picture is yours?

    and yes, he is! handsome, right? his name is Lucky, aka Pizza Dog. look, i’ll send you a pic.

    Thank you, Kate Bishop. I’ll take that in mind.

    Yes, he is very handsome.

    Then, And you look very pretty, too.

    Kate feels as if the room has run out of oxygen. She checks the picture she sent, making sure her face is nowhere in sight. And then she realizes: Yelena’s talking about her profile picture still, a candid shot her mother took of Lucky and her on Central Park, his tongue lolling to the side, Kate’s smile big and eyes crinkling.

    Oh, god. Yelena has totally looked at her profile picture, and not just at her dog.

    Here, I’ll show you a photo of my dog. Her name is Fanny.

    And yeah, she definitely wants to keep talking.

    Kate turns off the lights, burrowing further down her blanket hiding her rather silly smile on her pillow, watching entranced the picture of Yelena with an Akita dog that almost reaches her waist.

    And if she didn’t already think this girl was gorgeous, whoa. Seeing her smile on full force, dimples all out, hair dancing free around her face like a halo… Just, wow. It makes Kate feel the urge to have that smile pointed at her. To be the reason behind it.

    You and I were totally meant to meet.

    III - Colourway Chapter Notes Colourway - Novo Amor “I’m just saying I don’t understand why we have to learn about international relations theory when we’re law students,” Peter whines for what might as well be the fifth time in the last hour. The rest of the table barely listens to his complaining anymore, having gotten used to it in the couple of weeks they’ve known each other.

    Wanda - sweet, sweet Wanda - shoots him an understanding, compassionate look, seemingly ready to explain to him again why it’s important for them to know these things, when M.J.

    decides to cut in, “Maybe because we are studying international law? Now quit pouting and help me understand this neorealism shit.”

    Peter shuts up immediately, eyes wide and ear tips pink; Eddie and Kate sharing a knowing grin both silently agreeing to tease him about it later. And Kate can’t help but think of how comforting it is, this feeling of ease and belonging they’ve managed to build in such a short time. Even Yelena seems amused by the whole scene smirking quietly behind her notebook, having decided to grace them with her presence this afternoon.

    Yelena. Elusive, enigmatic Yelena.

    Out of the little group, she and Bucky are the ones who skip more often their study sessions (comprehensible, seeing the course these seem to take), and their almost regular hang-out meetings after class (less comprehensible - they are pretty fun to be around, Kate considers). Eddie had told them Bucky had always been more of a lone wolf, making it a great deal that he even contemplated joining them sometimes.

    And Yelena, well. Yelena was Yelena. But still, Kate wouldn’t complain.

    She had come three times this week to study, and had eaten with them yesterday. And they were still texting like, pretty regular. Kate knew now she and Wanda were sharing a small flat, not too far away from her own. She knew her sister Natasha was a lawyer, and a seriously good one considering she was only five years older than Yelena. She knew her favorite meal was mac and cheese - the boxed ones with a lot of hot sauce Kate Bishop, that’s the food of the gods - and she knew her favorite season was winter - who on their right mind enjoys sweating?

    Sure, perhaps they couldn’t be considered the closest of friends yet, but she totally counted it as a victory.

    “Children, we should get on moving. It’s almost time for Hill’s class,” Eddie says while firing text to someone - probably Bucky -, having given up on the other subject and packed all his stuff a while ago. Kate sighs relieved (she was just pretending to read, really). Out of the corner of her eye, she sees Yelena folding her notes, a content smile on her face.

    Another thing Kate had learned from their one on one chats: Human Rights was Yelena’s favorite subject.

    Maria Hill was a good professor. At least, she had given her that impression after their first lecture. She also seemed like one of those teachers that would make you sweat blood for a good grade, but those always ended up being the best ones anyways. Even if they scared the shit out of you when they asked a question no one knew the answer to.

    “So, I know last week I said you would have to work on a project about whichever subject regarding human rights you chose, but I’ve given it some thought after going through the list of students. Forty-six students, thirty pages per student, makes it almost one thousand four hundred pages for me to read. That’s too much, so -”

    “We can make it a fifteen pages essay?” someone among the first row bravely cuts in, probably regretting it the instant professor Hill shoots them a cold, pointed glare. Fifteen pages are out of question, then.

    “No. You will write the thirty pages, Willson. But you’ll do it in pairs, that way we’ll cut my work in half. And yours, too,” she adds as an afterthought, though clearly, that wasn’t her main worry, “And before you start mumbling among yourselves to see who you’ll work with, don’t. I’ll be pairing you in alphabetical order, that way it will be easier to grade for me.”

Except maybe he gets paired with someone above him in that list, and I’m pretty sure there’s a John around here, too, so he would actually be below him and -

    “Allan, you’ll work with Andrews,” professor Hill’s voice cuts through her mental reasoning, her eyes opening in surprise. Okay, so it’s last names then, “Atwood, with Barnes. Belova, you get Bishop. Brock, you go with Calderon -”

    Belova, that’s Yelena’s last name. And Bishop is hers. Belova, you get Bishop. So that means...

Yelena lifts her hand and smiles, twirling her finger and mouthing a later to her before looking up front again. Kate just nods because really, what were the odds of her getting paired up with the girl who’s been on her mind since she first laid eyes on her?

    “Fucking lucky, that’s what you are,” Peter whispers next to her, elbow digging softly on her side, thumbs up in a failed attempt at being discreet. Kate slaps at his hands but laughs anyway. Peter is the only one whom she’s told about her little crush on their blonde friend, having pried without much effort the truth from Kate when he caught her smiling down at her phone like a lovestruck teenager. In her defense, Yelena had just sent her a picture of Fanny in a cute green jersey writing We match! right below. But he only got to tease her about it in private, because Kate in return didn’t complain about hearing all about M.J.’s awesomeness every single day. At least not much.

Once you have it, please send me an email so I can make sure none of you work on the same topic. If you have any questions, I’m always available,” and with that, professor Hill put on her coat and leaves, a wake of murmuring trailing after her. So much for being available thinks Kate to herself eying the door. But then she feels a soft tap on her shoulder and all thoughts leave her mind.

    “Hello, partner,” a husky voice says from behind, making her turn around with a big grin plastered on her face, “Hiya, partner,” she says back to Yelena, who looks almost as happy as Kate is with this arrangement. Totally a victory, too.

    “Do you want to go somewhere else to talk about our theme?” and truly, she would say yes to absolutely everything this girl proposed. Kate just nods, picking up her things and making room for Yelena to go first, following dutifully after her. They leave their classroom and the building too, start walking in the direction Yelena always disappears once class ends. She thinks fleetly that they didn’t even say goodbye to their friends, but that thought disappears when she figures Yelena is leading them to her apartment.

But Kate doesn’t have time to dwell on the feeling of being so close to the other girl because Fanny is nipping at her pants, whining to get attention, so she dutifully kneels down to scratch the Akita behind her ears, letting her lick at her hands gleefully.

    “Well, that was a triumph if I ever saw one,” Yelena comments, apparently impressed by her dog’s greeting of their guest. Kate girns smugly up at her, “You should not underestimate me, Belova. I am practically the dog whisperer,” and maybe that was a dorky thing to say but it makes the other girl cackle again, so.

    “Yeah, no shit. That dog barely tolerated me when she got her.”

Kate stands up and steps closer to her friend, not really sure about what’s happening but sensing her unsettlement. A redhead woman who’s totally not Wanda is leaning against the kitchen door, the clinking of the spoon stirring whatever’s on the mug she’s holding the only sound on the room.

    “Natasha, what are you doing here?”

    Oh, so she’s Natasha. That would explain the amazingly good looks, their parents must be some sort of Russian demigods. She’s about to say as much when the older woman doesn’t respond, to try and ease the tension, but Natasha beats her to the punch, “I needed to pick up the folders I left here last Sunday,” she answers easily, clearly unbothered by her sister’s staredown.

    “And you decided to have a coffee before leaving?”

    “Tsk, don’t be ridiculous, Lena,” Natasha says humorously shaking her head, “I don’t drink coffee after midday, this is tea.”

    Kate manages to hold her chuckle in - thankfully, because she is pretty sure Yelena would not appreciate it-, but the truth is this woman doesn’t seem all that bad. Sure, don’t judge a book by its cover and all that (and if Yelena is upset with her she surely must have her reasons) but Kate likes to think she has a good sixth sense when meeting people, and Natasha seems pretty nice.

    “Great, I hope you enjoyed it. Now if you have everything you need, please leave. We need to start working on something,” and Yelena leans a little closer to Kate, probably unconsciously, but it’s enough for the brunette to understand that she wants this to be over as soon as possible.

    Natasha on the other hand seems to have other ideas in mind, “Yelena, we both agreed we need to talk. Communicate, try to understand each other,” she sighs, defeated, “Wanda texted me to say she wouldn’t be here until later this evening. That’s why I dropped by, to see if we could have a minute. Maybe we can try again another day,” she intends to make it a statement, but it comes up more as a doubtful question.

    Yelena purses her lips, her characteristic frown firm in place, but she nods her chin ever so slightly, “Yes, another day.”

    That seems to be as good as Natasha’s going to get today and she knows it, so she smiles at her sister sadly while putting on her coat, “Well, I better get going then. It was nice to meet you…”, and Kate is so stunned by this woman’s effortlessly coolness that she almost forgets her own name when she addresses her.

    “Kate Bishop,” Yelena thankfully supplies for her, her name rolling out her tongue easily by now.

    “Kate Bishop, huh,” she parrots back, cocking an eyebrow at her sister before looking back at her, “I’ve heard your name pop up these pasts weeks when I’ve been around. Now I’ve got a face to match.”

    “Natasha,” and Kate shouldn’t find that warning growl as hot as she does, mostly because this is not the best moment, but sue her, she’s only human.

    Meanwhile, the recipient of the scowl doesn’t seem affected at all, “I’m Natasha Romanoff, Yelena’s sister. As I said, it was a pleasure to meet you, Kate. I hope we see each other again soon,” and with a tight handshake she blows a kiss to Yelena and disappears swiftly behind the door. Kate doesn’t even get to reply.

    Yelena collapses onto the couch heaving a sigh, face hiding between her fingers. Kate feels a little lost but decides to act as she would with any other friend, sitting next to her and placing a comforting hand at the small of her back, “So that was your sister.”

    For a moment she worries she might have said the wrong thing feeling Yelena go taut under her touch, but it only lasts a second. All the fight drains from her petite body, her weight slumping on the back of the couch, effectively trapping Kate’s hand there. She nods wearily, “Yeah, that was Natasha. I’m sorry you had to see all that, I didn’t know she’d be here” she apologizes, looking at Kate straight in the eyes.

    Kate starts moving her hand up and down her back mindlessly while shaking her head, “Hey, no. There’s nothing to be sorry for. I told you, I’ve had my fair share of family feuds.

    There’re few things that can scare me off anymore.”

    And for all her blabber tendencies, Kate seems to be managing to say what Yelena needs to hear, so she pushes on, “I know we don’t know each other all that well yet, but I really meant it when I said I was willing to listen if you ever want to talk.”

    Yelena gazes at her with an emotion Kate has trouble naming, but guesses it was a good thing when the girl’s forehead falls to her shoulder briefly, hand squeezing her knee in thanks. It feels a lot like a promise.

    Kate is still processing the spark she felt ignite low on her belly with that touch when a thought suddenly catches up to her, “Did your sister say her last name was Romanoff? Is she married?”

    Yelena seems to be caught off guard by the question, her eyes skittishly avoiding hers. She worries she may have overstepped whatever imaginary boundaries they were threading on, apology ready on her lips, when the older girl straightens her back and answers, “No, she’s not married. We don’t share last names.”

    She gets up from the couch, extending a hand to help Kate stand, and wanders into the kitchen without further elaborating. Kate follows her instinctively, stoping at the exact same door Natasha was leaning in a few minutes ago. Yelena makes herself busy by getting glasses out of the cupboard and filling them with water, the sound of the drops hitting the cristal surface the only noise besides their breaths.

    She passes one to Kate and takes a drink from her own, hip bent against the counter, “We’re adopted, so we are not blood-related. And we, we were adopted when - we weren’t…” she stumbles over her own words, something Kate has never witnessed before, “We were old enough to have last names of our own, and they - mama and papa - first took us in as foster care parents, so they didn’t change them. And then time passed, and it stopped being important.”

    Kate suddenly images a younger version of her friend, hair in pigtails and mouth covered in chocolate, calling her parents mama and papa. Her heart turns to mush.

    “But she has always been there, you know? We literally grew up together,” Yelena continues, and if there is wetness in her eyes when she looks up, neither of them mentions it, “She was there since before I can even remember, probably the only constant in my life. My only family, for a pretty long time,” she ends nodding to herself, a little lost on her own head but apparently satisfied with her final explanation.

    Green meets blue, then, and Kate sees uncertainty lying underneath her eyes, probably wondering what she thinks about everything she has told, what she thinks of her now. She gets closer to her and gently lays her hand on top of Yelena’s, gently grasping it, “That sounds a lot like true love to me. Blood doesn’t always give you that,” she ducks her chin making sure to speak eye to eye, smiling tenderly, ”Blood doesn’t always mean family.”

    Yelena seems to have been stunned into silence, gaze fixed at her with her head tilted to the side and eyelashes still wet with unshed tears Kate really wishes she could wipe. When the blonde’s fingers interlock with hers she thinks that’s good, too. “I think that was the best way anyone has ever responded when I’ve told them about being an orphan,” emotion clogs at her voice, but Kate gets what she’s trying to say anyway.

    Thank you.

    Yelena didn’t deepen about their life before the adoption and Kate didn’t push, because even someone completely oblivious would have sensed that was a very sensitive matter for the older girl. And Kate wasn’t completely oblivious, mind you.

    They stand there, holding hands on the marble counter, neither of them willing to move. After a while, though, Yelena looks as if she was getting too drowned in her memories, so Kate decides to take over.

    “Penny for your thoughts?” mumbles gently the brunette, both curious and eager to keep the conversation going.

    “I was just wondering… You’ve said you’ve had family problems too, before. Were they also coming from an annoying sibling?”

    Fair question. After all, if she wants honesty Kate supposes she has to give something in return. More so considering everything the other girl has shared. She takes a deep breath looking up at Yelena, ready to expose one of her most private wounds, “No, I’m an only child. But my dad passed when I was twelve, since then it was always me and my mom. And we don’t exactly see eye to eye… I was closest with him, I guess.”

    It amazes Kate how, even after almost ten years, her eyes would still get moist just with the mention of her dad.

    She voices her musing out loud and Yelena just holds her hand tighter, “I don’t believe that’s a bad thing. Or weird. That time heals everything shit is… bullshit,” she whispers confidently, managing to make Kate chuckle with her philosophical conclusion wrapped in a beautiful accent, “It makes things bearable, but it will probably always hurt a little, and that’s okay. You are allowed to feel that.”

    It’s in that exact moment that Kate realizes she might be actually talking about her father’s death with someone who was as close to getting it as anyone had ever been in her life.

    Obviously, hers and Yelena’s were completely different stories, but the shared knowledge of love and loss was there.

    And if Kate wasn’t impossibly drawn to this girl before, well. She was a goner now.

    IV - To Believe Chapter Summary first chapter that hasn't been posted anywhere yet, i hope you like it!

    for this one, the song is To Believe - The Cinematic Orchestra ft. Moses Sumney.

    To say Kate was nervous would be a misunderstanding. Her apartment hadn’t been this clean since the day it was sold to her, probably. She had even made her bed and taken out all the trash (yes, including the pizza crust leftovers, much to Lucky’s dismay). She had fallen out of bed way too early for a Saturday, anxious at the knowledge of Yelena coming over that afternoon, so she had taken to cleaning.

    Now she still had like, three hours until her partner was to be here, and she had no idea what to do with herself. She practiced her aim for a while, getting bored of missing her target time and time again due to her unfocused mind and slightly trembling hands. She gave Lucky a short walk, too, hoping the fresh air would stop her from being so skittish - god, she hoped she managed to calm down before the blonde got here and noticed.

    The thing was, yesterday had been the first time they had ever been alone, together. Kate had thought about it, more so after they began texting so frequently. Had feared that perhaps the easiness of talking with each other may have been an illusion created by their phone screens. But surprisingly - or rather, unsurprisingly - it had gone over pretty well.

    More than well. It had been amazing.

    After the short encounter with Natasha, Kate had worried about Yelena closing of, upset at her having witnessed something so personal. But after the initial uncertainty (from both of them) about what was and wasn’t off-limits to discuss, Kate had been given the privilege to learn about a part of Yelena’s life that was unknown to most. And she had shared hers, too.

    There weren’t many people Kate thinks she would enjoy talking about her father to.

    Yesterday, Yelena became one of those few.

    That’s why Kate was so nervous about today. It was the feeling you got when you were just starting to know someone, the feeling of not knowing if the other person truly felt comfortable and clicked with you the same way you did with them, or if it was just a sporadic, one-time thing.

    Whoever said first times were difficult sure didn’t have to face a second time with high expectations.

    Her inner dissertation on interpersonal relationships is cut short by the buzzing of her phone, a text from Yelena informing her she’s outside. “Okay buddy, we’ve got this!” she whispers her dog, mustering as much confidence as she can manage. Lucky bounces over to the front door, already excited at the prospect of having guests, a soft knock getting a woof out of him as a greeting.

    When Kate opens the door she swears she swoons a little. Yelena is there, hair falling freely over her shoulders in soft, tousled waves replacing her everpresent braids, maroon tinted lips stretching in an utterly blinding smile that makes the green in her eyes almost disappear behind the crinkles in her skin.

    “Is that a doggy I hear?” she said it in such an innocent, childlike joy that Kate couldn’t even be mad about that smile not being for her. The cause of said glee rushes over to dance around Yelena’s legs, tail bouncing left and right. It was difficult to say who was happier to meet whom.

    “That is Lucky for you, miss Belova,” Kate grins down at the sight in front of her, Lucky standing on his rear legs, standing almost as tall as her friend who can’t seem to stop giggling at the kisses the dog is bestowing all over her face, “He seems to like you.”

    “And I like him! You’re even more handsome than in the pictures your owner has shown me,” this last part coming out as a coo to her dog, and Kate never thought she could feel jealous of him, but here they are. Yelena must catch her squinting eyes because she chuckles and adds, “You know you are very pretty too, miss Kate Bishop. I believe I told you so.”

    “Will you ever just call me Kate?” she fires back, blatantly trying to change the topic of conversation before she gets all red-faced, but Yelena manages to catch her off guard again, “I think Kate Bishop is a beautiful name. A beautiful name for a beautiful girl,” and she has the audacity to wink. She fumbles for a response while Yelena is clearly very pleased with the stunned silence she has drawn out of the usually overtalkative brunette.

    Kate’s a little at loss with this more relaxed, less guarded version of the older girl but one thing’s for sure, it does help ease her fears of going back to just classmates. She then remembers Wanda’s parting words yesterday. Patience does have its rewards, you see?

    When the redhead arrived home and saw the two of them huddled close together on the couch, softly spoken words drowned quietly by the sound of Yelena’s music, her poorly concealed surprise at finding them actually talking instead of working had been glaringly obvious, even to her best friend.

    Sure, Yelena may also have been a bit pissed off by the whole Natasha ambush and the Wanda-told-me-you’d-be-here-early thing, but that was just an extra. Her acting as if she had caught them having sex on their dining table totally hadn’t helped Wanda’s case. But before Kate hurried to leave under the fake premise of being late to something (partly because she guessed they needed to have a talk, partly because really, when did it get so late?), Wanda had walked her to the door, a soft knowing smile on her face, and muttered those words before waving her goodbye.

    So Kate resolves to keep on doing what she’s done until now - which surprisingly has been just to be herself, who knew? - but now, she will do so more confidently. She offers something to drink to her pretty guest, and without second-guessing herself, she shoots her question, “So… How did everything go, yesterday?”

    An open. She can take it and explain what happened after Kate left, or she can just brush it off with a generic answer. Whatever she chooses, Kate won’t push.

    Yelena seems to ponder her answer over the rim of her water glass, the light reflecting on it and casting beautiful shadows over her eyelashes, making her eyes glow. Kate kind of wishes she could take a picture without it being entirely too weird.

    Patience.

    “Wanda and I talked. I told her I would appreciate a heads up next time she and my sister plan an intervention. But,” she pauses, pursing her lips distraught, “She only wanted to help. I really am trying to get back on track with Natasha, and I suppose sometimes I need an extra push to meet people halfway,” and by the way she’s looking at her now, eyes round and searching, she has a feeling Yelena’s not talking only about Natasha.

    Kate nods reassuringly. Sure, she still doesn’t have the full picture, but with the blonde’s past she guesses it would be a wonder if Yelena didn’t have the slightest bit of trust issues. She also doesn’t doubt for a second that their friend was doing what she thought was right for Yelena. Wanda appears to be the sweet, selfless type. And there’s also no question about how much she loves Yelena.

    She says as much, and the older girl nods her head, a soft smile on her lips, “We’ve known each other for quite some time, too. I think she’s the closest thing I have to a real friend.”

    That affirmation makes Kate’s heart clench. Obviously, Wanda totally seemed to be the word’s best choice to have as your closest friend, but somehow that statement sounded sorrowful and… lonely. As if Yelena was actually amazed at managing to have one real friend.

    “Well, you told me yesterday that you guys went to college together and she stayed by your side when the whole Natasha debacle exploded, even though they were pretty good friends, too. She chose to stay with you here instead of rooming with her boyfriend who lives less than half an hour away from campus, and she refers to you as her best friend to anyone she talks to,” Kate numbers just a few of her irrefutable proofs, “I’m pretty certain that’s what makes a best friend.”

    “Plus,” she adds after a second, gathering courage, “I think we can become really good friends, too.”

    Yelena looks at her with the same emotion she didn’t recognize yesterday, one that feels kind of like pride and affection and sadness and joy all combined. She blinks three times, swallows, and nods, “You do?”

    Kate hides her hands on her back pockets to avoid the urge to reach out to her, to link their fingers together, “I think we are on it already,” she shrugs, trying to radiate confidence, “If that’s what you want to, too. Of course,” she adds hurriedly, and so much for confidence.

    “I would like that very much, Kate Bishop.”

    There are hundreds of questions burning at the tip of Kate’s tongue, her desire to unravel the mystery that is Yelena an almost uncontrollable need.

    Patience.

    Instead, she drops an arm around Yelena’s shoulders bringing her body close to her side, boldly pushing squeezing her in a one-arm hug. The blonde sneaks an arm around her waist, fingers hooking on the loopbelt of her jeans, resting her head on her shoulder. Kate softly guides her to the table where she has already set her computer, Lucky chasing after them, “Okay, partner. Let’s get on with it.”

    They’ve been working on their essay for a while, both lost on research and data, when Kate’s stomach decides to make its presence known. She grins sheepishly at Yelena’s smirk, the girl then rubbing mindlessly her eyes clearly strained for the computer’s blue light.

    “We deserve a break, don’t you think? I could order something to eat,” and it only takes the blonde about six seconds to close her laptop and stand up, arms stretched above her head.

    Kate catches a glimpse of skin where her top rides up, the image engraved behind her eyelids every time blinks. She takes a trembling breath shaking her head, unable to remember when was the last time someone affected her as much as the girl next to her does.

    “What do you want?”

    “Huh?” Kate shoots back rapidly, believing herself caught. She couldn’t possibly know where her mind had wandered, right? Peter had just been joking when she said that Russians could read minds, right?

    Yelena looks at her amused, and asks again, “What do you want? To eat?” she elaborates, and of course. Food. That’s what they were talking about.

    “How does pizza sound for you?” Kate doesn’t even have time to finish her question, Lucky already on his feet and next to her, drool almost dripping from his tongue at the mere mention of his favorite meal. “I think he’s settled it,” Yelena nods her chin at the dog, amused by the natural reaction. Kate nods her head like a proud mom, “Well, yeah. His nickname isn’t Pizza Dog for anything, you know?”

    The brunette places her order, patting the spot next to her on the couch, inviting Yelena to sit down with her. She moves swiftly to the kitchen first, to fill her glass with water again, and Kate can’t help but think that she looks like she belongs here, in her apartment. As if she had been here hundreds of times before.

    Yelena turns to head in her direction, clearly catching sight of the brunette’s expression, an unsure look appearing in her eyes “I hope you don’t mind. You said I could get whatever so I thought…-”

    Kate frowns, but quickly realizes she must have misunderstood the look she was giving her, “Oh no! No. No, no, Yelena. It’s totally cool, I meant it when I said you could grab whatever and go wherever. My home is yours whenever you need it,” she says hurriedly, worried about making the other girl feel unwelcome just for daydreaming without meaning to. Just to make things clearer, she adds, “I was just thinking it’s nice that we feel so comfortable around each other, sometimes I forget I’ve only known you guys for less than a month.”

    If that made her friend believe her, she was willing to embarrass herself a tiny bit sharing her corny thoughts.

    Her partner smiles as she sits down next to her, their thighs lightly grazing each other, looking as if she was mulling over something, “That’s a good thing, no?”

    “It is. For me, at least,” nothing to hide there. She is really fucking happy about the small group of people now she gets to call friends.

    She is even happier that little group includes the gorgeous girl sitting next to her.

    “I didn’t have that many friends before, you know? After high school,” Kate blurts that out, more as a self-deprecating comment than as something deep, but curious green eyes turn to her, silently willing her to continue.

    Well, she did say that Wanda was one of the few people she has ever counted as friends, so maybe it’s understandable that she wants to know more.

    “I went to the same school since I was three. I started kindergarten there and left after high school. So, seventeen. My friends had always been the same people, most of us had known each other since before we could even remember. And I had lots of friends there. Have, still. Most of them. They knew me before and after my dad passed, they knew everything because I lived it while I was with them, so it was easy, in a way. But then I went to NYU and everything kind of changed. I consider myself a pretty easygoing person, but I also know I may seem… weird, to some. And everyone was so mature, suddenly, and formed groups so quickly, and they didn’t know me, so I had to go through the same over and over again,” Kate stops to take a breath, aware now for the first time of the thumb tenderly caressing her knee, “I guess I stopped trying after a while. I met some nice people, don’t get me wrong, but I wouldn’t count many of them as friends. It was nothing traumatic or anything, just… solitary, I guess.”

    The hand on her leg doesn’t move after her monologue ends, thumb firmly going back and forth over the material of her jeans. The soothing motion helps Kate come back slowly, her own fingers squeezing Yelena’s, voice tight with emotion “Anyways, now you know I really mean it when I say I’m happy I’ve met you all.”

    Yelena lets her forehead fall to Kate’s shoulder, and the brunette can feel the smile Yelena sends her way without even looking down, “Me, too.”

    For a second Kate expects her to ask something else about her college days, perhaps prod a bit out of curiosity, but the other girl surprises her yet again by changing the object of their conversation, “Have I told you I’ve never been to New York?”

    If she notices Kate’s gratefulness in her blue eyes, she doesn’t show.

    “What? How is that even possible, we’re literally a couple hours away!”

    Yelena shrugs one shoulder, “When we came here from Russia, we moved to Ohio. I went to Chicago University and only moved to New Haven because my sister lives here and I got a scholarship for this. I haven’t had the chance to visit, yet,” she finishes quietly, lips downturned in a cute, sad pout.

    The younger girl takes her friend’s hand in between both her own solemnly, “I’ll take you. I’ll have to go visit my mom sometime during the semester, you can come with me. We’ll go to every tourist trap you want to see.”

    The beaming smile on the blonde’s face is totally worth the thought of having to push her way through seas of people to get to the top of the Empire State.

    The doorbell rings and saves Kate from totally embarrassing herself by comparing her partner to the sun or something equally lame. “Food!” she shouts instead, scrambling to her feet hoping that physical distance from this girl helps her mind get out of the gutter. Yelena laughs after her, but whether it is at the childish scream or at the comedy scene that must be Lucky running after her to get to the door first, she doesn’t know. It’s nice all the same.

    Kate grabs their food from the delivery guy who’s come regularly enough for her to actually make small - like, literally one minute and a half small - talk with, Yelena waiting for her, hovering around the table when she closes the door and turns around.

    If Kate didn’t know better, she’d swear the other girl was trying to eavesdrop not so stealthily, “Was that one of your friends?”

    Yelena looks like she’d be a good liar, probably one of those smart, sneaky women who managed to get the truth out of people without them noticing. Perhaps it has something to do with all the Russian stereotypes Kate has stored up in her mind after watching way too many spy movies with soviets trying to destabilize the country or whatever. The thing is, Yelena may look like she’d be good at lying, but is completely not.

    Her normally steady gaze is avoiding Kate’s, strangely focused on her kitchen drawers, opening and closing them without looking inside.

    “Who? Miles?” Kate asks dumbfounded, “No, he seems like a cool guy but we’ve barely talked. He just comes around often whenever I order, so we kind of know each other by now.”

    “Oh,” the blonde stops her movements, mumbling softly, “It just looked like he may like you.”

    Kate doesn’t get to ask “what?” or “how did you gather he liked me with just a one minute chat?” because the blonde opens another drawer and gasps dramatically interrupting her, “Kate Bishop, don’t tell me you only have one fork,” she indignantly rises the object on her hand, mouth open in utterly surprise at Kate’s seemingly disregard of cutlery norms.

    The brunette, in turn, blushes, “Well, I’m only one person!” she tries to justify plopping down onto the chair like a petulant child, their previous conversation not forgotten, but stored to revise later.

    Yelena tries to hide her amused smirk biting the inside of her cheek (again, not a very good liar) shaking her head disapprovingly, sitting next to Kate and patting her cheek sweetly, “Don’t worry, Kate Bishop. I’ll help you get the necessary utensils to become a functional young adult,” she jokes, clearly enjoying herself. Then, sobering up a little she adds, “I’ll tell you why they are important to me, too.”

    And those softly spoken words sound a lot like a promise.

    VI - Strangers in the night Chapter Notes Strangers in the night - Frank Sinatra It’s been two days. Thirty-three hours, to be more exact. Not that Kate was counting, because then it would be thirty-three hours and seventeen minutes.

    She hasn’t heard from Yelena since they all parted ways on Friday night - or rather, on Saturday’s early morning -, her last memory of the blonde being a hurried hug before she and Wanda got into a cab to avoid walking alone the stretch between Kate’s apartment and their own.

    They had texted each other constantly since they opened their own, private conversation almost two months ago.
