    “It’s because they are comfortably laying down,” Yelena points out, having followed Kate’s train of thought, “I’m tired but there’s no way I can sleep on the floor like this,” she motions to where she’s sitting, cross-legged on top of a blanket. The younger girl chuckles and nods, “Yeah, they totally played us. Let’s go find someplace better to sleep, tsarina,” Kate says while trying to get up as elegantly as she can (which isn’t much because she's a bit tipsy and her legs are a bit numb), wriggling her fingers at the blonde, offering her help.

    Just pulling the fucking air mattress out of the box is a nightmare - Eddie had clearly folded it in a bunch and forced it in there - but the fun part is trying to inflate it without any kind of instructions. After a few failed attempts and much giggling on Kate’s part, Yelena finally figures out the way the pedal is supposed to go. “Well, this might take a while,” she comments sardonically, pushing at the pedal with her foot and watching the small amount of air it gets into the mattress.

    The brunette snickers again, “I think it would be easier if we just squeezed on the couch, it seems big enough.”

    “Yeah, you are right,” and Kate has to do a double-take to make sure the blonde’s serious, because she was kinda joking - well, not completely, she just didn’t think Yelena would actually agree - but the Russian all but drops the stupidly small pedal and starts arranging the pillows on the couch, “Which end do you want, left or right?”

    Oh okay, we are going to take an end each. We aren’t going to cuddle on the couch all night. Okay. Now I see the logistics.

    “I let you choose, babe,” she all but yawns holding her blanket to her chest, and she’s decidedly going to blame it on her tiredness the idea that perhaps Yelena wanted to sleep curled up next to her. The blonde takes a sit on the right side, beckoning Kate to sit beside her, and she does as instructed, falling quite heavily onto the cushion and draping her arm behind the Russian’s back. For support.

    They turn off the lights but don’t make an attempt to move, carefully wrapping both blankets around each other and Yelena resting her head on Kate’s shoulder. She seems to be deep in thought, the younger girl letting a few minutes pass before caving in, “What’s going on in there?” she asks, gently tapping two fingers against her friend’s forehead.

    For a second she worries Yelena might have fallen asleep and she’s just woken her up, but the other girl just links her fingers through hers and turns to smile at her without raising her head from her shoulder, “I was thinking about the movie,” she mutters, raising her feet from the floor and hiding them beneath the blanket, “Do you think they were together?”

    Kate has to wrack her brain to follow her on that one, “The main characters? The blonde and the brunette?” she asks to make sure she’s understanding right. She feels Yelena nod against her, “Yeah, definitely. The blonde got her out of her bastard, abusive husband’s house, they raised her baby together, she was next to her when she died… Plus, the way they looked at each other is something else. Yes, I totally think they were a couple,” she kind of always has, even distinctly remembers her mother mentioning that it was more clearly stated in the book.

    Yelena remains quiet for a while, long enough for Kate to feel the weight of her eyelids. She unconsciously begins moving herself to lay down, surprised when the blonde comes willingly with her in the same direction. She curls a hand around the older girl’s waist to make sure she doesn’t fall and hides her face on golden curls. The scent of sandalwood and vanilla drifting through her nose and the motion of Yelena’s fingers toying with her own has her on the verge of sleep, the younger girl tightening her hold on her waist when the Russian speaks again, “But they didn’t say anything.”

    “Mhmm?” is as coherent as Kate can get.

    “They never said they were together. Or told anyone, specifically. Everybody thought they were friends, and they seemed okay with it, right?” she asks again, and there is something in her voice. It’s something Kate can’t exactly name, but she knows then that the question goes deeper, somehow.

    “I guess back then when that movie was made it wasn’t as easy as it is now to make your two leads gay for each other,” she answers honestly, because that is probably the reason why it wasn’t specifically said on the film. Instead of leaving it there, though, she chooses to elaborate, “But I guess hypothetically, in the forties or fifties or whenever it was supposed to be contextualized, it would have been normal to have to accept that you can only call each other friends, outside of the two of you. Perhaps if you were lucky and were very sure that the people around you would support you, but even then… it could be dangerous if the word got out, I think. So they probably didn’t say it, but I like to believe that they did know, y’know, that the other was in love with them. Even if it was reckless.”

    Their breaths are in synch, each inhale and exhale the only noises that can be heard in the room. The older girl has stopped playing with her fingers, clutching them close to her heart instead. And now Kate is wide awake because she realizes what they are talking about, how secret relationships and coming out and homophobia and fear of talking feelings can actually relate to Yelena’s own story, and she wonders if she said the right thing. She wonders what exactly would be the right thing to say.

    She feels the Russian dipping her head gently, her lips grazing lightly the knuckles of the brunette’s hand that she has tightly clasped between her own. She opens Kate’s palm and leaves a kiss there, too, repeating the process with each tip of her fingers before scooting even closer to her. Yelena’s whole back is touching her front, their legs practically intertwined because of the lack of space, and Kate feels like her skin is on fire, like she’s hyper-aware of every single nerve ending on her body, like she can’t possibly be closer to this girl and yet she wants to pull her in more, to feel her more.

    Then Yelena sighs, a broken, forlorn thing, “Yeah I - I don’t… I don’t know. If I’d be brave enough. I don’t think so.”

    And Kate is absolutely sure that she hears a heart breaking just then. If it’s hers or Yelena’s, she doesn’t know.

    (It’s probably both)

    Chapter End Notes well this one's the longest to date and i should probably re-read it some more but here we are anyways. i hope you like it, i'll say it could be considered the beginning of the end before the beginning, if that even makes sense :( if you want to yell at me you totally can, too. we can cry together.

    VIII - Anyone Who Loves Me Chapter Notes Anyone who loves me - Charlotte Cardin See the end of the chapter for more notes Things have been weird. Not necessarily in a bad way, but definitely not in a good way either. Just plain weird.

    After their conversation on the couch - after Yelena’s almost-but-not-quite-sort-of confession - Kate had been speechless. Not in a way of speaking, she had truly been rendered wordless. She had just tightened her hold on the blonde, had almost cried with happiness when she was sure the Russian wasn’t actually planning on pushing her away and running into the night to never be seen again, and they had fallen asleep just like that. Holding each other in a relatively small couch, limbs intertwined.

    The next morning she had woken up alone, back still pushed against the cushion but no warm body keeping her there. She had wandered into the kitchen to find Cass and M.J. nursing their hangovers with dignity and cereal, and they had told her Yelena and Wanda had left about an hour before she opened her eyes. Something about Wanda needing to run a few things over with Vis now that they were… well. Over.

    Kate had taken that in stride, not giving it much thought that Yelena had chosen to leave with their recently broken-up friend rather than stay and say good morning to her. She had tried to qualm the spark of fear that ignited inside her when she woke up alone, the little voice in her head that told her this was going to go as the rest of their important conversations or moments went. Unspoken, buried and forgotten.

    Yelena had texted her as soon as she arrived home, telling her all about their trip over to Wanda’s ex and how they both had handled things like actual grown-ups, how there had been a few tears shed but overall it had been a great success. She had asked her if she had slept well on the couch. If her side hurt as much as did hers. She sent her a picture of Fanny dressed in a yellow and green sweatshirt and had reminded her to drink water throughout the day so she wouldn’t get a hangover as bad as their friends’.

    And she had also completely avoided mentioning their conversation.

    It almost felt unreal. As if it had been a figment of Kate’s imagination, a genuine illusion she had come up with on the brink of sleep. If it wasn’t for the subtle way Yelena now fidgeted with her rings whenever there was a lull in the conversation, looking on edge and skittish at the thought that the brunette might bring it up, she would have actually believed it was.

    But as things always went between them Yelena didn’t move and Kate didn’t push, and now they were stuck in a limbo where they both knew there were things unsaid, but tried their best to ignore it so they could continue their relationship as it had been before.

    Kate would very much rather that than lose her altogether, thank you very much.

    That’s why when her phone began ringing on Tuesday night right as she was preparing her suitcase to go home for Thanksgiving, Kate wasn’t surprised to see the name of her Russian friend reflected on the screen, “Hey Yel, are you calling to remind me to bring you back your blue scarf before I leave? Because I swear you don’t have to worry, I’ve got it right here and -“

    “- Kate,” the voice on the other end sounds rough and scratchy, and the younger girl realizes something is wrong when her last name doesn’t fall out of Yelena’s lips as it has been doing for as long as they’ve known each other, “Kate, can you - can you come pick me up?”

    She hadn’t even finished pronouncing the last few words and Kate was already tugging on her converse, hastily pushing one arm through her coat and digging for her car keys on the drawer. “On my way. Tell me where you are,” she tried her best to sound calm even if her heart was beating unhealthily fast on her chest. Yelena hadn’t said anything especially worrisome, but she just knows that stutter and that cadence in her voice, she’s pretty sure there were tears on her eyes as they spoke.

    There was muttering on the other side of the line, Yelena quietly talking to herself in what Kate guessed was Russian, “Babe, I know I told you I was gonna learn that language of yours in Duolingo but you’re going to have to help me out a bit, okay? I need to know where you are so I can pick you up,” she was already poised behind the wheel, her car running and her foot hovering on the gas.

    “I think I’m close to the station,” the blonde sounded unsure, as if she was taking in on her surroundings for the first time now, “I think I see… Yeah, that’s the light of the entrance. I’m here,” she said clearing her throat. Kate took a breath for the first time in what felt like forever. The main station was fifteen minutes away, ten if she drove as she did back home, “Okay listen, I want you to go inside and wait there for me. I’ll be there in no time at all, I promise.”

    If she pulls through a couple of red lights on her way, well. She’ll pay the fine when it comes.

    She parks her car right by the door and gets out so quickly she almost forgets her keys inside, only calming down once the warm, humid air of the inside hits her face and her eyes find a familiar green coat sitting among the rows and rows of empty chairs. It’s almost eleven on a weekday, the station deserted as it can be with just a few people wandering around, probably about to take a train somewhere to spend the holidays with their family.

    Yelena hasn’t seen her yet, green eyes downcast as she plays with the ends of her coat belt, so Kate uses the opportunity to take her in. She doesn’t seem to be hurt. Her back is hunched and her elbows are digging into her knees, but other than tense she seems fine. Kate can almost breathe normally by the time she sits next to her, “Hey you,” she says gently, poking her friend’s shoulder with her finger. The blonde looks up then, redrimmed eyes and a soft smile on her face, “Hi.”

    Any remaining thoughts about their weirdness and their unmentioned conversations leave Kate the moment she sees her. She can think of little else but to take her in her arms.

    She doesn’t try to ask what happened, knows now is not the time nor the place, so she just stands up and offers the older girl her hand to lead her to the car, “Wanna get out of here?”

    The ride back is quiet, only the music playing on the radio station breaking the silence.

    Yelena is deep in her thoughts, eyes gazing out of the window and flickering to Kate’s face every few minutes, as if to make sure she’s really there. The brunette doesn’t ask where she’s driving her, she knows her well enough to understand that if Yelena called her and asked her to pick her up, she didn’t just want a ride home.

    While they wait for the elevator the blonde takes her hand in hers and squeezes, doesn’t let go after. Kate figures that she got it right, and for her that’s a loud thank you.

    Lucky’s delighted to see Yelena again, and he actually manages to force a giggle out of her friend so she bows to reward him later with the few french fries that are left on the plastic container in the fridge. She’s still unlacing her shoes, coat discarded somewhere between the table and a chair when the older girl surprises her by talking first, “I saw them. Kissing.”

    Yelena’s not looking at her, still fully concentrated on scratching her dog’s ears thoughtfully, but her expression betrays her. There’s a downturn in the tilt of her lips, one that only appears when she’s trying really hard to remain neutral but her mouth is not cooperating, and then there are the two soft lines that draw themselves between her eyebrows, the telltale sign that she’s probably forcing herself not to cry. All that added to the slightly runny nose and shinny wet eyes she had been sporting on the way here are enough proof for Kate to know this is serious. But she’s also completely lost, because whose kiss could upset the Russian so bad for her to end up running away in the middle of the night?

    She takes a seat next to the other girl, Lucky jumping off the couch and trotting to the kitchen - probably in hopes of finding something on his bowl - simultaneously leaving Yelena without an excuse to keep stalling. Kate figures her silence and her face must reflect that she’s not following her, because the blonde sighs and elaborates, “Wanda and Natasha.”

    If Kate was lost before, she’s completely dumbfounded now, “You saw Wanda and Natasha.

    Kissing,” she mimics back, trying to see where exactly her ears disconnected from her brain and messed up the message. Yelena only nods, though, making her shake her head, “You saw our Wanda kissing your sister Natasha?” and as soon as those words are out she realizes she’s treading dangerous territory, the blonde’s head snapping up with pursed lips as if daring her to question it, “No, okay. Okay, I’m sorry, I totally believe you. It’s just that… Wanda and Natasha?” she can’t wrap her head around it.

    “Wanda and Natasha,” it sounds resolute, and it sounds like it hurts.

    Kate’s brain is trying to process this information, her first thought being You fucking go Wanda because really, she only saw Natasha once but she’s almost as beautiful as Yelena herself, and that’s saying something. But then she looks at the girl sitting next to her, at the way she’s holding her arms around her legs as if trying to take the less space possible, at how her forehead is resting on her knees and her eyes seem closed but her breath is definitely not calm. She recalls everything she knows about her past, every conversation they’ve had about her sister and their problems and their parents’ problems. She remembers how she said she only wanted to have someone on her side, and how now this could look dangerously like a betrayal to her.

    She thinks, briefly, of her words on the couch.

    Kate doesn’t necessarily understand, but she gets thinks she gets her. “Do you want to talk about it?” she asks tentatively, placing a hand on the small of her back to draw her attention. At first the blonde shakes her head, but after a few breaths she caves, “I had left like, half an hour ago. I wanted to go to the library to get some books and get some reading done but then I realized I left my notes, so I went back. They weren’t expecting me, of course,” she says bitterly, “I saw them. They tried to explain it to me, said they had… that they loved each other, but neither of them thought -,“ Yelena huffs, angrily or disappointed or maybe both, “They lied to me.”

    “So that’s probably why Wanda broke up with Vis,” it comes out more like a statement than as a question, just Kate thinking out loud, but Yelena nods anyways, “Seems like it. I didn’t stick around long enough to get the details. Just turned around and left. I started walking around, didn’t know where to go. And then I called you, ”she looks almost apologetic now, sad green eyes boring into hers.

    The brunette grabs the blanket that’s lying on the back of her couch and throws it around her friend, passing her own arm around the older girl’s shoulder in the process, “As you should. I told you I’d pick you up whenever, didn’t I?” she shoots rhetorically, wanting to make sure Yelena understands she’s not a burden at all, “The real question here is, what do you wanna do now, babe?”

    She doesn’t want to meddle but she’s sure Wanda and Natasha must be going out of their minds with worry, she probably has a million voicemails from the redhead by now. And she kind of gets them, too. Kate feels for everyone involved in this mess. After all, it must not be easy having to choose between people you love. She sorrowfully thinks they shouldn’t have to choose at all, but sometimes love is difficult like that.

    Yelena rubs her palms over her eyes hard enough to redden the skin there and Kate has to fist her fingers to stop herself from reaching out, “I don’t know. I don’t want to see them right now because they’re going to want to talk, and I don’t. And I know me, I know I’ll say things I’ll regret but I just - I’m so fucking tired of being always the one left behind,” she whispers heartbrokenly and Kate feels her heart clench painfully in her chest.

    “Come with me then,” she hasn’t thought this through like, at all, but right now the idea of leaving Yelena behind to deal with all of this by herself seems downright wrong, “Come with me to New York. We can spend the holidays there and you can talk to them after giving it some time. It’ll only be four days anyways.”

    The older girl peeks at her cautiously, something very aking to hope shining in her eyes, “Won’t your family want to spend time with you? I don’t want to intrude,” she says honestly, and Kate’s certain the other girl wouldn’t hold it against her if she changed her mind and decided that she shouldn’t come. That’s exactly what roots her in the idea, “It’s just my mom and her boyfriend and a very big bunch of rich, unknown people coming over to eat and drink for free. If you’re willing to put up with snobs, there’s no problem at all. In fact, you’d be kind of saving me from going through it alone.”

    She makes a show of winking and nudging at her friend, grinning victoriously when laughter erupts from her. There’s a voice in the back of her mind that’s yelling at her, warning her about the dangerous territory she’s threading, but like the grown up she is, she chooses to ignore it.

    Their laughter calms down while Kate works up the courage she needs to continue, “Wanda needs to know, though. She’s probably going crazy right now thinking of every impossible thing that may have happened to you. Your sister too, I bet. They should know that you’re here and that you’re fine, and to not expect you in a few days,” she tries her best to not sound too much like a mom, but she isn’t sure she succeeds. Yelena doesn’t look all that convinced, so she presses on, “I can call her now and we can drop by your house tomorrow to pick some of your stuff. Or I can go by myself and you tell me what to bring.”

    She expects a bit of protest, for Yelena to grumble about it for at least a couple of minutes before begrundly accepting that Kate’s right and giving in. What the brunette doesn’t expect at all is to have strong arms throw themselves around her neck and to hear the blonde’s voice so extremely close to her ear, “Thank you, Kate Bishop.”

    The younger girl doesn’t know if it’s the earnestness on her friend’s voice or the fact that her last name is back on the equation, but she’s absolutely delighted at her victory. She squeezes the other girl just as tight and tries not to dwell on how much she had missed this these last few days.

    She ends up calling Wanda once Yelena jumps into the shower, making a quick exit to her roof the moment she hears the water running. She doesn’t want the blonde to overhear Kate talking about her, and at the same time, the younger girl feels like she owes their friend a good explanation. Probably some support, too.

    Exactly as she anticipated, there were five missed calls from Wanda and another three from an unknown number that Kate’s willing to bet on it being Natasha’s. And just as she expected, the redhead picks the phone before the second ring, “Kate, is she with you?”

    There’s another voice behind hers, one that Kate vaguely recognizes, asking the same question over and over again, “Is she okay?”

    Guilt brews at the pit of her stomach and she suddenly feels terrible for waiting almost an hour before calling them. She should have texted as soon as they made it back to her apartment, “Wan, breath. She’s here, she’s in the shower right now. And she’s okay, I swear,” she keeps her voice as soothing as she can, as if she were talking to a lost child. Wanda takes a shuddering breath on the other end, a whimper escaping her throat before she starts repeating everything to Natasha. She decides to make it short and to the point, like ripping a bandage, “She told me what happened between you guys. She called me a while ago and asked me to pick her up so I brought her here, but she’s fine. I’m really sorry I didn’t call before.”

    After a few seconds and some fondling on their part, Kate hears both their voices through the static, “I’ve put you on speaker, Kate,” and yeah, that one’s definitely not Wanda’s.

    “Oh, um. Okay,” she stumbles a bit at the imposing figure she remembers Natasha being, but finds her footing quite smoothly, “Hi, Natasha. It’s Kate.”

    Tremendously smooth indeed.

    The two women on the other end are sufficiently distraught not to laugh at her, “Wanda says my sister’s there with you?” she tries to pose it as a statement but it ends up coming as a question. Kate nods a bit before she realizes they can’t see her, “Yeah, yeah she’s here. She’s fine. We were talking and I couldn’t get around telling you guys she was with me before. I’m sorry.”

    “And she told you what happened?” now that’s Wanda’s voice, but it’s buried beneath guilt and shame and Kate’s heart breaks a bit because this girl is her friend too, and she hates to hear her like that.

    “She did, but listen guys you did nothing wrong. She was just blindsided, I guess. And now she’s hurt because you kind of kept it from her. But give her a few days and she’ll come around, I’m sure,” she doesn’t want to cross any lines but feels like they may need to hear it, “There’s no need to be ashamed of anything, is what I mean. I won’t say a thing to anyone either, so don’t worry about that.” She hopes they understand what she means because she’s not sure she can get more specific without dying from embarrassment.

    There’s a long silence on the line and Kate almost wonders if the call has been cut, Natasha’s voice stopping her from checking, “Thank you, Kate. That’s - yeah, thank you. Will she stay with you tonight?”

    “Well, I actually told her she could with me to New York for Thanksgiving. She said she needed some space and I didn’t want to leave her alone… I hope I didn’t overstep,” she mutters closing her eyes as if afraid of their reaction. Or rather, of Natasha’s reaction.

    They go silent again and the brunette would truly love to see the expression on their faces, she feels like she’s missing a whole other conversation right now. Finally, she hears a sigh, “No, no. Of course not. She wouldn’t want to stay here anyway. At least we’ll know where she is, and that she’s safe,” Natasha seems tired, but other than that Kate thinks that it went amazingly well.

    “Thank you for taking care of her, Katie,” Wanda’s voice makes her wish she could teleport over there just to give the redhead the biggest hug ever. She bows to herself that she will, as soon as she sees her. Which will more than likely be tomorrow.

    Kate tells them they’ll go there around tomorrow morning to pick some of Yelena’s clothes before heading south, and Wanda offers to prepare a bag for her so they don’t lose much time. She promises to keep them updated at least once a day, and even offers to show Natasha her license when they drop by so she can make sure she’s a hones to god certified driver.

    They don’t give any details about their relationship, don’t mention the issue of the fight, and Kate doesn’t ask. It’s not her place to, anyway.

    The call ends shortly after, with Natasha bidding her goodnight and thanking her again, and Wanda muttering an I love you that Kate more than willingly reassures back.

    It’s suspiciously quiet when she makes it back to her apartment and worry fills her when she doesn’t see Yelena. For a second she wonders if the other girl has made a run for it. Perhaps she has decided she doesn’t want to risk a very pending and possibly highly emotional conversation with Kate in exchange for a few days away. She calms down the second she enters her bedroom, though, the sight there almost making her melt into a puddle on the floor.

    Her friend is curled on top of her bed, Lucky pressed closely into her side with his head bracketed between her arms. She’s wearing the clothes Kate laid for her - her clothes - and her hair is still humid and curling in waves from the shower. She’s also fast asleep, probably exhausted after all the running and all the crying. She doesn’t have the heart to wake her up, not even to tuck her into bed, so instead she searches as quietly as she can through her wardrobe and throws her other comforter on top of her, seeing as her muscles relax and her nose twitches in contentment.

    She’s more than happy to take the couch tonight.

    Kate wakes up to the smell of coffee and a note from Yelena saying that she’s taken Lucky out for a walk and that she’s left some toast for her in the microwave. She has time to have breakfast and take a quick shower before both blondes come trotting through the door, her dog’s tail thumping happily on the floor and a fresh, happy smile on the Russian’s face.

    She helps Kate finish her suitcase by picking her outfits while the brunette searches for her toiletries in the bathroom, and once they manage to strap Lucky securely on the back seat - with a lot of gentle parenting on Yelena’s part and a bit of bribing with pizza crust on his owner’s part - Kate’s fingers hover over the engine button. She chances a look at the girl next to her, sees her nibbling at her thumbnail, “I told them we’re coming. We can just pick up your bag and leave, no pressure,” she says reassuringly, one hand on the wheel and the other on the blonde’s knee.

    Strong fingers wiggle themselves in the spaces between her own, a soft squeeze and a nod the only response she gets from her friend.

    Yelena waits in the car while Kate jogs the few steps between the sidewalk and the doorway of the building where Wanda’s waiting for them, a rolling suitcase at her feet and a duffle bag hanging from her shoulder. Natasha’s nowhere to be seen, and she figures they may have had the same idea as Yelena and her: let the more neutral parts of the encounter handle the conversation.

    She pulls the redhead into her arms the moment she’s within reach, the older girl pliant in her embrace. Kate hears her sniff but pretends she doesn’t notice, “How are you holding up, Wan?” she asks instead, worry clouding her blue eyes as she searches her friend’s face closely. She looks like she didn’t get much sleep, but it doesn’t seem that she’s been crying, so there’s that.

    Wanda shoots a tender smile, “I’m fine. We both are. Just a bit worried, and sad. I didn’t mean to hurt her, Kate. I didn’t want to fail her,” she talks so fervently that even if she didn’t know her, Kate would believe every word she says. She does know her, though, and knows how much she cares for the girl sitting in her car, so it only makes it more dispairing.

    “Hey, no. Stop that. Don’t beat yourself up. Things happened that way and there’s room to fix it, okay? That’s what matters,” she ducks her chin to maintain eye contact, wanting to make sure the redhead knows she doesn’t blame her at all, “She loves you guys. Both of you. If she didn’t she wouldn’t have cared about any of it. You didn’t tell her and I’m sure you had your reasons. Give it a few days and she’ll be willing to listen to them. At least, as willing as Yelena can get,” she finishes jokingly, trying to lighten the conversation.

    Her mama may not have made her the brightest but she made her funny, thank god. Wanda cracks a grin and throws her arms around her waist again, burning her face in the crook of her neck, “Don’t let her push you away. She will try, because she can’t help her. They are more similar than they like to admit. You don’t let her,” she repeats pointing a finger at her chest as she talks, and Kate’s not sure what she’s talking about exactly but she nods. She’s not letting Yelena go anywhere.

    Her friend must be satisfied with her nod because she cups her cheek and kisses her on the forehead, “Okay, now go before she starts getting anxious.”

    Lucky woofs at her when she opens the trunk to load the bags, and Yelena smiles fleetly at her when she gets behind the wheel again, “Everything okay?” she asks when Kate’s buckled her seatbelt. The younger girl knows that’s her very sly way of asking if Wanda and Natasha are fine, but she humors her anyway, “Yeah, Wanda said to drive carefully and to text her when we get there. She sent you a kiss.”

    A frown appears on the blonde’s forehead as she bites her lower lip and looks out of the window. Wanda’s still there, waiting for them to pull into the highway. She lifts her hand hesitatingly when she finds green eyes on her, and to Kate’s surprise, Yelena waves back.

    The air feels loaded as they head down to the interstate, but the brunette had kind of anticipated it. That’s why she had her phone ready to hand it over to her friend, “Why don’t you play some music?”

    Yelena’s face lit up immediately, asking for the code to unlock the screen with fingers eager to dive through Kate’s library, “Do you have preferences? It’s your car, after all,” she’s dawdling, the younger girl’s pretty sure she already has the song picked and ready to play. A quick glance to her left confirms it, so she just smirks and shakes her head, “Music’s actually on the copilot, so surprise me.”

    A piano starts thrumming through the speakers as American Pie starts to play, Yelena joining in the moment the lyrics begin to roll. She’s looking out the window as cars pass by, chin on her hand and a content smile as she mouths the words. Lucky’s lightly snoring in the back seat, tongue poking out when she looks through the rearview mirror. Kate idly thinks that this is a million times better than making the road alone.

    She makes a quick pitstop at the first gas station they cross, filling the tank while Yelena takes Lucky to stretch his legs and do his business. The younger girl waits for them to return before going inside to pay, “You want something from the store?” she asks as she holds the blonde’s door open for her. The Russian looks at her hopefully as she buckles herself in, “Do you think they’ll have licorice?”

    Kate is totally going to blame the stench of gas filling her brain for the little swoon she has to hold in at the tone of her friend’s voice, “I sure hope they do.”

    It actually takes about thirty seconds to find all kinds of licorice amongst the candy section, the brunette picking up a few to make sure it lasts them for the rest of the journey. She’s got a sweet tooth, and she’s almost positive Yelena can win her on that.

    When it’s her turn to pay she points outside to the pump where her car is parked, “Number three. And these too, please,” she says as she begins to dig through her bag for her wallet. The lady at the counter scans the packages and punches a few numbers on the screen before looking outside again, “Hey kid, I think your girl’s trying to tell you something.”

    Kate looks up puzzled, eyes scanning the room to see if there is someone else she could be talking to, but it’s only them. She follows the cashier’s gaze outside and finds Yelena shaking an empty bottle of water by the window, eyebrows up and fingers pointing at it. “My guess is she wants some water,” the clerk smirks at the blonde’s antics, and Kate can only bite the inside of her cheek to keep from smiling like a fool. She gives her friend a thumbs up, barely containing a laugh when the blonde fogs up the window with her breath to draw a heart and an arrow while winking at her.

    “Ah, young love,” the lady coos sweetly at her as she counts a few coins to give her change, “You two have a safe trip, miss.”

    They spend the following three hours singing (terribly) every and each song Yelena plays, the blonde taking her duty as a copilot very seriously. She also opens the water bottle for Kate so she doesn’t have to take her eyes off the road and passes piece after piece of candy whenever the brunette runs out. Somewhere between Stamford and Greenwich, she takes Kate’s hand in hers under the pretense to wipe her fingers with a tissue, claiming the sugary powder was beginning to stick to the wheel.

    After, Yelena threads her fingers through hers.

    Kate never gets her hand back.

    IX - Rapide Chapter Notes Rapide - Mahmood See the end of the chapter for more notes “Well, you certainly didn’t mention that you were ridiculously rich.”

    They had made it to the city a bit later than Kate had originally planned, the traffic as dense and slow as she remembered. They were now thankfully parked at her mom’s garage, the brunette busy getting all their stuff from the car while Yelena holds tightly onto Lucky’s leash, the puppy still half-sleep against her leg.

    “That’s because I’m not,” Kate shoots back easily, having anticipated her friend’s reaction. She had never specified what her mother did for a living, nor how she sustained herself. Most of her friends either kept a half-time job like Peter or were on a full-ride scholarship like M.J. or Yelena herself. And just to note, Kate had also been awarded a scholarship to pay for her studies, it was just that she could have made it without. But that’s not something she enjoyed discussing, so she tended to keep it to herself. It was nothing to be ashamed of, she knew, but she’d had a few experiences with people changing the way they treated her once they learned that Eleanor Bishop was one of the richest women on the east coast, so she was a bit apprehensive.

    She hears Yelena chuckle, a disbelieving smile and an arched eyebrow pointed her way.

    “What? I am not rich. My mom is,” she tries to defend herself, but knows she’s done for when the blonde throws her head back with laughter, “That’s exactly what a rich kid would say, Kate Bishop.” She seemed entirely too amused by Kate’s flush, but there was a fond smile on her lips that made the younger girl smile back despite her best efforts to remain indignant.

    “That did sound a bit like something a spoiled kid would say,” she concedes, much to her friend’s delight, “But anyway, it’s not relevant. Or would you have liked me more if you had known my mom was kinda rich?” she squints her eyes at the Russian, trying to mask the dip of self-consciousness in her voice with humor.

    Yelena purses her lips and tilts her head slightly, as if studying her, “No. I can’t think of anything that would have made me like you more than I already did when we met,” she answers, shrugging delicately a shoulder before stalking over to take both their bags, leaving a stunned Kate behind with their suitcases. She makes a beeline for the elevator, Lucky dutifully trailing behind her, “Are you coming, Kate Bishop? Or do I have to carry you too?”

    The brunette manages to close her agape mouth and the trunk in a relatively smooth motion considering her brain was scrambled all over the place. It was a very nice, friendly thing to say, she tried to convince herself, you taking it like flirting is just wishful thinking, Bishop.

    She may be smiling like an idiot when she reaches her friend, but if Yelena notices she doesn’t mention it. They push all their stuff into the elevator and Kate clicks the twelfth-floor number, “Ready to meet the parents?” she blurts out before she can think of the implications of that statement. She remains from banging her head on the wall when she sees her friend actually bits her lip before shighin, fingers toying with the rope of Lucky’s leash, “I have never done it before,” she confesses nervously, “I’ve never been… never gotten close enough with anyone for them to introduce me to their parents. Not even Wanda’s.”

    It’s in moments like these that Kate realizes how often she forgets Yelena didn’t have the same childhood the rest of them did. She’s suddenly reminded that there wasn’t probably much of meeting your friend’s parents when you grow up in an orphanage. And after they made it to America Kate’s pretty sure she went to live on her own, like most students do once they go to college. It’s not extremely weird to not meet your college friend’s parents, either, especially if you don’t get too personal with them. So this being Yelena’s first time meeting someone’s family shouldn’t have come as a surprise, really.

    A surge of protectiveness overcomes her suddenly, softened by the poorly concealed unease look on her friend’s face. She passes a comforting arm around her shoulders dragging her close, “Hey, that’s fine. They’ll love you. Mom’s pretty decent and Jack’s cool. Well, he’s actually a nerd but he’s a cool nerd.”

    “You think so?” the vulnerability in her voice makes Kate regret not bringing this up before. She’s suddenly grateful to have an overprotective mother and an excitable, childishly fun stepfather. At least she doesn't have to worry about being Yelena’s first bad experience. “Of course I’m sure. Besides,” she decides to add, gathering her courage, “I like you, that’s all the reason they need for them to do the same.”

    Her words seem to have the desired effect if the small, sincere smile on Yelena’s face is anything to go by. Her shoulders lose some tension under her arm, and when they reach their floor she’s the first one out. Kate smiles and begs whoever’s listening to let this go as smoothly as possible, “Hello!” she shouts into the empty foyer once she’s dropped everything on the floor, “Mom? Jack?”

    “Ah, here you are! I was about to call you, Kate. I was beginning to get worried,” a voice calls from the living room. Kate’s about to drag Yelena over there when Jack appears down the stairs, “I thought I heard your voice! Hello kid,” he says once he reaches them, pulling Kate into a hug that the brunette returns with soft pats to his back. He lets her go quicker than he normally would, walking directly to her friend, “You must be Yelena. Nice to meet you, my dear,” he greets the blonde, offering his hand, “Kate mentioned my wife that you were beautiful, I see she was clearly understating it.”

    Of course, just because they were going to be nice to Yelena it didn’t mean she wasn’t going to go through the standard embarrassment of bringing someone you like over to your parents’ house.

    Kate barely holds back a groan when her mom emerges from the living room, “Come on, dear. Play nice,” she admonishes Jack before stopping by Kate and dropping a quick kiss to her cheek, “Hello, sweetheart.”

    “Hi, mom,” she smiles to her mother, suddenly aware of how much she had missed her these past months. Admittedly, it has been nice to leave the city and start anew, but the truth was that despite everything her mom was one of her closest confidants. She’d had to revise the terms of their confidentiality agreement, though, she totally shouldn’t be snitching to Jack.

    “My god, she truly wasn’t exaggerating. You are gorgeous dear,” Eleanor said as she pulls Yelena into a hug, the blonde barely having time to thank her through her blush and return it before her mother lets go of her and takes her arm with hers, “Let’s go sit down a bit. You must be starving, I’ll get you something to eat before you girls go upstairs.”

    Kate shoots a reassuring smile at Yelena as she hurries to follow them, thinking idly that perhaps she should just stop confiding in her mother altogether. Jack winks at her and he pinches her arm as he makes a show of running past her with Lucky on his side. The brunette can just shake her head and resign herself to whatever happens these days.

    Jack brings them each a glass of water and promises to have dinner ready in a bit, “I don’t know if we’ll be able to join you ladies. We’ve been dealing with some unexpected problems with our lawyers, lots of late calls and late meetings. I’d rather see them tonight than have them ruin our day tomorrow.”

    It’s not unusual for her mother to keep the most ridiculous working hours, but the mention of lawyers makes Kate frown, “Is everything okay? Something I should know?” she asks her mother, trying her best to gauge her reaction. Unlike Kate, Eleanor was exceptionally good at keeping a poker face.

    “No, dear. Nothing to worry about, just bureaucracy,” she waves her hand nonchalantly, “But let’s not bore our lovely guest with work. Kate mentioned you’d never been here before, Yelena?”

The younger girl touches her knee with hers encouragingly. “No, this is my first time here. And thank you for having me, by the way. It’s really nice of you, most of all during family holidays,” it’s admirable how confident she sounds, if Kate didn’t know her as well as she does she would think she’s unperturbed. She does know her, though, knows the fidgeting of her thumb finger over her ring, the slight pink tint on the high points of her cheeks. I mean it,” she reassures her motherly. You’ll have to come by again so we can all spend some time together!”

    Yeah, Kate is definitely never telling them anything ever again.

    “I would love to,” Yelena replies eagerly, a glowing smile on her face as she looks over her shoulder at Kate, round green eyes searching dubiously in baby blues. Kate thinks she would endure a whole year of this continuous mortification if that meant her friend was going to keep that smile, “We can come back on Christmas, you’ll get to see the lights.”

Her mother chuckles at her husband’s antics, looking at them exasperated and shaking her head dramatically. Yelena lets out a cute little laugh, something delicate but true, and the remaining tension leaves her body as she rests her back comfortably against the couch.

Yelena seems to actually look forward to it, so Kate doesn’t refrain from the impulse of kissing her mom’s cheek and hugging Jack goodbye.

    Whisky on the rocks is something my deceased grandfather would have ordered.”

Kate melts at that being directed at her and feels an overwhelming desire to kiss her, to close the gap between them and pour everything she’s feeling into it. Instead, she brings her body back against her, the older girl falling willingly and pliant in her arms.

    Hands wander through skin, fingers brushing and caressing while they rock softly on the bed, both taking the comfort they need from the other. Kate’s feeling drained, and by the way Yelena grows heavier on top of her she guesses her friend is exhausted, too.

    The thought of having to pull away, though, is unbearable right now.

    “Stay?” she whispers quietly on the blonde’s ear, tightening her grip on her.

    Yelena turns her head up, makeup smudged with tears, eyes shining, and nose red. She’s still the most beautiful girl I've ever seen, Kate thinks belatedly as the Russian looks at her tenderly and nods.

    She really wishes that she has understood her now, too.

    Don’t you ever leave.
