    The long, shuddering breath the Russian takes seems painful, Kate wincing internally when she sees her bite the inside of her cheek as her eyes close and her face turns upwards. Then, a forced smile appears on Yelena’s lips as she tilts her head back to look at the younger girl briefly before her eyes dart to the window again, “Yes, yes. I’m sorry if I’ve been weird. I’m really tired and I’m not feeling all that great. But everything’s fine.”

    Everything is not us, Kate wants to scream, I’m not talking about everything, I’m asking about you and me.

    Instead, she nods, because she already knew this was a lost cause and the only thing she’s going to get if she continues pushing is to drive the other girl farther away. She can’t bear that thought, honestly, so sue her if she’s choosing to act like a coward, “Oh, okay. Yeah. It’s fine, I get it,” she really doesn’t, but she’s exhausted and she kind of wants to crawl into her bed and cry, so she needs this to be as quick and painless as possible.

    Maybe Kate sounds like she’s about to burst into tears, that would explain the anguished look Yelena casts her way before biting her lip and steering her eyes down to her hands, “I really am sorry,” she repeats, distress and sorrow caressing her thickened accent, and Kate doesn’t know what she’s apologizing for anymore. Or she does, but she thinks she’s going to need to ignore it at least for a bit more. She might do something really pathetic like begin to weep right now, if not.

    “It’s okay,” she rasps out as she gulps, mustering all her willpower to lift a hand and place it gently on the blonde’s knee. Because even now, even when Yelena is hurting her, Kate can’t stand to see her in pain.

    The light touch seems to ignite something inside Yelena because suddenly there’s a soft push of lips against her own, a hand cradling tenderly her cheek as feels the Russian’s breath against her nose. She barely gets a second to relish in it, fingers hopelessly clinging at the older girl’s neck before Yelena pulls away. Kate doesn’t try to chase her - knows it’s hopeless - and watches defeatedly instead as the blonde unbuckles her seatbelt and turns to open the door.

    Yelena grabs her suitcase and her bag from the trunk and drops by the backseat to ruffle lovingly between Lucky’s ears before turning to Kate, “Text me when you get home,” she whispers, and the brunette can only nod and hold tighter onto the wheel, knuckles white with force. The other girl sighs resignedly and purses her lips, her eyes clouded with exhaustion as she seems to ponder something. Then, abruptly, she crawls over the seat to reach out for Kate’s lips again.

    The kiss is short and desperate and ends before either of them can really process it, and before anything else can be said Yelena shuts the door behind her and pushes her things up the way into her apartment. She turns around one last time when she reaches the building’s main entrance, but Kate doesn’t stay long enough to actually see the look on her face. She’s too busy trying not to drown in the finality that goodbye seemed to hold.

    By the time she makes it home she doesn’t know how she’s managed to hold everything together. The long drag of her legs along the stairs had seemed impossible, Kate had been sure her body would just collapse under its own weight and leave her there in the open, tired and sad and almost crying.

    Lucky yelps at her when she narrowly avoids stepping onto his tail, and the brunette’s not sure if she’s just dying for some comfort and projecting but she’s pretty sure his face is one of concern and sadness, too. As if he had understood what had happened between her and Yelena and knew he would not see her again soon, either.

    Okay, maybe that was a tad overdramatic, but she wasn’t feeling all that hopeful right now. The way Yelena had kissed her before she left… Kate didn’t want to sound conceited, but she knows she’s learned to understand the blonde’s actions as well as her words, and that kiss had felt like an ending.

    She’s having a hard time wrapping her head around everything that’s happened in under three days. She knows that she’s not imagining things, that Yelena feels the same way Kate does about her - at least to some extent. She has to. And sure, Kate understands the weight on the blonde’s shoulders, she understands why Yelena would be scared. What she doesn’t understand is why would she push her away instead of talking to her.

    Her eyes are burning and her nose is runny and there’s a pounding in her temples that she just knows won’t let her sleep tonight, but she decides that she’s going to hold back tears some more and stop thinking. She has to at least take her things out of her suitcase so future Kate doesn’t have to hurry off to class tomorrow with a killer headache and dirty clothes.

    The first thing that catches her eye when she opens her bag is her phone, and she remembers that she promised to text Yelena so the other girl knew she’d made it home safe.

    The childish, most selfish part of her brain wants to hold back just to see if the other girl will care enough to swallow whatever’s going down her throat to check on Kate first. The mature, more reasonable half knows deep down that’s borderline manipulation, and she may be hurt and angry but that doesn’t necessarily mean she wants Yelena to worry.

    (There’s also a tiny, minuscule part of her brain that’s plainly terrified doing it and Yelena not reaching out at all.)

    Resigned with her own inner monologue, Kate picks up the phone and types in a quick message. It’s short and there are no endearing terms or smiley faces because she might not want the blonde to worry about her being alive, but she sure as hell wants Yelena to know about her being angry.

    already home.

    Kate leaves her phone on her bed and begins to throw all her dirty clothes into the washing machine, stripping down and putting her road outfit inside, too. Then, marches into the bathroom ready for a soothing, scalding-hot shower before giving in and curling into her bed to weep like a baby.

    She stops dead in her tracks the second she catches a glimpse of herself in the mirror, though. She’s completely naked, face bare of makeup and hair up in a messy bun, but that’s not what makes her eyes begin to burn again.

    There, resting regally across her collarbones, is a gold pendant with a moon and a sun engraved on it. Yelena’s fucking necklace, warm and heavy against her skin. She had totally forgotten about it, its weight has become something she’s grown used to, even giving her comfort. If she tries hard enough, she can almost imagine that the warmth radiating from it came from the Russian herself.

    The tears come with vengeance now, not even a bit mindful of Kate’s carefully curated plan to hold back until she had everything under control. She starts sobbing almost desperately, hands reaching back clumsily as her fingers try to unclip the offending jewelry off her body. She has half a mind to just pull at it until it breaks, thinks she would even find some poetic justice in it, but she’s suddenly so tired her arms barely hold any strength in them.

    Instead, her fingers close around the gold charm, nails digging into her own palm as she squeezes the metal miserably. Her legs grow heavy under her and she lets herself fall softly to the floor, her head resting against the sink’s cold tiles.

    She stays there long enough for her legs to cramp and get numb and for her body to freeze all over. And Kate would love to say it’s her own self-esteem what brings her to her feet and out of the bathroom (if anyone were to ask that’s what she’d say), but the truth is that the sound of her phone beeping from her bedroom breaks through the haze in her mind and forces her to stand up.

    Wrapping a towel around her body, she nears her bed apprehensively. It kind of reminds her of the first time she texted Yelena, but now there is no giddy expectation bubbling in her stomach. Now, there’s dread about whatever one-word answer the blonde may have decided to shoot back at her.

    Okay, sleep well.

    She was expecting it, of course, but it still stings. There had been a glimmer of hope in her chest, the silly thought that perhaps the little bubble that was their text conversations may remain unstrained. It was stupid, though, to expect that to continue as if nothing had happened when clearly Yelena was closing off all around.

    Kate crawls into bed and buries herself under her comforter, face down into the pillow until it gets suffocating. She kind of wants to scream, be overdramatic like they are in the movies, but she doesn’t have the energy. She doesn’t even have the energy to cry about it anymore.

    Not even bothering to answer - as if there was something she could answer to that and not feel pathetic - she closes her eyes and wills herself to sleep. Hopefully tomorrow she’ll see things differently. Maybe tomorrow they’ll see each other in class and everything will be back to normal. Maybe they can fall back into their old little routine and New York will be but a perfect and distant dream.

    It’s a sacrifice Kate thinks she would be willing to make.

    Things are, of course, not different at all on Monday.

    Kate had somehow managed to wake up before her alarm, having enough time to get ready and walk quietly down the campus. The cold air had helped her clear her head, and miraculously there didn’t seem to be a headache lurking in the shadows. Overall, she felt optimistic and a bit silly for yesterday’s dramatics.

    All her good thoughts leave her when a yellow and black checkered coat and a blonde braid enter the classroom - alone, Kate notes cautiously as she wonders where is Wanda - and green eyes barely lift to acknowledge her. Her breath gets stuck on her lungs and she doesn’t even dare to move, afraid of startling the other girl as Yelena makes her way to sit next to her.

    At least she’s not changing her seat just to avoid her. Pitifully, Kate thinks that is something worth celebrating.

    “Hello,” the Russian says shortly as she begins to take out her laptop and notebook from her backpack, glancing up briefly and sending Kate and Peter a curt smile. Peter reaches around Kate to clap at Yelena’s shoulder fondly, the brunette noticing how the other girl stiffens briefly before slumping her shoulders again.

    “Hi, Yel,” she rasps out, her voice low.

    She ponders for a second how to proceed, what’s the best approach for this. She could ask how she’s feeling, but she guesses that would be highly unappreciated by the Russian seeing as they are not alone and she definitely doesn’t seem to be too keen on discussing it, even between the two of them. She could also opt for the classic are you okay, but it would undoubtedly reward her with either a nod or a plain yes, so that’s out, too.

    In the end, it’s Peter who decides for her, “Hey, Lena. Did you and Wanda get split up this morning? You always get here together. It’s like seeing a shooting star, catching one of you without the other here. I think it only happened on our first day.”

    Kate winces visibly because of course Peter doesn’t have the slightless clue about everything that went down before Thanksgivings. Sure, she briefly texted him and M.J., updating them on their little impromptu trip, but she had skidded over the main topics. She knew it wasn’t her place to tell, and her friends hadn’t pushed, either.

    Still, they probably thought it was just a fun, last-time idea and not a whole tribulation of family drama and betrayal.

    Yelena stops her mindless fidgeting and purses her lips, hands suspended above her desk. She doesn’t look at them when she answers, “I haven’t seen Wanda today, yet.” It’s all she says before she continues as if they weren’t sitting right next to her, and Kate can feel Peter’s questioning stare at the back of her neck. She sends him a later look, before trying again with the blonde, “Did you get to talk with her last night?”

    How did it go? Did you guys fix things? Are you okay?

    As if summoned, the redhead appears through the door and saves Yelena the trouble of answering. Wanda nears them with Bucky and Eddie trailing close behind, and Kate lets out a slow breath when she notices that the Sokovian lingers unsurely by their row of seats. She finally decides to sit next to Peter, and it’s wouldn’t necessarily be weird if there wasn’t an empty spot next to Yelena. None of the boys seem to notice, already lost in mindless conversation about their break, but Kate definitely does.

    So does Yelena, apparently, because she decides to reply, “I think that answers your question.” Her chin rests on the palm of her hand and her eyes are fixated on the board, but the brunette sees the tightness of her jaw and the fine lines between her eyebrows. She’s upset even if she’s trying not to show it.

    Kate glances to her right and feels a pair of clear green eyes looking at her. Wanda sends her a sweet, tired smile before glancing at the blonde sitting next to Kate forlornly. The redhead returns her attention to her and shakes her phone a bit in her hand before pointing to Kate’s bag. The younger girl nods shortly and smiles back at her friend just when their professor arrives.

    The rest of the classroom falls quiet as their lecture begins, and Kate waits a few prudential minutes before reaching for her phone discretely.

    Wait for me after class, we’ll talk.

    Kate looks at Yelena out of the corner of her eye, fingers typing aggressively and posture stiff, and decides that it’s safe to believe the blonde is completely ignoring her by now.

    you got it wan.

    Sneaking around Yelena turns out to be incredibly easy when they all agree to have lunch together after their lecture to catch up and the blonde - unsurprisingly - declines. Again, the rest of their friends pay her no mind. It wasn’t unusual for her to skip some of their meetings before, but Kate can’t help but feel a pang in her chest at the sight of Yelena’s retreating back.

    A warm hand curls around her elbow as Wanda falls into step next to her, “How are you feeling, Kate?”

    The question catches the brunette completely by surprise. She was expecting either some kind of interrogatory about Yelena’s stay in New York during the holidays, or a long tirade about whatever happened - or didn’t happen - last night between the blonde and Wanda. She has tried to ignore her own turmoil of feelings since yesterday, feeling a bit selfish about the idea of being so distraught when Yelena clearly had the shortest stick out of the both of them.

    Sometimes she swears Wanda is some kind of sorceress who’s able to read minds, “It’s okay to be upset, you know?” the older girl mutters softly as she holds her hand firmly on Kate’s arm, “Do you want to come over so we can talk?” She barely waits for Kate’s nod before addressing the three boys who’ve wandered ahead of them, “Guys, Kate and I are going to have to reschedule okay? We’ll see you tomorrow!”

    There’s some grumbling and pouting behind them (mostly Eddie’s) but Wanda just waves a hand and smiles sweetly at them and really, there’s not much to do against that. The unrelenting pressure of her hand is what keeps Kate moving forward, but she’s scrambling a bit, “Where are we going, exactly? Because Yelena is going to be at your place, and my apartment is in the other direction,” she says pointing a finger over her shoulder.

    Wanda just shakes her head and continues walking, “No, we are going somewhere else.

    There’s someone who wants to be a part of this conversation, too.”

    That someone turns out to be no other than Natasha Romanoff. They had spoken on the phone before, and the brunette had texted her while Yelena stayed with her in the city. She had met her in person once, too. So there’s no logical explanation for Kate’s overwhelming nerves when Natasha opens the door for them, “Hey baby. Hello, Kate Bishop.”

    God damn these fucking Russians and their fucking accent and their fucking need to call me by my last name. These sisters are going to be the death of me.

    Natasha steps aside to let them in, but it takes a gentle push at her back to get her to move - Wanda’s courtesy. She stumbles forward as gracefully as she can and pretends to keep her eyes on her shoes when the two redheads kiss. It’s short and sweet and both of them smile into it. She tries her best not to think about the rather similar kisses she’s shared with Yelena but still, pathetically, it almost brings tears to her eyes.

    “Take a seat, Kate,” Wanda gestures to the table and the brunette feels like she’s totally going to get interrogated now. It must show on her face because Natasha chuckles as she passes her and pats her shoulder, “Don’t worry, Kate. This will be mostly painless, I promise.”

    “Nat,” her friend chastises the older redhead with a stern voice, and Kate has to marvel at the thought of her sweet, gentle Wanda having the upper hand against such an imposing woman.

    She’s even more amazed when there’s not a single protest from the other side, just a huff and a, “I’m kidding, I’m kidding. Do you want something to drink, Kate? I’m making tea.”

    The brunette sits next to Wanda and nods, “Yeah, tea works for me. Thanks,” and then turns to her friend and asks softly, “Are you staying here, now? Yelena said you guys didn’t see each other last night.”

    Wanda shakes her head, “No, no. I was home last night, she just barreled in through the door without saying hello and then proceeded to barricade herself in her room. I knocked a few times but she didn’t answer, so I thought it would be better to give her space. This morning she was nowhere to be found when I woke up,” the Sokovian finishes looking up at Natasha, who’s balancing three cups in her hand as she also listens to her girlfriend’s explanation.

    “I guess she’s still hurt. Must not be ready to talk face to face yet,” the older woman sighs tiredly as she sits in front of them. She stirs her drink a few seconds before her eyes meet Kate’s, “I wanted to thank you for what you did for her, Kate. And for us, too. That’s why I had Wanda bring you over. Thank you for keeping us updated and for getting her to speak to me. I have no idea how you did it, really, but when she actually texted back I was stunned. Even if it was a fucking “yeah, everything’s great”, it was way more than what I expected, so thank you.”

    The knot on her throat is keeping her from speaking and she feels like she’s going to be sick. They think Yelena is still mad at them and Natasha is thanking Kate for taking care of her sister when she’s probably the reason why Yelena hasn’t spoken to any of them yet.

    “I think I messed up,” is what she finally manages to rasp out when she’s gathered enough nerve to talk, “She’s mad at me, not at you guys. I’m so sorry. I didn’t mean to upset her, things just got so, so out of hand. I mean - I didn’t realize they had gotten out of hand until everything exploded in our faces and when I wanted to fix it she was completely closed off, she didn’t even look at me! It has been a miracle that she’s dared to sit next to me today at all. But you saw her, Wan, she barely glanced my way and I -“

    A firm hand on her shoulder forces her to stop and take a breath, “Kate, relax. Tell us what happened and we’ll see if it is so terrible,” Natasha says from where she’s leaning over her. She’s moved to sit on the empty seat next to the brunette, Wanda placing a gentle hand on top of her own encouragingly, and now Kate really wants to cry.

    She’s debating with herself, unsure of what she should do. She wants to tell them everything that happened but at the same time, it feels like she’d be betraying Yelena’s trust in a way. Kate is pretty sure the last thing the blonde wants is for anyone to know what happened between them, especially not her sister. But she’s desperate and she needs help, so she decides to concede, “We kissed and she got scared, I think. I didn’t get a chance to talk with her about it because she totally avoided me. She still is. And now I’m afraid I may have driven her away for good.”

    Both women share a look and Kate hates the feeling of missing a conversation that’s happening right in front of her, but it doesn’t last long, “You understand she’s not actually out to anyone right? Probably not even to herself,” Natasha explains softly, not a trace of surprise in her voice.

    The younger girl hadn’t stopped to think about it, but it made sense that Yelena had never dared to say anything to anyone. It makes her heart hurt even more because fuck, what a heavy secret to carry on her own.

    Nodding quietly, Kate studies the older woman, “And yet you knew,” she means to phrase it as a question but it comes out as a statement. She eyes Wanda carefully for a second and adds, “You knew, too. She never said anything to you? You’re her best friend.”

    Natasha brings a hand up to her temple and presses her fingers to the bridge of her nose, “I had the feeling. She never confirmed it, of course, neither before nor after shit went down with our parents. But she’s my sister and even if it spites her, I know her. And then with you,” she motions to Kate with her hand, a small smile tilting in her lips, “You know we were separated for a few years. This is the first time we’ve been together since… everything, but she was still very upset when she came. And yet, she mentioned you when we were together, even when she hadn’t wanted to talk about anything with me, so I guessed you were pretty important.”

    “We never talked about boys,” Wanda chimes in, “Not about girls either, of course. She never seemed interested in anyone at all so I took it for what it was. I mean, it wouldn’t have been a problem either way. But there were little things, details I would catch and would make me wonder…” the redhead trails off, probably reminiscing some of those same details that now made a lot more sense in her head. She takes a breath before continuing, “I never pushed because I knew it was a sensitive subject, but it’s not something that blows my mind, either. And especially not that it happened with you, Kate. Besides, I didn’t expect to like women and yet, here I am.”

    Kate lets out a chuckle at that as Natasha winks at the younger redhead, but their smiles disappear as quickly as they came, “She’s terrified, Kate. I don’t know exactly how much she told you but our parents are - they won’t like it. And she’s already had to deal with my mess, so I can only imagine the dread she must feel at the idea of going through that again,” the Russian mutters sadly, almost as if she was talking to herself. The brunette nods, remembering very similar words falling from Yelena’s lips a few nights ago.

    “Right now the three of us represent what scares her the most, so it makes sense she’s not all that willing to speak with us,” Wanda rests her forehead in the palm of her hand defeatedly, “The best thing we can do is give her space. She needs to sort her feelings first, but Kate - you did nothing wrong, okay?”

    There’s a hand on her shoulder and another on her knee and Kate feels like she doesn’t deserve to be the one receiving this. It’s not right that she gets to be here crying while Yelena is alone in her room thinking god knows what about herself. Especially not when she’s the one who pushed too hard, “No, guys, you don’t get it. I should have known better, I should have gone slower. Because I knew all of this, she told me. And yet I got greedy and I wanted everything and now whatever little progress she had made I’ve crushed it,” she doesn’t sob, thankfully, but the self-loathing in her voice is equally sorrowful.

    “Kate, listen. My sister is a lot of things, but a pushover is not one of them. Whatever happened between you and her, it wasn’t one-sided. She would have stopped you and she’d let you know. This mess comes from what happens after, when she processes things and overthinks them and lets herself be pulled under all the fucking hate that’s been spit our way our whole lives. You were the trigger, but not the problem,” the solemnity on Natasha’s voice finally makes a tear spill from Kate’s eyes, but it also feels like a weight has been lifted from her shoulders. She still thinks she overstepped, but hearing those words from Yelena’s sister makes her feel a bit better.

    She wipes at her eyes with the back of her hand and nods, “What should I do, then?” she asks as her blue eyes dance between the redheads flanking her sides. They seem as lost as she feels, but at least now the burden’s shared and it doesn’t feel as heavy.

    “Now we wait. Don’t push her, she’ll probably lash out and it’ll be worse. We both tend to say a lot of things we don’t mean when we’re feeling cornered. It’s definitely something we should try to work on,” Natasha confesses regretfully as Wanda snorts knowingly, “Just let her sort things out on her own and don’t take anything personally.”

    A thought occurs to Kate then, “How long did it take her to forgive you and come to you on her own?”

    The Russian sighs dejectedly, “Years.”

    No point in lying. Kate had known the answer before she asked the question. “And you really believe the best we can do is to leave her alone?” the thought of Yelena drowning in her own hateful ideas makes her want to throw up, “I feel like she’s going to get swallowed by all of this if we don’t pull her out.”

    Now it’s Wanda’s time to sigh as she looks sadly at her, “Kate, we can’t tell you what to do. But Nat’s gone through this with her before and so have I, to a smaller extent. We can only share with you what our experiences have shown us.”

    Patience, she remembers then, don’t let her push you away. It wasn’t mere advice, it was a lesson learned. Kate links her fingers through her friend’s hand and squeezes in understanding.

    She was going to have to find the balance between those two things by herself, now.

    Chapter End Notes we've got the drama! for how long, remains to be seen.

    full disclosure now, this story is (although obviously modified to fit these two) heavily heavily inspired by a personal experience that i have wanted to write down for a bit now.

    some of you have mentioned that you relate to this on a personal level (either with kate or yelena), so i thought i'd share my part.

    also, this week is being hell and i may not be able to update again until next week so i'm sorry but i love you guys :( XIII - If I Say Chapter Notes If I Say - Mumford & Sons See the end of the chapter for more notes Kate keeps her resolve to give Yelena time for almost three weeks.

    Honestly, it’s three weeks more time than what she had initially thought she’d manage, so she believes it’s a testament to how much she took her conversation with Natasha and Wanda to heart. She has even kept in touch with the older redhead since then, surprisingly. Of course, they were nowhere close to being friends, but Natasha had checked up on her every once in a while, even shared whatever little scraps Yelena fed her, and Kate really appreciates it.

    Out of the three of them, Wanda had been the one to take the brunt of the Russian’s silent treatment the hardest, what with the whole living together thing. Yelena wasn’t mean, but it still sucked to share space with someone who barely blinked your way. That was also the main reason why she had started to spend some nights at Natasha’s apartment, too. Giving them both room to breathe seemed kind of necessary.

    Still, Wanda may have been the one who had to deal with it closer but Kate was, hands down, the one who was dealing with it worse.

    They had gone from talking all the time every fucking day to complete radio silence. The brunette can’t help but remember the first time she thought Yelena may have gone awol on her, back when they first went partying together, and she has to laugh at her dramatics. A couple of days had felt like an eternity then, but now she wishes that was all it took for things to go back to normal.

    Her friends have been the sole reason why she hasn’t lost her mind completely, M.J. and Peter reading between the lines of her rather abstract explanation and being heartily supportive without asking for details. Sure, M.J. had moaned and complained that she had missed everything by going to Los Angeles instead of visiting her family in New York during Thanksgiving, but that had been the extent of her pushing.

    They had spent almost every weekend together, either at Kate’s apartment or over at Peter’s place, and during the week they’d acted as a buffer whenever there threatened to be an uncomfortable silence between Yelena and her (or Wanda, for the matter).

    That had been collateral damage, too. Kate couldn’t help but feel that their little group had fallen a bit apart because of their mess. Peter and M.J. had sided with her mostly because she was the one closest to them, Wanda should have been with Yelena but we all know how that went, and Eddie and Bucky had wisely chosen to stay neutral. If they noticed something was off - and they had to because it was rather obvious - neither of them said anything. Instead, Eddie tried his best to bring them all together at least a couple of days a week, even managing to drag Yelena sometimes, and Kate was eternally grateful for him.

    So yeah, three weeks of awkward silences and sidelong glances and unsure smiles were as long as Kate’s strength of will had handled. It took one picture for everything to come crumbling down.

    It’s a Thursday around the early afternoon and the brunette had just come back from a long walk with Lucky. She’s putting away the few groceries she had bought along the way when her phone buzzes on the counter and she grabs it mindlessly, seeing the text notification from Peter and opening the message without a second thought.

    She knows it’s a mistake the second her brain processes the image in front of her. She’s sure her friend didn’t mean to send this to her because the writing underneath is definitely not the way he would break this to Kate. But he’s sent it and now she’s seen it, and there are a million things running across her mind.

    Wtf? The text under the picture reads, and it couldn’t capture Kate’s feelings better. In the shot, there’s a blonde girl with her hair parted in two braids, the green coat unmistakeably Yelena’s. There’s a man next to her, dark brown hair and a glimpse of a beard, and he has an arm around her shoulders. Kate can’t be absolutely sure, but she swears that’s Bucky.

    What the fuck indeed.

    Her first suspicion is confirmed when Peter begins to text her nonstop, apologizing and saying he totally didn’t mean to send that to her chat. He probably intended to send it to M.J., possibly feeling as confused as she was now and searching for an explanation or someone to share the gossip with, and he had messed up in the process.

    Her second suspicion is also confirmed when, after a bit of harsh pushing, he begrudgingly affirms that it was Bucky the one next to Yelena.

    Kate stops replying to his texts whining about how sorry he is and how angry M.J.’s going to be at him once she begins to process the information. She knew that out of all of them, aside from Eddie, Yelena had been the one who managed to get closer to Bucky. It must have had something to do with both of them being quite cautious around people, and also less prone to the somewhat childish behavior the rest of their friends liked to indulge in sometimes. Still, it was completely surprising to see them that close.

    She had never considered herself to be the jealous type. She’s had a few relationships before and she’s never worried about her partner’s friends or acquaintances, never felt insecure about them hanging out with other people or being physical with them. And yet, she’s pretty certain that the hot and cold feeling that’s coursing through her whole body right now is something really close to envy. A fit of jealousy she’s got absolutely no right to feel, she knows, but that she’s feeling anyway and she doesn’t know how to deal with.

    What she has always considered (and somewhat prided herself in) is being impulsive. The brunette liked to believe that her act now think later attitude has always brought her more joy than pain. That’s mainly the reason she has never actively tried to change it, and she’s definitely not starting today, either.

    Grabbing her purse and slamming the door behind her, she makes her way down to Yelena’s apartment. She doesn’t even know if she’ll be there, but Kate’s willing to wait. She’s had enough of this tiptoeing around each other, and she says as much to Wanda, shooting out a quick text to inform her of her current destination before turning her phone off completely.

    The look of surprise on the blonde’s face almost makes her feel vindicated, like for once she has the upper hand in this situation. Deep down she knows it shouldn’t feel like a competition to see who can push the other harder, but right now she’s confused and hurt and willing to be as petulant as she can.

    “What are you doing here?” are the words Yelena chooses as a greeting, peeking over her shoulder to see if there’s someone else with the younger girl. Perhaps she was worried about an ambush with her sister and Wanda. Kate just clicks her tongue and asks, “Can I come in?”

    There’s a shadow of doubt on the Russian’s face but it fades quickly into stoicness. She steps aside to let the younger girl in and closes the door behind her. It takes her a second to turn around, probably pondering what to say or trying to get her act together, but Kate’s not about to waste time.

    “We need to talk, Yelena,” she’s trying to keep her cool, really, she is. But there’s so much she has been holding in for the past three weeks - and even from before that - and piled with the weird feeling of envy that’s eating at her it’s definitely turning out to be a challenge.

    To her credit, Yelena only nods as she walks into the apartment, Kate following close behind. The older girl steps behind the counter that separates the kitchen from the living room, using it as a makeshift border between them, and yeah, Kate’s totally had enough.

    The Russian crosses her arms across her chest and purses her lips, looking straight into Kate’s eyes, “What do you want to talk about?”

    The whole innocent act is infuriating and the brunette has to keep count of her breaths to steady her nerves, “Really? You have no idea about what I might want to talk with you? Not a single clue?” she chuckles deprecatingly as she shakes her head.

    “Well, you’re the one who came storming through the door into my house so I guess it’s only fair I’d ask,” she shrugs noncommittally before she turns around and begins to dig around the shelves, “Do you want something to drink?”

    “No, Yelena. I don’t want anything to drink. I’d like to have a conversation with you, face to face if it were possible,” she adds almost pleadingly now. She loves this girl and she knows Yelena cares about her, too. That’s why it’s so frustrating to see the easiness the blonde has to brush her over as if it was nothing. As if they were nothing.

    The tone in her voice must crack something in the other girl because Kate sees her heave a deep breath before turning around, “Okay, let’s talk,” she concedes as she rests her hands on the kitchen counter, green eyes finally meeting blue.

    “Are you seeing Bucky?” is, out of all the things Kate had been meaning to say, probably the least appropriate and the most inopportune one. But it’s what slips past her lips because she has not been able to erase the image from her mind.

    The older girl looks flabbergasted, eyes wide open and mouth slightly parted in offense as she hugs herself tighter, “What?” is apparently the only thing she can reply, her face betraying for once everything she’s feeling. Kate just stares stubbornly at her because she’s not about to back down from this one, and she watches almost awed as Yelena composes herself, face returning to a blank slate, “Why would that be any of your business anyway, Kate?”

    There’s no regret or empathy in her voice, just plain numbness that makes the words nail themselves deeper into Kate’s chest. “You’re being mean right now, Yelena,” she answers honestly because she’s done with games. She’s done with the sneaking around and the carefulness and the keeping things to herself. She’s fucking done, “You know damn well why I would care about that.”

    Thankfully she’s not a crier when she’s fighting. At least not when she’s in the midst of the fight, anyway. That’s the only comfort she finds in this situation, that at least she’s not going to give Yelena the satisfaction of breaking down in her living room. But the blonde doesn’t seem to be softer in this, chin jutted out pridefully and eyes blazing with self-righteousness, and Kate knows that if she wants to at least get her answers she’s going to have to concede.

    Sighing, she rubs her fingers tiredly across her eyelids and takes a step forward to stand closer to the blonde. Placing her hands on the counter that separates them, she tries again, “Listen, I know I have absolutely no fucking right to demand an explanation because you and I are - We’re not… anything, okay? I know that. But I feel like even if that’s the case, even if you don’t owe it to me, it would be fair of you to tell me where the fuck we’re standing, Yel. Because I swear I have absolutely no fucking idea. And I’ve tried to give you time and space and I understand that things are not easy for you, but at least I think I deserve -“

    Kate trails off because she’s not even sure what to say anymore. This is such a huge mess she has a hard time wrapping her head around it. Who was she to demand an explanation about whatever it was that could be going on between Yelena and anyone, really? At best, she was a friend. At worst she was exactly that - nothing.

    Her inner turmoil must shine in her face because she sees the blonde deflate, shoulders slumping over her petite frame as she closes tightly her eyes. She’s debating something, Kate knows, recognizes the tilt in her head and the movement of her lips. When Yelena opens her eyes again, the younger girl knows whatever she says next is going to hurt, “It was a mistake, Kate. It shouldn’t have happened.”

    There’s about a minute of doubt in the air, the brunette wondering what exactly is Yelena talking about, but before she has a chance to ask the Russian continues, “In New York I was - I didn’t think things through. I should have been more careful, because I never meant to hurt you and now it’s glaringly obvious that I am. What happened between us, I…” she sighs defeatedly and forces her eyes away from Kate’s, “I think we should just stay as friends.”

    To say her mind was reeling would be a complete misunderstanding, but she had come here looking for answers, and that - whether it hurt her or not - was definitely an answer. Not the one she wished for, but not exactly unexpected, either.

    “I don’t want to lose you,” Yelena whispers, and that is the first thing that she’s said tonight that feels somewhat close to the Yelena Kate knows. It was a mirage, probably, but it still tears at her heart, the thought that beneath all that aloofness and firm resolution about them being a mistake, the other girl could still overcome her own inner fears to accept out loud that Kate meant something after all.

    All the overpowering decisiveness that had driven her here leaves the brunette’s body as soon as she realizes what’s happening: Yelena is very clearly closing the door and leaving her out. And Kate is so tired and hurt that she doesn’t have the stamina to fight back. She barely nods, numbly pushing her hands inside her pockets to hide the force in which her nails are digging onto her skin. She knows Yelena is waiting for an answer. She’s looking at her expectantly and, if Kate dares say, afraid, but there are just no words left in her.

    “Okay,” is the best she can give her, rasping out the word and clearing her throat before she tries to tilt her lips upwards. She’s pretty sure it must have come out as a grimace, but she lacks the energy to pretend any better.

    The conversation is absolutely over because Kate refuses to keep dragging herself down this spiral of hopelessness, so she grabs her purse and aims for the door. She hears footsteps padding quickly behind her, and she feels Yelena’s need to say something else, but before it can happen the younger girl catches a glimpse of a familiar picture as she passes the Russian’s bedroom in the hallway. She halts and Yelena stumbles into her before taking a couple steps back. She seems lost as to what has made Kate stop so abruptly, but she must catch on pretty quickly because her eyes follow Kate’s and a chagrin look washes over her face.

    Kate alternates between looking at her and glaring at the framed picture the two of them took in New York. They’re sitting in the grass at the park, the sunset lighting both their faces almost as much as their smiles. Kate is sitting between Yelena’s legs, the older girl’s arms wrapped tightly around her shoulders from behind as the brunette places a hand on top of Yelena’s and snaps the picture with the other. It’s the same picture Kate still has set as a screensaver on her phone, her absolute favorite picture of them.

    Right now she has no idea about how she should feel.

    “You’re a coward,” she spits resignedly as her eyes stay transfixed into the picture, “I just - I think you are an absolute coward.” Yelena keeps her eyes firmly downcast, a flush on her cheeks as her teeth sink on her bottom lip. If Kate didn’t know better she’d say she’s holding back tears, but that though, after everything that has just happened, seems illusory at best.

    There’s nothing more to say, neither of them has anything else to give, so Kate chances a final, sorrowful look at the girl she fucking loves and closes the door behind her quietly.

    By the time she gets home she has what could be described as a very pitiful breakdown. She’s so absolutely angry at everyone and everything because she knew that Yelena felt the same way, at least to some extent, but the thought is not comforting now. It doesn’t matter what the blonde feels or doesn’t feel because there will always be external forces that escape from their control and that will keep them away. If she’s not brave enough to try and fight them there’s not much else Kate can do.

    Her phone is blown away with texts and missed calls when she turns it on. There’s Peter begging her to call her back and apologizing, M.J. asking her to please not do anything stupid and to think things over, Wanda reminding her that pushing was definitely not the way to go, and even Natasha cautioning her to be careful. She barely has enough energy to text each of them back with the same reply before curling underneath her sheets and blocking the world away:

    it’s over.

    Things get quieter after that. Kate is equal parts too ashamed and too tired to reach out again, and Yelena unsurprisingly chooses to keep her distance. The only improvement there seems to be is that, at least, the undercurrent tension that followed them every time they shared space had diminished into almost nothing.

    Kate can’t say she’s glad because she most certainly isn’t, but at least she knows where they stand now. Before, there had been a lot of things unsaid and a constant fear of being on the brink of losing whatever little illusion they had built together. The good thing now was that it was perfectly clear everything had gone as bad as it could, so she didn’t have to keep worrying about salvaging scraps or keeping up her part.

    Of course, after the initial steam had cooled off she had felt like shit. She knew she had crossed a few lines during their last conversation, she knew it wasn’t fair to demand Yelena to overcome the shame and fears that had been ingrained into her mind since she was a baby instantly, just for her. She had been wrong and she was sorry for that, but while she recognized the Russian’s right to keep her pace and even pull away if she wished to, Kate also knew that she deserved at least an explanation. It wasn’t fair of Yelena to expect her to act as if nothing had happened, and she should have known that the brunette would have understood. They could have talked things over, she could have asked for time, but Yelena plainly refused and chose to act as if Kate was an inconvenience, and that wasn’t right, either.

    They both were aware of their mistakes, Kate was sure. That had to be the reason why - even if things definitely were not back to normal - they at least had managed not to act absolutely cold towards the other. She would catch Yelena looking at her at times, eyes sad, and even if the blonde pulled away instantly she understood. After all, Kate was pretty sure she glanced at her the same way, too.

    Plus, they shared a group of friends and they certainly didn’t deserve to have to choose sides or bear with their uncomfortable energy, so they had made an effort to act civil.

    The unknown situationship between Yelena and Bucky remained a mystery to Kate, but she had preferred it to stay like that. Peter and M.J. had offered to pull a bit at Eddie to see if they could get some gossip, but she really didn’t want to know. Whatever it was, it didn’t concern her, and she refused to keep hurting herself over this. If they were friends, great. If they were something else then - she’d rather not think about it.

    Yelena was free to be with whoever she chose to, but it didn’t mean that Kate had to be completely immune to it. That’s why the longer she could keep herself in the dark, the better.

    She should know by now that things don’t tend to remain quiet in her life for long, though.

    It had been a week and a half since hers and Yelena’s crash and burn, and now Christmas break was only a few days away. Eddie had insisted (read: begged) during a library group date that they should all go out together before leaving for the holidays, making a round of puppy eyes until each of them caved.

    Peter and M.J. had backed him up immediately, the two having gotten closer and disgustingly coupley even if they refused to acknowledge it; Wanda had happily agreed at the suggestion that she brought her brother with her; Bucky and Yelena had just nodded while they continued their reading, and now his eyes were onto Kate.

    She wanted a good night out with all of them before Christmas, and after chancing a peek at Yelena she figured that they could manage this same level of detachment in a club, “Only if Cass comes along,” she grinned at Eddie as he fist-pumped the air and let out a scandalous fuck yeah that earned him quite a few dirty looks.

    That’s how she finds herself in a kind of deja vu: M.J. splayed on her bed, clothes thrown haphazardly around the room, and an unnecessary amount of make-up sitting on her vanity.

    The only difference with their last escapade was that now Peter was there with them, too.

    “Dude, I wish I had boobs. That thing looks ridiculously hot on you,” M.J. compliments her when Kate emerges from the bathroom, a deep burgundy leatherly corsette hugging at her torso. It had been a Christmas gift from Franny and she had never found the right moment to wear it, so she figures that this time was as good as any.

    Kate chuckles when she sees the appreciative look her friend sends her, and laughs obnoxiously loud when she realizes that Peter was actually nodding along. The boy flushes before defending himself, “I mean, you both look very good. I told you before we came here,” he points a finger at M.J. as if daring her to contradict him.

    The curly-haired girl just snorts and pats him at the shoulder, “That you did.” It’s patronizing but also loving, and they share a rather sweet look that has Kate looking away and feeling as if she was intruding. She’s terribly happy for them, whether they’ve discussed feelings or not, because it’s not a matter of if, just a matter of when. There’s a pang of jealousy inside of her, but it’s the good kind.

    “I’m fucking glad we’ll be able to see each other during break. I’m actually looking forward to spending time in the city with both of you,” M.J. sighed as they began to put on their coats. Snow pilled at the corners of each street and the icy wind weaved itself through their hair, and Kate has half a mind to go up again and change into thicker pants. Her friend seems to read her mind and grabs firmly at her arm instead, “No, no, no. You’re going to show that hot body of yours in this leather outfit, Katie Kate. Let her see what she’s missing.”

    They all know who M.J’s talking about. Kate had poured her fucking soul out to them the day after the picture incident. She had skipped the most personal parts, and she trusted them to keep the rest to themselves. Peter had been the most diplomatic, gently reminding her that Yelena’s position wasn’t an easy one, while M.J. had taken over the role of the overprotective friend and - even if she never spoke a bad word about the Russian - chose to remind her every chance she got that Kate deserved to be with someone who could be proud of being with her.

    A silence descends upon them and again, it’s up to Peter’s more mediator ways to steer the conversation into something that won’t make Kate get too lost inside her head, “You’re sure we’ll all fit in your car? I mean, suitcases and all.” They had discussed their road trip home like twice already, but the brunette is thankful for the change of topic anyway. She links her arms through theirs and hugs her friends close to body.

    They had all agreed that this time they’d rather go somewhere quieter where they could drink and talk with each other, so instead of a club Eddie had dragged them to a rather small pub with a pretty cool pool and a game of darts that Kate couldn’t help but make googly eyes at.

    The rest of their group is already inside when they get there. M.J. having gotten sick of turning left and right and pulling out her phone to search on her maps the place, much to Peter’s I’m-great-at-orienting-myself dismay. She’s glad that they agreed to come over to her place first because she’d have to come by herself then, and now she can use them as a shield to stall the greeting part.

    Cassie is the first one to throw her arms around Kate even if they had seen each other a few days ago, and Eddie follows close behind placing a cold beer in her hand, “I’ve got your back little bird,” he says as he winks at her. She turns to hug Wanda and Pietro, who pulls her into a conversation right away and actually saves her from having to finish her round of hellos. Bucky and his friend (Sam, the nice man who bought her a shot once if she remembers correctly) sit at the far end of the booth, both of them lifting their hands in acknowledgment, and right next to them is Yelena. Their eyes meet and it feels like too much for a second, but then the younger girl offers a tentative smile that the blonde returns eagerly and she doesn’t feel as bad for skipping her.

    They order food and more drinks, and the warmer it gets the more comfortable Kate feels.

    She’s squished between the Maximoff siblings, Peter and M.J. sitting at their left and Cass and Eddie in front of them. The conversation flows easily between all of them, and even if Yelena and her don’t speak directly they do share a few complicit laughs.

    It’s unbelievable sad the way such an ordinary thing makes Kate’s chest feel as if it was about to explode.

    At some point during the night someone suggests a game of pool, most of them hurrying over to the table and dividing into teams. It ends up being Sam, Bucky and Yelena against M.J., Peter and Eddie, and the giddiness Kate was beginning to feel diminishes a bit when she sees Bucky smile down at Yelena as she whispers something close to his ear while pointing at the pool table.

    She forces herself to pull her eyes away and tries to catch up with the book debate Cass and Wanda are having now, but a nudge at her side stops her from pretending to nod along, “My sister says you are an archer, is that right?” Pietro asks as he returns to his seat beside her, eyebrow arched and a glint in his eyes, “You up to prove your aim at the darts?”

    Kate grins so hard her cheeks hurt and she nods her head frantically, “Oh man, I’ve been wanting to play since we came in and I saw the target hanging in the wall. I won’t go easy on you, though, so get ready to lose, ” she smirks, energy already thrumming through her body in anticipation. Pietro chuckles as he gets up from the booth, offering her a hand to pull her out of her seat, “Well then, lead the way Katniss.”

    The barman gives them a cup full of darts and they drag a high stool near them to use as a makeshift table, “Now, before we get started, what does the winner get?” Pietro grins while he counts the small arrows, making two even piles for them to play. The brunette snorts and looks at him faking arrogance, “You want to bet against me? That’s risky,” but her act melts away embarrassingly quick, eagerness taking over, “Okay, if I win you have to let me use that cool telescope Wanda told me you have at your apartment. I saw the pictures she took with it of the moon and they are awesome,” she gushes enthusiastically. She’s confident she’s going to win anyway, so the prospect of a reward gets her excited.

    “Fair,” the Sokovian concedes while rubbing a thumb across his chin contemplatively, “And if I win you have to let me use your bow sometime. I’ve never tried it but shooting actual arrows has to be really cool,” he says as he hands her one of the piles.

    Nodding, Kate positions herself to get ready to aim and sends the silver-haired boy a sympathetic look over her shoulder, “It really is. It’s a shame you’re about to lose, you’d definitely enjoy it,” and with a last playful smirk, she takes a dart between her fingers and throws it confidently towards the target. It hits the bullseye easily and she lets out a content yelp before turning around again, “We take three shots each and then make it the best out of five?” she asks her opponent who’s looking quite impressed with her first take. He agrees and motions at her to go on, “Blow me away, miss Everdeen.”

    Two other shots hit the target barely inches apart, and as she makes her way to retrieve them she does a little victory dance that doesn’t go unnoticed by Pietro, “Don’t get cocky on me, Kate. I still stand a chance.” His chuckle turns into a full-on laugh when Kate raises her hands in mock innocence and takes her beer from his fingers. She takes a thirsty gulp and hides her smile on the rim of the bottle, leaning her hip against the stool while she observes the older boy get ready.

    Pietro hits the bullseye nearly as centered as Kate did, and the brunette doesn’t hold back the woop of surprise that bubbles at her throat. He turns around and winks at her, “I told you I could hold my own, I’ve had my fair share of practice. Throwing darts is my go-to concentration method for my physics exams.”

    Kate lets out an amazed laugh and she claps her hands appreciatively while the Sokovian bows theatrically. He takes a drink before he prepares to throw again, and she tries to pay attention but she can’t shake the feeling of having eyes on her. She chances a look around and realizes for the first time that they have an audience. A couple of tables are now looking curiously at them, some even giving her a short applause as she smiles kindly at them.

    Then, she turns to their friends. She sees Cassie and Wanda still huddled together talking, and the others still engrossed with their own game. All of them except one, that is. There’s a pair of green eyes firmly fixed on them, Yelena gripping her pool stick tightly and her face settled into a deep frown. Their eyes meet and Kate has a hard time trying to decipher the emotion on the blonde’s gaze. She offers a soft smile at the Russian, one that the other girl seems to reciprocate when the corners of her lips begin to pull upwards before it falls altogether. Kate tilts her head confused at Yelena’s sudden scowl when she feels a light tap on her shoulder, “You’re up again,” Pietro gestures to the target, and by the time she turns to the blonde again the other girl is no longer looking at them.

    Determined not to let herself get thrown off by the older girl’s mood swings she pours all her attention into the game. They go through their rounds and Pietro admiringly does hold his own against Kate’s more experienced game. She’s leading by the time they reach the last round, but not by much. The crowd that has gathered around them is larger now as they wait for Pietro to do his final throws, their friends pilling up behind Kate too as they encourage them on.

    “What has to happen for you to win? I want you to win,” M.J. repeats tipsily from where she’s perched on Kate’s shoulder. The older brunette laughs as she points to the game again, “He has to fail at least one of his shots. If he doesn’t, then we’ll have to do a tiebreaker.” Her friend nods even though it’s pretty clear she still doesn’t get it and Wanda and Peter snort next to them.

    Kate bites her lip to keep from laughing when sees out of the corner of her eye Eddie and Cassie obnoxiously giving her a thumbs up from their table. She’s about to shake her head resignedly at her friends’ antics, but Yelena comes into view. She’s sitting on the other end of their booth, alone, and she’s smiling genuinely at Kate. It’s a tender smile that the younger girl swears is beaming with a glimpse of pride, and she has to swallow the pain away because it feels like an eternity since Yelena last looked at her like that.

    She’s so deep inside her head that she almost misses the loud cheer that breaks through the pub, her friends shaking her and clapping at her back in congratulations. It takes a minute for her to realize that Pietro had indeed missed the bullseye with his last shot and therefore she had won the game. The Sokovian bows to her reverently as he gracefully accepts defeat, “You win, miss Everdeen. Whenever you want to collect your reward, you let me know.”

    “I’ll admit I was holding my breath the whole time. You are a worthy opponent, Maximoff.

    Only for that I’ll bend the rules a bit and I’ll let you practice with my bow,” she whispers conspirationally at the older boy. He laughs again and pulls her into a one-sided hug, “That’s very magnanimous of you Kate. I appreciate it, truly,” this last part is completely sincere, and she’s actually glad for this night because she thinks they could get to be really good friends.

    When they return to their table there’s a full round of shots waiting for them, on the house, and while she drowns hers Kate realizes that Yelena is nowhere to be found. Bucky and Sam had left a while earlier, something about meeting with another friend at the other end of town, and the brunette thinks bitterly that perhaps she chose to continue the night with them.

    Unsurprisingly by now, Wanda reads her mind, “She went outside a few minutes ago, took her cigarettes with her,” she speaks lowly on her ear while pointing towards the door. Kate looks at the redhead uncertain and her friend gives her a small smile, “It’s your call, Kate. You don’t have to reach out to her if you don’t want to. It would be fair. Just… Don’t let it be a pride thing.”

    It only takes twelve breaths of restlessness in her seat for Kate to excuse herself quietly and make a beeline for the exit. The frosty wind hits her body instantly, taking away part of the warmth the alcohol had provided, so she hugs her arms to her body and squints her eyes as she searches for a mane of blonde hair.

    “Looking for someone?”

    Kate wonders, not for the first time, if there will ever be someone who’s able to turn her inside out just by uttering a couple of words. Yelena’s voice is husky and rough, a bit breathless as she puffs the smoke out of her lungs. She can admit, at least to herself, that she’s pretty sure no one has ever held the power the blonde has over her. But she’s putting herself first this time, so Kate gets her act together, “Yeah, a blonde girl, must be around your age. She’s short, has a cute accent. Don’t know if you’ve seen her.”

    Yelena gazes at her contemplatively and there’s a smile threatening to break on her face but she purses her lips and stubs her cigarette instead, “I thought you’d be looking for a silver- haired man. He’s got a cute accent too, so I hear,” she mutters as she crosses her arms tightly, eyes never leaving her own shoes. There’s jealousy thinly veiled in her voice, and Kate has to step closer to make sure she’s hearing her right.

    “You can’t be serious,” the younger girl chuckles incredulously at the other girl as she rubs her forehead. Yelena doesn’t seem to find the humor, though, because her eyes dart to find Kate’s the second she utters those words, “What, like you didn’t barge into my apartment demanding to know if there was something going on with me and Bucky,” she defends herself, “You were pretty chummy with Pietro back there, it doesn’t seem like such a farfetched idea.”

    Now she’s got two options. She can do the mature thing and end up this conversation before it degenerates into something ugly, or she can strike back. Kate would really love to say she’s choosing to be the bigger person, but Yelena has a way to get under her skin and she’s fucking tired of being dragged around.

    “It’s not the same. It’s not remotely close to being the same and you know it, Yelena,” she begins, taking a deep breath to calm her frazzling nerves, “I was being friendly back there, and there was no secrecy and no hiding around. I still haven’t got a fucking idea of what’s going on between you and Bucky - and I don’t give a damn,” she raises a hand to stop the blonde from interrupting her because she feels like she needs to get this out of her chest now. “I don’t want an explanation if you don’t think I deserve it. I’m trying to make peace with that, I’m trying my best to carry on with the knowledge of what happened - of what could happen - between us and salvage whatever is left of our friendship because, believe it or not, I fucking care about you,” she chokes back a sob and steps back when she sees Yelena attempting to reach out to touch her.

    The Russian has a pained look on her face now, eyebrows pulled together and bottom lip trembling, but Kate’s on a run, “I can try and do that for you if that’s what you need. I can try to pretend not to want you, and I guess it’s fair to an extent. I can’t expect you to reciprocate and want the same when you clearly don’t. And I’m willing to do that because I love you, but you don’t get to be jealous, then. You don’t get to make me feel bad for talking with other people, or flirting with them, or kissing them. You don’t get to do that because I’m not yours, and it’s not fucking fair of you to expect me to - What? Be faithful to you when you’ve made it perfectly clear there’s nothing here?” she’s crying now, can feel the hotness of her tears streaming down her cheeks, the trail getting cold with the blowing wind. They’re both breathing heavily, and when she wipes away her tears and her vision clears she realizes that Yelena’s eyes are glistening, too.

    Kate lets herself fall back against the wall Yelena was leaning into, head dropped back as she stares at the moon before closing her eyes, “So, no. I came here looking for a beautiful girl who drives me fucking crazy, but I think maybe I should stop doing that. Maybe it’s for the best. For both of us.”

    She turns her head to look at the blonde, eyes wide and pleading, begging her to say something - anything - to soothe the ache on her chest. Something to cling to, something Kate can take as an excuse to keep trying.

    To her credit, Yelena does look like she’s about to speak. It just so happens that a bunch of fairly drunk men chose that exact moment to leave the pub shouting like maniacs, and of course, the blonde darts her eyes and pulls away the second they are not alone anymore. Kate just nods defeatedly rubbing tiredly at her cheeks again before turning around and catching the door before it closes, slipping silently inside and leaving Yelena behind.

    Their group is also getting ready to leave, Wanda and M.J. already carrying their things with them as they make their way to the exit. Kate takes her purse from M.J.’s fingers and musters a smile for her friends’ shake, even though she’s pretty sure both girls are acutely aware of the path their conversation must have taken. Still, neither of them pushes it, and when Kate mutters that she’s heading home and waves a hand around mindlessly, M.J. and Peter stick by her side and insist on walking her to her apartment while Wanda kisses tenderly her forehead as a goodbye.

    She welcomes the freezing air now, feels it alleviating the pressure in her head and the throb behind her eyes, but sadly it doesn’t manage to wipe away the words pounding over and over in her mind.

    Now it really feels like it’s over.

    She’s fucking magnificent.

    They spend longer than necessary just looking at each other, soft smiles on their lips as they breathe in unison. And then there’s commotion outside Yelena’s bedroom followed by a heavy knock on the door and the bubble pops. A man’s voice drifts through Kate’s speakers but she doesn’t understand what he says. She doesn’t need to think too hard, though, to figure that’s Yelena’s father and that he’s demanding her attention.     They both get up at the same time and walk to their respective doors, one hand on the handle while the other hovers above the hang-up button, though neither makes the move. Kate watches as Yelena leans her temple against her door and smiles earnestly at her, “There’s a saying,” she begins tentatively, “There’s a saying that goes something like… The way you begin the New Year is the way it will end.     They started the year together, Kate thinks then. Even miles apart Yelena had managed to sneak into her new beginning and the brunette doesn’t have it in her to pretend not to love it.

    “You know what, I think I like that one, too.”
