She parks her car askew and jumps out the door immediately, eyes darting around the handful of bodies that surround the place. It’s a pub the brunette’s pretty sure they’ve been to before, and the music still pumps through the walls even at the late hour.

    There are a few, scattered small groups of friends taking a smoke outside, another handful of people waiting around probably waiting to be picked up, and no fucking sight of Yelena. Or Bucky, for the matter.

    Her finger hovers hesitantly over the list of contacts on her phone, unsure if she should call her friend or Bucky. She’s saved from thinking any longer when her name rings out across the street. She doesn’t recognize the voice but Kate looks up anyway, seeing a dark-skinned man wave at her from the corner of the building. Sam.

    Kate trots over to where he’s standing and the question burning at her lips dies instantly when she finally catches sight of blonde hair.

    The older girl is sitting down on a small stretch of stairs that lead to the backdoor of the pub, a bicker jacket wrapped tightly around her body as her forehead rests against the cool metal of the railing. Her eyes are closed but Kate can still see the tear streaks that run down her cheeks, drawing rivulets on her make-up.

    If she wasn’t anxious before, Kate is now absolutely worried sick now.

    “What the hell happened?” she asks the older man as she contemplates whether or not it’s smart for her to disturb Yelena by getting closer. Green eyes open instantly at the sound of her voice, though, red and wide and slightly unfocused but clearly distressed, and Kate makes her decision right away.

    Sitting on the small spot next to her, she drapes an arm around Yelena’s shoulders and the Russian collapses against her body at once. She’s mumbling something lowly on the skin of Kate’s neck, but either she’s speaking in Russian without meaning to or she’s far too gone to even know what she’s saying. Her eyes close again and Kate’s stomach sinks as she takes her in.

    “She had one too many tonight,” Sam begins when her eyes meet his, arms crossed on his chest. He’s simultaneously keeping his distance and hovering over them, “She looks like she can hold her alcohol, though, so either she wasn’t on her best game tonight or something hit her way too hard.”

    The cogs on her brain begin to speed up. Tightening her arms around the older girl, Kate bristles at his words, “Are you suggesting that - what? someone spiked her drink?” Her worry is shifting briskly into anger, something sharp and ugly twisting inside her, and even though Sam is absolutely not at fault here, he’s the only one on the receiving end.

    He seems to follow her reasoning and widens his eyes. Trying to smile soothingly, he raises his arms to placate her, “No, no, Kate. I assure you that didn’t happen. She was with us the whole time.” His words barely ease the brunette’s uneasiness, heart stuttering away on her chest, “Admittedly, we should have stopped her sooner.”

    “Yeah, no shit. She hardly ever drinks, Sam,” Kate practically growls, bringing her hands to rub across the Russian’s shoulder blades, “And anyway, that doesn’t explain why the hell she looks like she’s been crying. And where the hell is Bucky? Why is he not here?” It feels weird to talk about Yelena as if she wasn’t right there, but the truth is that the blonde looks barely conscious of their conversation and Kate desperately needs to understand what has put the older girl in this state.

    The hardness on Sam’s voice brings her attention back to the older man, “Bucky lost sight of her like an hour ago, more or less. He came outside looking for her, figuring she was out smoking, and saw her trying to push some disgusting bastard off her. Yelena held her own even wasted, though, but got absolutely terrified after. And I mean like, completely freaked out. She wouldn’t let either of us get too close to her.”

    Kate feels the tension on her jaw increase as she grits her teeth together, her left hand curling into a fist around Yelena’s jacket as her right arm pulls her even closer, the blonde whimpering softly on her collarbones. She’s having a very difficult time trying to fight the urge to get up and do something.

    “She just kept repeating that she wanted you,” Sam nods his chin at them, as if their predicament was self-explanatory, “Threatened us with bloody murder if we told Natasha, too. So, that’s when Bucky called. But shortly before you came here he thought he caught sight of the asshole and went after him. Don’t know if he’s found him yet, or if it was the same person at all, but if he is, well.”

    The sentiment on his words is one Kate is all too willing to get behind.

    “That’s why he’s not here, and why you are,” He finishes with a flutter of his hand around the air. Reaching for his phone and scrolling with his thumb through the screen, he heaves a sigh, “I’m going to try and call him again.”

    Yelena shivers in her arms and Kate hurries to take off her own coat and drape it around her body. She looks terribly small right now, vulnerable in an awful way. It’s an image she had never thought she’d associate with Yelena. “Should we take her to the hospital or something?” she bites her lip to keep from doing something feeble like crying.

    Clicking his tongue, Sam hangs up the phone and pocket it inside his jacket again before turning back to both girls, “I don’t think that’s necessary. I mean, she will most likely throw her guts up and have a killer hangover tomorrow, but other than ibuprofen there’s not much a doctor will give her. Other than a lecture.” His attempt at lightening the mood is immediately dampened by the unintelligent muttering of the Russian and the deeply woeful look on Kate’s blue eyes, “We should get her to the car and you should take her home. Let her rest, see if you can put something in her stomach even if it will end up out anyway. Give her lots of water.”

    Somewhere in her fogged mind, it must finally register that they’re talking about her because green eyes flutter open and her curled-up body tenses when Sam steps closer to them. Kate feels the subtle change in Yelena’s posture and understands the meaning, “Yeah, okay. Let’s do that. You pick up her stuff, I’ve got her,” the younger girl instructs him as she stands up. The Russian moans at the loss of support and reaches out her arms to Kate immediately, making it mercifully easy to lift her up.

    “My car is over there,” Kate points a finger loosely in the right direction and flings her keys to Sam, silently willing him to go ahead. Then she throws Yelena’s arm around her shoulders as her own hand goes to dip between the blonde’s waist and hip, hoisting her body against hers. “Now you’re going to have to work with me, babe,” she mutters mostly to herself, half- convinced the older girl isn’t fully comprehending anything that’s happening.

    That’s why she’s fairly surprised to hear Yelena’s voice babble something that almost sounds like English, “What was that?” she coaxes, feeling like if she keeps her talking the Russian will get back to her senses quicker. Kate begins to walk them to her car, careful eyes watching their step as her ear stays dutifully trained to the sound of Yelena’s voice.

    Something resembling I like that falls from the older girl’s lips as Kate adjusts her grip on her waist to stabilize them. “You like that?” the brunette parrots back, earning a nod in return, “What it is that you like, babe?”

    They are almost done crossing - uncivilly - the road when Yelena hums contently and nods, “Babe.” A treacherous blush creeps on the younger girl’s face as she bits the inside of her cheek. Her breath hitches and she doesn’t know how to respond to that, so Kate reaches for the limp hand dangling from her shoulder and curls her fingers around it.

    Her car is up and running when they reach it, Sam holding the passenger door open for Yelena while Kate fights with the seatbelt to buckle her up, “Text me when you get home, will you? Lena has my number.”

    Kate heaves a sigh and tempers her nerves, “Thank you, Sam. For everything. Tell Bucky I said so, too. I’ll let you know how she’s holding up.” She sounds as apologetic as she can muster her voice to, already regretting the way she had barked at the older man before.

    A comforting hand falls on her shoulder and squeezes hard, but Kate welcomes the feeling, “Nothing to be thankful for, Kate. Take care of her.” His kind, attentive eyes dart across her face as the brunette nods softly.

    They make the drive back home with the windows rolled down, Kate hoping the chilling breeze helps Yelena ginger up while simultaneously welcoming the bite of the cold on her burning head. She can feel a headache coming, but pushes the thought to the back of her mind with the rest of her unhelpful thoughts.

    Remembering Sam’s words, Kate stops the car when she sees opened the Chinese restaurant that’s a few blocks away from her apartment. She dials the number illuminated on their sign and exactly six minutes later a lovely Chinese grandpa comes out of the door holding a bag with two lidded bowls full of soup.

    By the time they are trailing their way up the stairs Kate is balancing their food, their coats, and most of Yelena’s weight but still, the older girl’s steps feel somewhat steadier.

    The blonde’s mumbling has been going on and off the whole ride, alternating between Russian and English and what Kate’s sure is just nonsensical whining, and the younger girl is torn between wanting to prod and feeling like she’s intruding on Yelena’s mind.

    Lucky greets them the second Kate opens the door, dancing around them excitedly as his tail thumps from side to side at the sight of his apparently favorite person - not Kate - while they stumble to the sofa. The brunette places carefully the containers on the small table next to the carpet and lowers Yelena gently on the cushions.

    Strong fingers curl desperately around Kate’s elbow when she attempts to stand up to go put the food away in the kitchen. Yelena’s eyes are wide and still moist around the edges and the younger girl feels a tug at her stomach, “I’ll be a second, I swear. I’ll bring you something to drink now and we can have this in a bit, okay?” she explains gently, lifting the bag on her hand as to reinforce her point.

    Yelena looks like she’s ready to stand up and follow her but her body probably doesn’t cooperate, so Kate scurries away and makes a quick job of flittering through her kitchen drawers for some pills while she fills a glass with water. She hands the cup to the older girl and sets to take off her own boots before repeating the process with the Russian’s shoes.

    “How did it go?”

    Startled, Kate looks up from where she’s sitting on the carpet to see Yelena’s eyes firmly set on the drink in her hands, head bent forward and shoulders slightly tense. Her words are a bit jumbled and her accent sits heavy on her tongue, but it’s a testament to her witts coming back to her. Still, Kate wishes she would have chosen a different thing to begin the conversation, so she settles for fake ignorance, “How did what go?”

    A bare foot nudges at Kate’s thigh, “Your date,” Yelena shrugs simply, pulling up her legs once the brunette finishes untying her other shoe and hugging her arms around her knees. Her eyes have regained some of their natural sharpness but her movements are lagged. Sighing, Kate tries to veer the topic, “Why don’t you tell me how your night went, Yel? You fucking scared me back there,” she forces down the berating tone on her voice, making an effort to keep the worry-induced anger at bay and only letting her concern coat her words.

    That seems to be the absolute worst thing to say. Yelena tenses as she draws a sharp breath and folds into herself, her small frame almost disappearing on the couch. “I was scared too,” she whispers, voice shaken and a clouded look on her face, “I thought - I don’t know what would have happened if… He was all over me before I could even realize - hands wandering.

    His breath reeked of alcohol,” shuddering, she lifts her wrist to wipe away a stray tear before she continues her slurred, choppy speech, “It was like being back - I hate people touching me.”

    It comes out almost candidly, as if it was the natural conclusion to something she had faced countless times before. Kate’s stomach churns as bile rises on her throat because she understands what Yelena is talking about. She gets what the blonde means by being back even if a huge part of the younger girl’s mind wishes for her to be completely wrong about this. She doesn’t get to ask, though, because as it would appear she’s not the only one fighting nausea.

    Yelena shoots to her feet in an instant as she bolts unsteadily to the bathroom. It’s admirable that she manages to make it to the toilet and even lift the lid before throwing up the contents of her stomach down the porcelain bowl. Kate hurries behind, kneeling next to her and pilling her tousled waves into a high ponytail before she begins to rub at the older girl’s back comfortingly.

    Wincing sympathetically, she reaches to wipe the tears gathered at the corner of Yelena’s eyes with the back of her fingers tenderly when the other girl stops retching. She lowers the lid and flushes the toilet while the Russian shifts her position and rests her forehead against the cool material of the bath and closes her eyes.

    After grabbing a towel and dampening the tip on the sink the brunette lowers next to her to dab the wet cloth across Yelena’s skin, cleaning her make-up and swabbing at her mouth. The other girl makes a content sound at the back of her throat as she opens her eyes and catches Kate’s blue ones with them. Then, her face grows dejected, “I hate it.”

    Kate smiles commiseratingly, “Yeah, throwing up is always the less fun part of drinking your bodyweight on alcohol.”

    “No,” Yelena shakes her head purposefully, gently lowering the younger girl’s hands from her face as she rubs at her own forehead, “No. That’s not it.” She clicks her tongue and swallows soundly, “I hate - this. Having to see you with somebody else. I can’t stand it. It’s just - I know I’m not supposed to bring it up but I fucking hate it. When I saw you with him… I didn’t know what to do with myself. There was a burning inside of me that I couldn’t control, Kate. It was so hard to open up the door again after I saw you.” Her jaw quivers and the frown on her forehead looks pained, “I thought I could forget about it tonight, if I drank enough. But it got even worse because you were the only damn thing on my mind.” She stumbles on some words but the wretched tilt on her voice makes the message feel burdening.

    Kate’s hands want to reach out, to link her fingers with hers or to hold out her arms or to kiss her cheek but she wills herself to stay still.

    The Russian is adamantly avoiding the younger girl’s stare as she valiantly tries and fails to calm her breath, “I’m so sorry, Kate,” she utters her name in a heartwrenching sob that unchains free her tears again. All her resolve evaporates as she takes Yelena into her arms without a second thought. The older girl clings desperately at her shoulders as she hides her face on her neck and Kate slips her hand underneath her shirt to dig her fingers on the soft skin of her back.

    “Shh, it’s okay, baby. It’s okay,” Kate mutters comfortingly on blonde hair, closing her eyes to keep her own tears away.

    The truth is there’s nothing okay in any of this. They’ve both screwed up royally because while it would be easy to place all the blame on Yelena’s shoulders, she had to admit that she hadn’t been the most reasonable, either. She knew there was nothing to be guilty about spending time with whoever she wanted, but she had also known that there weren’t any romantical implications on her relationship with Pietro (at least on her part), so, why had she deliberately chosen to hide the fact that it was him who she would be hanging out with?

    Sure, she could say that she had intended to avoid poking at the blonde’s weird feelings about her and the Sokovian, but then again, wasn’t that kind of doing exactly the same thing she had recriminated Yelena about Bucky?

    Her mind is reeling and her legs are beginning to go numb but it’s Yelena’s shivering what finally gives her the strength to move. The older girl sniffles as her eyes follow Kate’s movements, hands reaching out immediately. The brunette helps her to her feet and she sways a bit, probably lightheaded from her episode in the bathroom, so Kate grabs her by the shoulders and walks her to her bed.

    No words are exchanged while Kate grabs a sweatshirt from her closet and begins to tug at Yelena’s shirt to help her off of it. The Russian pulls the clothing over her underwear and slips out of her pants, too, scorting over the pillows and moving her legs to pull back the comforter. The younger girl was already on her sleepwear before, so she takes the opportunity to rush to the kitchen to reheat the soup after pouring it into a bowl.

    Yelena’s face scrunches into a disgusted grimace but Kate’s face leaves no room for discussion, so she begins to take small spoonfuls of the concoction to her mouth. Slowly, color begins to return to her cheeks and the younger girl watches enthralled the way her pink lips curl around the cutlery and her throat wobbles.

    “He’s just a friend,” comes Yelena’s mumbled whisper, her voice steady for the first time tonight, “I’ve never - There’s nothing between me and Bucky. Never has been. He’s just a good friend, he gets me. But he’s only a friend.”

    Kate opens her eyes in surprise - both at her words and at the reality behind them, because she had been so sure - and Yelena must catch the meaning hidden in her expression, “I should have told you that day. But I was… I don’t know. I was scared and I wanted to push you away, so I thought that would make it easier. And then I was angry, too, because you - you barreled in and made those kind of assumptions and you believed them and it hurt me. But it was my own doing, really.”

    There’s an assumed defeat on the way her shoulders slump and her eyes stay down, so Kate reaches for her hand and links Yelena’s fingers with hers, “I was on the wrong, too. I should have faced it differently. But we were at odds then, and I was feeling you begin to drift away and I got scared. It’s not an excuse though, we’re both to blame on this.”

    There’s more to say, Kate knows. But she takes on the dark rings under Yelena’s eyes, on the slouched posture against the headboard, on the way she clutches at her stomach upset.

    There’s more to say, but it can wait until tomorrow.

    Green eyes study her apprehensively as a delicate thumb sweeps across Kate’s knuckles.

    Yelena seems to be gathering her will to speak, and the younger girl makes it to fourteen breaths before her husky voice rings out again, “Do you hate me now?”

    It’s vulnerable and afraid and regretful and Kate hates it. She hates to think that Yelena would even contemplate that option. She hates to think about how long that thought must have been eating away at the older girl. She hates to think that she’s even slightly to blame for that idea appearing on her mind.

    “No, baby, no. I could never hate you, I swear,” she whispers fervently as she crawls the distance between their bodies to launch herself at the blonde, Yelena’s arms already open and waiting. She nudges her nose on the crook of the older girl’s neck and inhales deeply, her perfume and her natural scent mixing in her nostrils.

    Yelena’s nails dig into her waist, her throat trembling as she tries to hold back a whimper, “Will you stay here with me tonight?”

    It’s probably not the best idea, perhaps.

    “Always.”

Kate had counted each draw of air dutifully, the older girl’s forehead resting snuggly against her temple. The little exhales keep hitting the brunette’s skin warmly even now, hours later after a few fitful hours of rest. Kate didn’t know when she herself had finally succumbed to her droopy eyelids, but what she was certainly amazed of was that they hadn’t moved an inch during the night.

    Calm and steady, Yelena’s breath kept grazing at her chin, all the remnant tension from last night having left the Russian’s body as she fully settles on Kate’s arms. The fingers on the younger girl’s waist have loosened the tight hold on her shirt, her fingertips instead tickling softly where the piece of clothing had ridden up and Kate’s skin was showing.

    She doesn’t want to reach out for her phone in fears of disturbing the slumbering girl, fully aware of the extent of her exhaustion and also, cowardly, reluctant to lose the close embrace they are in. Kate knows that once they leave the bed they will have to face the reality of everything that’s gone wrongly unsaid until now. It promises to be a heavily taxing conversation, so sue her if she selfishly wants to cling to this interlude of peace for a bit longer.

    Out of all the times they’ve shared a bed, this is the first one Kate hasn’t woken up alone. Maybe it shouldn’t, but the fact ignites a small spark of hope inside her chest. She really wants to believe that things may actually get to be different this time.

    The thoughts running free inside her head are mostly going in circles around the girl in her arms, but somewhere in the back of her mind, a voice picks up. Suddenly she’s reminded of Sam’s words, asking Kate to text him once they made it home. She had miraculously remembered to do that, but now the brunette wonders if perhaps she should have texted Wanda and Natasha too. Sure, maybe both redheads were staying over at Natasha’s place and they didn’t know Yelena hadn’t returned to her apartment yet, but it was probably a matter of time before either one noticed the blonde missing.

    Worrying her bottom lip between her teeth, Kate slips her right arm from where it’s trapped under Yelena’s left one carefully. She tries her best to keep her body still as she bends her arm around, celebrating internally when her fingers touch her phone instantly. Craning her head she sees that it’s still pretty early for a Sunday, and she lets out a relieved sigh when she notices the lack of frantic texts from any Slavic women.

    Yelena moves then, stretching her limbs before she curls around the younger girl again, and Kate knows she’s on her way to consciousness when her eyes begin to flutter under her eyelids and her nose twitches against her throat. She leaves her phone forgotten behind her and lifts a hand to push away the blonde lock of hair that’s about to get stuck on slightly parted lips.

    That seems to be the last push the Russian needs to blink her eyes open, emerald orbs taking Kate in awe even as her gaze remains slightly dazed. The brunette tilts her lips upwards, her smile growing when Yelena timidly but surely mirrors her, “How are you feeling, Yel?”

    The tentative smile surprisingly remains even when the blonde wrinkles her nose in disgust, “Like I got run over by a truck and something is rotting away inside of me.” Her voice is raspy and there’s clearly a bit of shame and a lot of regret about her last night’s choices, but Yelena manages to sound clear even in the midst of what surely is a pretty mean hangover.

    Once she’s made sure that the girl doesn’t feel like crying from a killer headache Kate lets out an amused snort, “Yeah, that’s about it. I’m surprised you aren’t feeling dizzy right now. When I get wasted I need at least five tries before I can open my eyes without feeling like the room is spinning.” She deliberately keeps any lecture out of her speech for now, aware that even if she’s trying not to show it, Yelena still feels uneasy - both physically and mentally. Instead, Kate chooses a different route, “What do you say I make some breakfast while you settle down a bit?” She also needs some time to think exactly how she wants to tackle this conversation, so a few minutes to prepare would be a godsend.

    A flash of reluctance shines across Yelena’s face at the thought of pulling away, so Kate squeezes the hand resting at her back reassuringly. The older girl softens at that, smiling sheepishly when her stomach grumbles, “Can I take a shower?”

    Nodding swiftly as she flattens her hands across the Russian’s back, Kate lets her fingertips dance in circles for a few seconds to gather the strength to pull away, “Sure thing. I’ll fetch you something clean to wear.”

    They are both aware that those words were their cue to let go, but neither makes the first move. Kate’s fingers keep dancing on their own accord along the expanse of the blonde’s skin, and Yelena pushes their foreheads together briefly as she closes her eyes. Their chests rise and fall in synch and the younger girl forces herself to stay present at the moment, to enjoy it for what it is. For what may not be again after today.

    It’s Yelena the one who ends up breaking their embrace, her need to rush to the bathroom stronger than her will to stay in bed, and Kate rummages through her wardrobe for a pair of sweatpants and a comfy t-shirt to leave on top of her comforter before heading downstairs. She heads straight for the coffee pot and gets Lucky’s food ready with the noise of the shower filling the air.

    After a short debate with herself, Kate ends up writing a simple message to Wanda, informing her of Yelena’s whereabouts before either she or Natasha try to contact the blonde. Yelena’s phone had died last night, briefly after they made it home and just in time for Kate to text Sam, so she figures it’s better to be safe than sorry.

    hey wanwan, yel’s here at my apartment. stayed the night. i’ll tell you later, just giving you a heads up.

    The reply comes almost instantly and makes the brunette smile and feel confident in her decision.

    Thanks, Kate. Nat was about to call her in a few, she was supposed to come by today for lunch. Let us know if you need us.

    Her eyes stay unfocused somewhere above her living room window as she begins to pick around her brain for the words she needs to say. Kate knows this is probably the best opportunity she’s going to get to lay down everything she’s been meaning to tell Yelena, but she’s also aware that if she pushes too hard it may just result in the Russian leaving again.

    She’s almost about to go grab a pen and some paper to write down her thoughts when the sound of water falling stops and Kate hears soft steps wander around her room. Instead, she busies herself with making some toasts and tea for the other girl, very intentionally keeping her back to the door to have some extra seconds to school her face into a not-lovesick expression. She knows she will need it once she sees the blonde in her clothes.

    “This will be good for your stomach,” Kate says, turning around when she feels eyes on her. Yelena sits down on the table, hair damp and face fresh and completely engulfed in Kate’s way too big for her t-shirt. She looks alluring even when nursing a hangover and it’s just so not fucking fair. The older girl smiles genuinely at her and that gets Kate to move, settling the food and their drinks down as she sits on the chair opposite to her.

    There had been an instant, a fleeting impulse to sit on the empty spot next to Yelena, her intrinsic need to be as close to her as possible shining through, but Kate resists. She knows that a bit of distance will help her think clearly. At least, she hopes so. She’ll need to.

    Yelena offers her a sweet, soft thanks as she munches carefully at the bread, still wary of the way her stomach might react after yesterday’s fallout. The brunette sips at her coffee and hides a smile at the endearing sight in front of her.

    The peaceful silence lasts for as long as it takes Yelena to finish her food, the older girl relieving in the clinking of her spoon against her tea mug for a breath before she straightens her shoulders and speaks, “I’m sorry, Kate.” Her eyes stay down only for a second longer before Yelena forces her gaze to Kate’s, and there’s a myriad of emotions packed in her eyes that makes the brunette inhale sharply through her nose.

    It’s a start, so Kate nods acknowledging, “You scared me yesterday,” she admits carefully, her voice soft and just a touch of worried to make sure the Russian understands that she’s not mad but needs her to know nonetheless. Kate has had enough of keeping things to herself.

    “Yeah I - I’m sorry about that, too.” Grimacing, Yelena darts her eyes to the kitchen, a regretful look taking over her face, “It definitely wasn’t my smartest move, for sure. You know I barely ever drink, so I guess… it hit me all at once. I should have thought about it better.” She’s ashamed, Kate realizes, so the younger girl hurries to clarify, “We’ve all had one too many, Yel. That’s not what I was talking about. I meant after. The reason you told me why you did it for.”

    Realization dawns on Yelena’s face as her eyes widen slightly, “Oh,” she mutters, body stiff as the memories from last night flood her, “I don’t remember exactly what I said, but… I don’t think there’s a point in denying it now.”

    Kate knows this is making the other girl terribly uncomfortable, she can see it in the hard lines around her mouth as she tightens her jaw and in the deep frown that means her head is probably spinning out of control. But she also knows they both need this, so, “No, I think it would be good if we stopped denying things already,” and then, as a peace offering, “I can go first, if you want me to.”

    The other girl tilts her head confused, and Kate smiles gently at that, “You’re not the only one who has things to apologize for here, Yel,” the brunette begins gravely, lips twitching downwards, “The way I reacted when - you know, Bucky,” she stumbles, shame clogging her throat, “I was way out of line. I shouldn’t have cornered you like that, much less demand an explanation for something that didn’t need one. I guess it was my insecurities talking, but I’m not like that - I don’t want to be like that, so I hope you can forgive me.”

    She speaks wistfully, measuring her breath as she waits for the older girl to speak, fingers wriggling against each other in anxiousness.

    Her words seem to have surprised Yelena. She probably believed herself to be the sole responsible for their situation, and that only serves to steer Kate’s resolve to make this right. “I don’t need you to say you’re sorry, Kate. If I had given you the truth to begin with, there wouldn’t have been any reason for you to react like that,” the blonde offers simply, and so the other girl hurries to shake her head, “No, Yelena. I mean - yes, it would have saved us some trouble, but that’s not the point. The point is I shouldn’t have been making assumptions and ambushing you like that. And, I kind of did the same thing to you, after. With Pietro. I should have just told you there wasn’t anything between us, but I didn’t.”

    That gets the blonde to lift her head sharply, mouth agape at Kate’s confession, “I didn’t want to fuel your resentment towards him, but… I think there was a part of me that wanted you to think there could be something there, to see if you cared. And that was a very awful thing to do. Hypocritical, too.” It’s painful to make that admission out loud, but Yelena deserves to know even the ugly bits.

    “He does like you, though,” Yelena points out, and Kate has to keep herself from rolling her eyes fondly because of course she would tackle that issue first.

He was cool with us staying as friends, because those are the only feelings I have towards him.” She makes a conscious effort to hold the Russian’s stare, letting her read the emotions brimming in her blue eyes.

    It seems to be enough to placate any remaining doubt the Russian may harbor about the topic, Yelena nodding her head as she sighs, “I believe you. But Kate, even if there was something else between you two, it wouldn’t be my place to say anything about it.” There’s so much reluctance in her words that the brunette has a hard time realizing this is Yelena accepting her role as only a friend.

What happened?” She really wants to know, needs to see if the other girl has any explanation, any clue that may explain why they went from kissing in her bed to begrudgingly acknowledging that there’s nothing more than a frail friendship between them.

    Yelena winces and chuckles self-deprecatingly, “I think that’s on me, too,” she ruefully admits, running a hand through her hair and drumming her fingers on the table nervously.

    “Do you remember when my mama called? The day we were supposed to come back from New York,” the Russian pauses to make sure Kate is following, a curt nod encouraging her on, “She called me while we were still in bed, twice. I went to the other room not to disturb you because I figured she wanted to guilt-trip me into visiting soon, you know,” she shakes her head contemptuously, a bitter smile on her face as she reminiscences the moment.

But then she started telling me how much she missed me, how glad she was to at least have one daughter left who loved her. She told me how depressed papa was about the whole thing, about how much he misses Nat and how much it would cheer him to see me and I -“ a whimper breaks through her lips halting her speech, and Kate abandons all thoughts about keeping her distance.     “What about your feelings, what about you want?” the younger girl elaborates, tightening her hold on the blonde’s hand, “If you put everyone’s wellbeing before your own, who’s going to make sure you’re happy?” Kate knows she’s speaking from a privileged position, she knows she’s never had to worry about anyone’s needs before because it had never been an issue. It’s not something new, but it feels heavier hearing it from Yelena herself.

And it shouldn’t matter, no one else should matter. But they do because you love them and because they love you and you don’t want to lose that, so you get scared and hide. You hide parts of yourself, little bits and pieces, even without realizing it.”

    Kate keeps quiet, the lump in her throat and the sting behind her eyes almost imperceptible in comparison to the deathly hold Yelena has on her fingers. She clings to it equally desperate, trying to convey her feelings through her touch. I’m here, I’m listening, I see you.

    I love you.

    It seems that either Yelena is too deep in her thoughts to register that she’s still talking, or that she’s finally opened this can of worms and she can’t stop the words from flowing now. “I didn’t share heartful confessions with my friends because mine wouldn’t be the kind of cute gossip people want to know. I kept to myself, and I stopped reaching out in the end. And people didn’t try, either, so I became this - this shell, unable to make connections. But it was safe. It was for the best,” she repeats muttering to herself. Who she’s trying to convince, Kate’s not sure. It’s probably for both of them.

    Accepted what I gave her and never prodded for more. She was… my first person.”

Sure, she knows both Natasha and Wanda kind of had this more or less established based on their own relationships with the blonde, but she’s pretty sure Yelena has never voiced these words out loud.

    Perhaps it shouldn’t, but it makes her feel extremely privileged.

This feels better, Kate thinks as she drapes her free arm around Yelena’s shoulders, if we’re doing this, we might as well be both comfortable.

    “Can I ask you something?” she begins delicately, making sure the other girl knows she can say no without any consequences. When she feels a soft nod on the crook of her elbow, Kate continues, “What did you do when you liked someone? It must have happened before, right?”

    She wants the whole picture, she wants every detail Yelena may be willing to share to understand her to the best of her capabilities.

    “I’ve liked people before but I’ve never let myself… It never amounted to anything. Even if I thought it may be mutual, I never tried. No one ever tried for me, either. So I was left to mourn by myself something I never shared with anyone, not even my closest friend. But then,” she turns her head to look at Kate, her face clouded with something soft and tender and affectionate, “Then, you came along.”

    Her breath catches in her chest at the blonde’s words, at the weight they carry and the meaning they have. She didn’t need it, but to finally have Yelena’s confirmation that this was mutual, that it wasn’t just something in her head, well. It fills her with a warmth she wants to hold onto forever.

A cold nose nudges her own and Kate smiles encouragingly, blinking slowly as she waits for the other girl to continue. She doesn’t pull away, and neither does Yelena, “I saw you and I was helpless. I couldn’t even pretend not to like you, couldn’t cover it. And I couldn’t keep myself away, either. It had never been like this before.” Her voice is barely a whisper but her words ring loud on Kate’s ears as Yelena’s breath hits her lips.

    The brunette takes a long look at the other girl and studies the relaxed set of her shoulders and the content tilt of her lips that shines even through her still wet eyes. Kate figures that perhaps Yelena is feeling a bit of freedom at sharing this glimpse of herself. The younger girl can’t imagine what it must be like to carry that weight on her own for so long.

    She’s so lost in her own thoughts that the Russian’s voice startles her, “I got so caught up in it that we both ended up getting hurt,” Yelena admits sadly, guiltiness clinging at her accent as her eyes search Kate’s.

    “No, Yelena. Stop. Don’t say that,” she mutters vehemently, eyes wide open so the blonde can read everything easily on them, “It’s not because you felt for me that we got hurt. It has got nothing to do with that. It wasn’t selfish and it wasn’t one-sided, either. I was a willing participant, so anything that happens because of this,” their clasped hands move in the short space between their chests, “It’s on both of us. And it’s up to the two of us to make it right, too. You’re not alone in this, in any of it, for as long as you let me.”

Kate holds her close by the waist, moving so the older girl is practically straddling her lap. She twists her fingers in the worn t-shirt and inhales the sweet scent of her own body wash and the remains of Yelena’s sandalwood perfume.

And I’m sorry, too.” She deposits those words reverently in the skin of Yelena’s neck along with a soft brush of her lips to quiet the protest she feels burning on the Russian’s mouth, “I was selfish and greedy and wanted too much too quickly, even after you let me in on your background. I should have been better, and for that I’m sorry,” Kate speaks softly, craning her head back to look up at the older girl.

    If they want to do this right, everything has to be laid down in the open, so while her following words sit heavy on her tongue, Kate forces herself to push them out before she can cower in the comfort of their embrace. “I want everything with you. I think you know, but in case you didn’t, I’m telling you now,” she starts, grinning affectionately at the blush that creeps on the high points of Yelena’s cheekbones, “I like you so much it just - I don’t think I’ve ever felt like this before, really.     Now, to the difficult part, “But - you have to let me know what I can expect. I understand your reality, but you can’t just pick and chose when and where you want me. You can’t just take and then repent and backpedal, because it’s unfair to me. It hurt me and it scared me when I thought I had lost you, and I don’t want to go through that again. I don’t want either of us to. So, tell me what you need from me, what you want, and I promise I’ll give it to you. Just be honest, that’s all I ask.”

    Her heart feels as if it were about to beat out of her chest, and the fidgeting of Yelena’s fingers on the nape of her neck lets Kate know that she’s not the only one nervous out of her mind. She makes it to eleven erratic intakes of breath before the Russian moves, pulling herself back a little to speak again, “I can’t give you what you need, not now at least.” Her small voice carries so much shame and regret that Kate can’t even find it in herself to care about the sharp pain in her heart. She was expecting it, anyway, and she’s beginning to comprehend that Yelena’s pain hurts her more than her own in all of this.

    “I need you to know I’m trying, though. I need to do this on my own, but I’m trying because the risk was worth it, Kate. You are worth it. But I think the only thing fair of me to ask of you is to be there as a friend. At least for now,” it’s more than enough, really. Yelena is being painfully honest right now, and there’s nothing else she can promise but to try.

    They both know it doesn’t necessarily mean success, nor does it guarantee that they’ll both still want this by the end of it, but it’s the best chance they’ve got, and Kate is willing to take it if Yelena is in, too.

    Impulse gets the better of her and she barely manages to catch herself and redirect the kiss to the corner of Yelena’s mouth, her lips tender and lovingly grazing the edge of the blonde’s smile. Lucky for her the older girl doesn’t call her out on it, closing her green eyes instead and letting herself enjoy this kiss that feels like both an ending and a beginning.

    “Then I’ll be your friend for as long as you need me to,” she promises genuinely, her lips still brushing the skin of the Russian’s jaw as she gives her waist a squeeze.

    They let the other go gently, keeping their hands close together and within reach, and take a long look at the other. Kate cracks first, offering a wet smile that Yelena returns instantly. Then the blonde frowns slightly, “It won’t be easy,” it’s a timid warning, a reminder that they may have taken a step in the right direction but there’s still a lot more to come. “I will probably still be… stupid about other people liking you. I’ll try not to, but I can’t promise I’ll succeed.”

    Kate chuckles at the displeased scowl on Yelena’s face and her very deliberate choice to leave out the world jealous, but she will return the favor and doesn’t point it out. “That’s okay, I wouldn’t be all that much smarter. I wasn’t, actually,” the brunette reassures the older girl, smiling demurely at her, “We just have to make an effort and communicate. Set boundaries if it comes down to it. But let’s not worry about that now, okay? We’ll cross the bridges as they come” she mumbles fervently, lifting her pinky gently to graze lightly against Yelena’s.

    The older girl hooks their fingers together and nods solemnly, “As they come,” she parrots back, offering an endearing smile that coaxes the crinkles around her eyes to deepen.

    It almost feels unreal, the idea that they’ve managed to share all that pain and still be able to smile together, as friends. There’s hope burning in her chest. Kate sees it mirrored in Yelena’s green irises, comforting, and for once she decides to let it sit there.

    There are still a few things that Kate wants to know about, mostly regarding the Russian’s past and her way of approaching relationships, but it can wait. They’ve both had enough for today as far as emotionally exhausting talks go.

    “I texted Wanda this morning to let her know you were here,” Kate mentions then, remembering suddenly the redhead’s words, “I’m just letting you know, in case you want to give them a call. She said you were meant to have lunch with them?”

    Yelena’s petulant face makes her look younger than she is, and the brunette has to bite down at her lip to keep a silly grin to grow embarrassingly big on her face, “Ugh, I had completely forgotten. I won’t hear the end of it now,” she whines as she rubs both hands across her face, leaving red patches of skin behind.

    Frowning, Kate grabs at her wrists gently, “Don’t do that, you’ll hurt yourself,” she chastens the older girl, rolling her eyes at the blonde’s poked-out tongue, “And I didn’t tell them anything about yesterday. Just that you slept here. It’s up to you what you want to share, I won’t say anything you don’t want me to.”

    That seems to ease Yelena’s anxious frown into a contemplative look. Her eyes stay laser-

    sharp on Kate’s face, the brunette growing bashful under her loaded stare. She manages to hold it for about three seconds before trying to divert the attention, “Things are getting better? Between the three of you, I mean.” She’s actually curious, and the expectant tilt of her voice seems to drive Yelena’s concentration elsewhere.

    “We are trying. They are - it’s a bit hard, sometimes. Mostly between Natasha and me, which in turn forces Wanda to either pick a side or stay neutral and face twice the shit. But, we keep trying, and they’ve been helping me. They are patient,” the last bit is said fondly, almost as if Yelena didn’t believe herself worthy of such treatment. It makes Kate’s soft spot for both redheads grow exponentially bigger.

    “That’s all that matters. As long as you guys keep trying, it’s only a question of time. You’ll get there,” she’s confident, not a shadow of doubt in her mind about it. She’s seen the way the three of them care about each other, knows Yelena loves them even if she pretends not to.

    They’ll make it just fine.

    The tender look on Yelena’s face forces Kate to chant the world friend over and over in her head as she keeps eye contact with her. “So will we, right?” the older girl asks candidly but vulnerable, and Kate doesn’t hold back the emotions thickening her voice this time as she agrees.

    “Yeah, so will we.”

Before, they had either not crossed the friendship line but been acutely aware that they could - and that the other wanted to, too-, or they had barreled through it and then promptly acted as it had never happened. Now, the undercurrent tension that had always been there - first ignored and then thrown into each other’s face - had been addressed, so while the two of them are aware it still exists, removing the uncertainty about how the other would act has helped them move forwards in spite of it.

    It had been a good change, welcomed for sure. It didn’t mean that she had complete control over her feelings towards the Russian, but at least they had managed to settle into a middle ground that allowed them to be close without either of them burning down.

They may have overlooked a few of the boundaries that divide friendship and something else, but disregarding that fact, Yelena had become literally her fucking best friend. Kate had shared more with the blonde in the short time they had known each other than she had with anyone else before. And sure, it didn’t mean that everything had changed now (she still was, by far, Kate’s favorite person) but there was a new timidness to their encounters that hadn’t been there previously to their fallout.

    Shaking her head before she can keep drowning in her own insanity she walks over to where her friend is waiting patiently for her to answer. “No, Yel said she had already taken her out for a run before I came around,” she repeats the words the Russian had shot at her over her shoulder as she rushed to her sister’s car with a promise to see each other later tonight.     Pietro.

    The silver-haired boy was already trotting down the stairs with a bow that thankfully wasn’t Kate’s because the way he kept hitting it against the wall would have earned him an arrow through the shoulder. “Hey, Wan, how would you feel about being an only child?” the younger girl asks as her eyes shoot draggers to where the older Sokovian had just disappeared to, “Cause me throttling your brother is swiftly becoming a very real possibility.”

Pietro sends his friend a text telling her to just dart inside once she gets there and when Kate points out that saving a spot for your nemesis is not a very villain-ly thing to do, he just looks at her dissaprobinlgy and reminds her that Well, she may be my rival but I have manners.

The arrow still sinks on the bullseye (just not in the middle) but the mortifying display remains on the air. Pietro shoots her an unimpressed look while the newcomer observes her with a wicked smirk on her lips.

What she does, though, is shoot a last, longing glance above her shoulder at the younger girl. Kate kinda wishes she wasn’t so in tune with the blonde’s emotions, kinda wished she could miss a few more things.

    “Hm, that was something,” Carol’s voice brings her back to where she’s precariously balancing herself in a pair of wheeled shoes, her uneasiness coming back full force now that there’s not a strong, firm body keeping her upright. The brunette stumbles a bit on her spot and the older woman reacts instantly, jumping over the railings and taking Kate’s elbow delicately with her left hand as her right one falls to the small of her back.

Kate knows Carol wasn’t jealous - they had discussed this before, so it just didn’t make sense - so she was completely lost as to what had been the blonde’s intention before. Sure, perhaps it had been a reflex reaction from someone prideful at being faced with an equal, although something told her that wasn’t exactly it.

    Still, Carol had tried to deescalate the situation when she’d picked on Kate’s discomfort, and she was now babbling about how Sam and his friends had made her get here later than she’d intended because they had gotten lost and she’d had to drive halfway back to guide them and - she’s not going to mention the incident if Kate doesn’t bring it up first.

    “I wouldn’t try poking fun at the only thing saving you from eating concrete, Bishop,” Carol chuckles as she readjusts her grip on her body, now throwing her arm completely around the younger girl’s waist. She returns the kiss, placing hers right in the corner of Kate’s mouth, a giggle slipping from the brunette’s lips involuntarily as they lock eyes.

    Understanding shines on Carol’s eyes unabashedly and Kate’s suddenly very grateful because she definitely needed someone like this woman in her life.

    After the newcomers had collected their own skates and had drowned a few more drinks with the rest of them, Eddie had marched right up to the DJ booth to demand (read: beg) for eighties songs to dance to. He insisted this was the only way his birthday fantasy would be complete, and their group was way too drunk and bored with his shenanigans to even try and go against it.

    Carol’s presence now didn’t mean she was glued to the brunette’s side at all, choosing instead to race with Pietro and Sam across the rink childishly and winking at Kate every time she caught her eye.

    What it did mean, though, was that Yelena definitely wasn’t coming anywhere near Kate, either. She figures it had something to do with their encounter earlier, but still, the younger girl couldn’t help but selfishly wish the Russian would find a way to overcome her aversion towards Carol and stop acting as if acknowledging Kate’s existence was a chore now.

    Then, Kate remembers the twisting in her gut when she used to think Yelena and Bucky had something going on. She remembers the need to escape, to just not see, and so she bites her cheek and shuts her mouth.

    The smoking committee - as M.J. had smartly baptized Bucky, Yelena, Pietro, and Sam -

    walk outside after the birthday boy had deemed he’d made an enough fool of himself after his (admittedly good) rendition of I Want to Break Free, Yelena leading and hitting the door open with more force than it would probably be deemed necessary.

    No one else seems to catch the scene except for Pietro, who very unsubtly turns around to catch the brunette’s eyes. Kate only manages to give him a discouraged shrug of her shoulders and smile feebly at the Sokovian’s attempt to lift up her spirits by pretending to walk into the now-closed door.

    The main reason why she gets away without having to explain Yelena’s display to the rest of her friends is because they are absolutely smitten by Carol. They hadn’t had the chance to properly talk with the older blonde before, seeing as Kate has monopolized the woman’s attention so far, so now they are discovering exactly the same Kate did a few weeks ago: that Carol is probably the epitome of cool.

    “So you’ve been going around riding a fucking motorcycle with your arms around a hot ass blonde and you haven’t said a single word?” M.J. hisses affronted at her, “I thought we were sisters. It is your duty as such to keep me informed of every single person you hook up with. I can’t be getting the news as everyone else!”

    Kate groans helplessly as Carol shoots them a shit-eating grin, but the blonde gets interrupted before she can take any jab at Kate’s expense. Another of Eddie’s friends - a raven-haired woman named Jessica, if Kate’s foggy brain remembers correctly - places her hand on Carol’s knee and turns her phone her way, a picture of whatever accessory for their bikes they were talking about on her screen. Eddie squeezes himself in between them, highly interested in the conversation, “So, Danvers, you’ve gotten here on your bike, you say? Cause I definitely wanna see that baby,” he says sweetly, making googly eyes at the blonde.

    It turns out no one, not even Carol Danvers herself, is immune to Edward Brock’s charm.

    That’s how Kate is promptly out of her skates and on her feet, following after Jessica and Eddie who talk excitedly about mechanics, Carol at her side with an arm around her shoulders and M.J. hot on her heels and pushing her forward.

    Coincidentally, of course, Carol’s vehicle happens to be parked a short distance away from where their other friends are still blowing out smoke in a semicircle.

    The three friends begin to talk in very technical words about Carol’s bike, Eddie and Jessica nodding along raptly at the blonde’s explanation as she points at different parts of the motorcycle. Even M.J. is listening intently. “Dude, you don’t even have a driver’s license. What’s gotten you so interested in a bike’s engineering?” Kate asks amused at the other brunette, an unladylike snort forcing its way out when the younger girl looks at her haughtily and mumbles that she’s interested in everything a hot miss lady like Carol deems interesting.

    It is a very hot bike, Kate can admit to it. Of course she’s not an expert, but even she can see that it’s big and wide and has like - more buttons than she imagines a regular motorcycle having. It seems to pike the others’ interest too, Sam and Pietro cutting down the distance between the two groups to stand next to the blonde and admire the vehicle from up close.

    Even Bucky seems intrigued, honestly. But he stays loyally put next to Yelena, who looks downright appalled by the whole exhibition. The brunette musses it must have to do more with who is doing the explaining than to the object in itself - Kate knows Yelena finds motorcycles absolutely awesome too, the older girl going as far as to mention she’d like to have one someday more than once.

    Carol hands Jessica the remote to start the engine and the black-haired woman low-whistles at the sound. The boys crow around, all awed mumbles and admiring hands as they caress the seat and the body of the vehicle. Out of the corner of her eye, Kate sees Yelena roll her eyes hard at the display and mumble something that sounds suspiciously like heterobasic men.

    The conversation flows easily between the older bunch of their friends, and M.J. takes her opportunity to sit at the bike admiringly once Jessica dismounts and helps her gently on. The other New Yorker doesn’t stop making awestruck gestures with her hand at Kate, who just shakes her head affectionally at her and listens indulgingly at the other girl’s ramble about how absolutely cool it is that Kate managed to land such a cool lady.

    Later, Kate will reflect on all the mistakes that lead to this imminent outcome.

    First one: being rather tipsy. Self-explanatory, really. Had she been fully conscious, she may have been able to see (and avoid) the mistakes that follow.

    Second one: walking to stand in front of the bike. May not seem like such a huge thing, but then -

    Third one: not killing the bike’s engine before M.J. gets on it. That one is no one’s fault, honestly. Carol wasn’t paying attention, Jessica plainly doesn’t know that the younger girl has absolutely no idea about how motor vehicles work, and M.J. well - she’s fucking clueless as to how motor vehicles work.

    It all happens in an instant. M.J. rolls tentatively the throttle between her fingers. It’s a caress, tops, but it’s enough for the engine to rev and jolt the bike forward in a short, sharp movement. The momentum pushes Kate back and she trips, her current lack of balance and the force of the impact making the fall harder than it would have been under other circumstances.

    She falls on her left side and an agonizing pain shoots through her elbow and her wrist straight away. And then, chaos breaks loose.

    There’s shouting all around, different voices meshing together anxiously and pounding on Kate’s ears. There’s motion, too. Sharp and erratic and crowding. But the only thing the brunette manages to focus on is the aching throb on her arm. She’s holding it close to her chest even though she can’t for the life of her remember when she sat up and moved. There’s a burning sting in her eyes where exertion is pushing tears out, and she closes them as she tightens her grasp on her elbow in reflex.

    Absolutely a terrible idea.

    “Hey, no. Don’t do that malyshka. You’re going to hurt yourself more.” A sweet, gentle voice whispers right in her ear as mindful fingers come to cradle Kate’s tender arm. She lets out a whimper as her own hand gets pried away and replaced by a warmer, more careful one.

    Those new fingers apply pressure in a way that makes the pain first shoot through her whole body merciless but then dissipate a bit, and she moans as the person holding her shushes and mumbles affectionately against her temple.

    It takes Kate exactly one breath to know the arms around her belong to Yelena.

    She lets her forehead fall to rest against the blonde’s collarbones, wondering if Yelena is unusually cold or if she’s as scorchingly hot as she feels. Kate tries to call the older girl’s name, if only to make sure she’s really there, but it only comes as a pained groan. Still, Yelena brings her closer by the shoulder and keeps talking to her, and Kate forces herself to focus on her words and her lips on her temple.

    Through the deafening ringing in her ears she catches a loop of ongoing I’m so sorry’s that sounds a lot like M.J., a fierce Don’t touch her that she’s got no doubt is absolutely Yelena, an offended Who the fuck do you think you are that Kate has trouble identifying but resembles Carol’s voice, and a panicked Cut the testosterone showdown girls, she’s hurt that comes from further away but, if the brunette had to guess, she’d say belongs to Pietro.

    Then, after all the stillness, the world seems to come back into a hazardous motion. Kate’s being jostled up by a pair of strong arms, her hurt elbow placed precariously against a hard chest as to not hurt her worse. The movement causes the pain to come back fiercely anyway, and she reaches out desperately for Yelena again. The Russian is at her side immediately, hand clasping hers as she keeps up the pace of whoever’s carrying the injured brunette.

    (Later Kate will learn that was Bucky. She’ll purposely kiss his cheek in thanks and thoroughly enjoy the blush that will redden his skin.)

    The comforting presence of Yelena’s hand on her own disappears only for a minute, which is how long it takes for the older girl to crawl into Pietro’s back seat before reaching for Kate’s body meticulously as Bucky deposits her inside. Her hand returns then to hold Kate’s wounded arm and the brunette hides her face on the crook of her neck again, inhaling Yelena’s perfume trying to use the grounding scent to pull herself out of her shocked, semi- conscious state. Or at least, to not dwell deeper into it.

    Another person gets into the car and sits next to her, a hand hurriedly clutching at Kate’s tight and then moving up to pull back her hair. It’s Carol, the brunette’s almost positive she can recognize the callousness of her fingers against her scalp. Then the car starts and they hurry off to where the younger girl can only hope is the hospital. She’d love to ask, really, but she’s pretty sure the only noises coming out of her mouth right now are going to be pained cries and mumbles. And she’s not sure she would be able to process the answer anyway either, so she stays quiet and focuses on the rise and fall of Yelena’s chest.

    “Don’t worry malyshka, it’s okay. I’m here. I’ve got you,” the blonde repeats fervently, her soft lips caressing her ear, and it’s all it takes for Kate to close her eyes and feel the air begin to blow softly into her lungs again.

She knows because she hears the sneering voices, feels the tension on Yelena’s body and the angry tremble on Carol’s hand, but after an irked Pietro speaks in a low, serious voice - threatening in a way Kate didn’t think was possible of him, honestly - a still silence engulfs them for the rest of the ride.

    They get to the hospital in an impressively short time, and Kate has to wonder just how many laws Pietro has broken to get them here. The brunette still needs help to get out of the car, her limp arm keeping her from pushing her own body out, but at least the stormy haze that had taken over her brain is slowly evaporating and giving way to throbbing consciousness.

    Kate walks into the ER by her own feet but with two unrelenting blondes flanking each of her sides.

    The second she’s inside there’s a nurse next to her and a very kind man offering her a wheelchair to sit down on. Then she’s being ushered inside as she hears the older woman inform her friends hastily that only one of them can come along. Kate misses whatever process of decision they follow, but Yelena materializes at her side instantly

    She briefly contemplates the possibility that Yelena didn’t really give a choice, but the younger girl doesn’t even try to hide the relief on her face anyway.

    After swallowing the pills she’s handled and thirstily drowning a full glass of water she begins to feel more grounded. Yet, Kate’s immensely grateful when Yelena takes it on herself to explain to the doctor what had happened once the imposing woman walks into the room, the brunette’s choked-up throat still not up for long speeches.

    Dr. Cruz begins to prod and move her arm in an absurdly torturous way, the pain hitting Kate full force now that she’s becoming completely aware of it. She holds back her pitiful whines better this time though, mostly thanks to the Russian next to her who offers her hand as an outlet for Kate to grip at it fiercely. Yelena never once stops rubbing calming circles with her thumb on her knuckles, not even when the younger girl is pretty sure she’s about to crush her fingers.

We’ll run some X-rays now to fully confirm this,” her doctor happily announces, congratulating Kate on miraculously not having broken any bone. She has to bite her tongue to keep from asking if this is really something to be so cheerful about, the burning ache on her arm mocking her silently.

I think if you’re wearing an undershirt you’ll be able to skip the hospital gown for this.” She actually lifts Kate’s sweater to make sure she’s wearing something else underneath instead of waiting for an answer, nodding approvingly when she finds a purple tanktop, “Fantastic. Take this and all your jewelry off, then you’re good to go.”
