She’s a bit apprehensive about what happens now - Is Yelena going to stay tonight? Is Carol? Are they both staying? Are they leaving after she settles in?

    Lucky jumps her the second she opens the door, Pietro swiftly holding her by the shoulders so she doesn’t stumble backward.“What did we just say about heart attacks Katie Kate? God. Gimme the dog’s leash, I’m walking him before we have to make another trip to the hospital. You guys are going to give me wrinkles. I’ll send you my surgeon’s bill!” he mutters, already making his way down the stairs again with a happy puppy in tow.

    Those are two damn sneaky golden retrievers leaving Kate to fend for herself.

    When she finally walks fully inside her apartment she’s honestly amazed at the way the two blondes seem to be somehow sharing space and standing as farther away from each other as possible simultaneously. The frown on Carol’s face matches the one on Yelena’s, the only difference being the latter has her eyes closed and is rubbing at the bridge of her nose resignedly.

    Taking a deep breath she decides it’s pretty much her responsibility to break the thick tension, “I’m going to need a bit of help to take these off.” She points to her tightly laced-up boots, shaking her foot to draw their attention to her shoes, and watches raptly the silent exchange that takes place before her eyes.

    Carol and Yelena look at each other for what’s definitely an unnecessary amount of time, the glare not softening on either side but seemingly reaching a tolerant truce after a few breaths. Surprisingly, it’s Yelena the one who caves first, “You help her, I’ll go make some tea,” she almost grits between her teeth, marching into the kitchen and busying herself with fiddling with the cabinets like she owns the place.

    It’s probably part of whatever it is she’s trying to prove, Kate thinks, but then again Carol knows her way around her apartment pretty well by now too, so it’s an even fight on that front. The older blonde watches the scene unfold quietly, arms crossed as her hazel eyes dance from Yelena to settle on Kate. Sighing, she opens her arms invitingly, “Come on, Bishop. Let’s get you into something more comfortable.”

    As Carol helps her sit on her bed and kneels to undo her boots’ laces, Kate feels the question burning her way up her throat, “What did just happen?” There was a full conversation - or argument, probably - that she had missed, of that she was sure. She was pretty okay with it too, but she kind of needed to know where they stood. Where they all stand.

    “An understanding happened,” the blonde whispers straightforwardly, shrugging a shoulder in such a Yelena way that Kate knows for certain she does have a fucking type. Carol is not going to elaborate, that much she knows, but Kate’s okay with it. The two of them may be similar in a lot of ways, but where Yelena may be teetering at times Carol has proven herself to be steady and riskless. The younger girl trusts her judgment almost more than her own, so she just smiles and takes the answer for what it is.

    A kiss falls to the corner of her mouth before she’s hit squarely on the face with her sleepwear, and Kate smiles despite herself.

    There’s gleeful barking when they make their way to the living room again, Pietro lounging contentedly on Kate’s couch as Yelena sits on a stool and rubs at Lucky’s ears lovingly. That dog adores her more than he does his own owner, but the brunette can’t bring herself to be mad about it. She shares the sentiment, after all.

    Yelena watches her with gentle eyes, pushing a cup of steamy tea her way when Kate sits on the empty chair next to her. The smile on her face is sweet and tired and unguarded and it makes the brunette feel so many things she’s pretty sure she’s going to give herself the concussion she dodged earlier.

    Conversation flows easily between Pietro and Carol, with Kate chiming in every few minutes as she fights her need to doze off. Yelena stays quiet but present next to her, and at some point - that the younger girl absolutely misses (or missed) - it’s been decided that neither of them will be leaving for the night.

    As Kate takes a look around the room she has to hold back the urge to pinch herself. How the hell did she manage to trick these three fucking amazing, beautiful humans into even glancing her way - let alone wanting to be with her - she doesn’t know, but she’s thanking the universe purposefully for them every single damn day from now on.

    The day after the debacle had been almost as chaotic as the previous night: M.J. had been knocking frantically at her door at eight in the morning, a sleepy Peter in tow balancing precariously a tray full of coffees and a bag of pastries in his hands. The other brunette had almost reduced to tears when she saw Kate’s arm on a sling, apologies falling from her mouth way faster than Kate’s groggy brain was able to process.

    It had taken two cups of tea courtesy of one drowsy Yelena and a patiently affectionate explanation of how high-end vehicles work and how M.J. couldn’t really have predicted that outcome from Carol for the younger girl to calm down. Kate had reassured her over and over again that she was fine and that she had been the careless one, and after an hour and a half of banter, they had reached the agreement that no one had truly been at fault.

    Pietro slept uselessly through the whole thing as expected and woke up just in time to steal the last chocolate chip cookie from Kate’s waiting fingers, and everything tilted back into its axis again.

    The rest of her friends reached out to her during that day to make sure she was alright: Wanda had popped up around midday and had spent a good twenty minutes scolding her and another two hours pampering her like a mother hen; Eddie and Cassie had appeared unannounced shortly after that and stayed for a while, and both Bucky and Sam called and made her promise to save a spot in her cast for them to sign. Even Jessica had sent her well wishes through Eddie and Carol, so all in all the warm feeling in her chest mostly outweighed the painful, persistent pulsation on her elbow.

    Life progresses painfully slow after that.

    Kate’s thankful that she managed to maim her left arm and not the right one (although the thought of messing up her string arm worries her to no end) because at least she can keep some semblance of being a semi-functional human. She curses herself at least five times a day for being dumb enough to get in front of a running motorcycle anyway.

    It takes some time to adjust to working around with only one arm, and it also takes some time for her to stop hitting every single wall with her cast. Yelena always magically happens to be nearby to roll her eyes fondly at her after Kate grins sheepishly, and the brunette wonders whether she does it unconsciously on purpose to see the affection on the Russian’s eyes, or if like Wanda, Yelena also happens to be a sorcerer who materializes at the right times.

    It’s also incredibly frustrating for someone as independent as Kate to need help so often now because, for as much as she assures her mother (whom she only calls because Yelena downright forces her to) that she’s okay and she can manage on her own, the truth is she can’t. Or rather - she can, but it takes way more time than usual which for the impatient brunette is almost worse.

    That’s mainly why she doesn’t complain much when Carol insists on dropping by almost every night to check on her, and why she doesn’t grumble too much when Yelena appears at her door on the mornings more often than not. The two blondes miraculously never once cross paths, the nights Carol stays in curiously being the following mornings where Yelena doesn’t come by. If she didn’t know better Kate would think they have the whole thing planned.

    She knows better though, because even if both women put up with the other’s existence for Kate’s sake, they don’t skimp on thinly veiled jabs at each other.

    Admittedly, Carol is way more inconspicuous about it, reducing the extent of her dislike for the Russian to some cross-eyed mocking stares sent Kate’s way when the brunette mentions something about Yelena and carefully even-toned questions about the other blonde’s actions or whereabouts.

    This, of course, is another thing that sets them apart, because Yelena on the other hand is as evident and blatantly obvious as she can be. Kate’s learned not to say anything about Carol when she’s around, because then she breaks Yelena’s bubble of ignorance and the Russian can’t pretend Carol doesn’t exist and she lashes out the only way she knows how - by being cold and closing off.

    So, with the sole purpose to make sure Kate survives those few weeks in a cast, the two women find a way to dance around each other and keep the brunette as a middle ground, the younger girl also making a conscious effort to say put. If all of it also happens to make the situation way messier with her affection for both of them growing and tangling, that’s a problem Kate’s keeping for herself.

    Two weeks and six days are what Dr. Cruz ends up deeming the sufficient amount of time for Kate to be put through the misery of wearing a cast. It’s one day less than three weeks, and even if the doctor insists she’ll have to wear a removable splint for some time, the brunette chooses to focus on her wins.

    “One would say we’re going off to Disney with the way you keep vibrating in your seat, Bishop,” Carol chuckles, side eying her with an amused smirk on her lips. She’s driving them - on Kate’s car, because she may still think bikes are hot but she’s grown a tad apprehensive about them - to the hospital so Kate can finally be free from the torture device on her arm. To say she’s ecstatic would be a complete understatement.

    The nurse that walks them into the doctor’s office is (thankfully) different from the other two that tended to Kate the night of her mishap, but she’s sure there’s a curious, mirthful glint on Dr. Cruz’s eyes when the woman spots a totally different blonde next to her patient this time.

    While Carol observes entranced the process of cutting open the cast, Kate feels a speckle of nostalgia at seeing all the cute little doodles her friends had drawn on it. The thought lasts for a total of three seconds, which is how long it takes for her to begin to fold her elbow back and forth carefully. She lets out a sigh of relief when her nails hit the skin on her forearm, and then that fucking mass of plaster can burn in hell for all she cares.

    After a short revision and a couple of stretches and bends, the doctor seems satisfied with the results and patiently reminds Kate of the rehab exercises she’s meant to do and the use she has to make of her splint to make sure she doesn’t land herself on her clinic again. The older woman sends them off with more painkiller prescriptions and an “I’ll see you and your companion in three months, Kate!” and the brunette’s sure that was a conscious dig, but she lets it slide. She’s just gotten her arm back, after all.

    They order pizza once they get back to Kate’s apartment, the blonde nursing a beer on her couch while the younger girl runs up and down throwing things messily into the open suitcase that sits on her living room table.

    Eying the spot on the carpet where Kate’s already tripped twice, Carol sips her drink and frowns, “If you trip and hurt yourself again I will not be happy, Kate.” The older woman has no choice but to snort and shake her head when Kate begins to theatrically walk slowly around the place for a few seconds before sticking her tongue out at her.

    Then she begins to race around the rooms because it’s getting late and she’s got a lot of stuff to pack.

    The trip to Washington was announced to them by professor Myriad on their introductory lesson (yeah, the one Kate missed), the whole thing being an annual event that their Master’s board organize so they can visit a few institutions and show of the pictures on their webpage to sell the course to the next generation of students.

    Kate’s not complaining though, she gets three days without classes and out of town with her friends. If the price are a few boring lectures by some fatigued official at the World Bank or at the International Monetary Fund then she’s more than willing to pay it.

    “Help me, what should I take - this one,” the brunette asks holding up a pair of black leather pants and a cute red blouse, “Or this one,” a brown skirt with a white corset-like shirt. Carol narrows her eyes and drags her thumb across her chin pensively, “You look hotter in leather pants, that’s for sure, but - the white thingy there, that looks spectacular.” The blonde nods her head decisively and tips her drink back again, satisfied with her answer.

    It’s not very helpful because Kate feels like the outfits she had planned in her head are all mismatched now, but she appreciates the effort and the compliment. Smiling as she places the four pieces of clothing neatly folded inside her suitcase and after checking her mental list, she zips it closed.

    There’s a nagging feeling at the back of her head that she tries her best to ignore as she fiddles around with her toiletry, but the older woman catches on to her restless energy nonetheless. “What’s got you so on edge? Are you really that excited about going to Washington?” Carol asks jokingly, but the way she places her hands on her shoulders clearly aims to soothe her nerves.

    Sighing, Kate bites her lip as she avoids the blonde’s gaze, “You’re going to laugh at me.” But the other girl just waits patiently in silence in the way she knows will end up with Kate giving in, so the younger girl resigns herself to the mockery, “I’m just not a big fan of airplanes, okay? Those huge, heavy things have absolutely no business flying a thousand miles up in the air for such a long time.”

    She crosses her arms petulantly to further prove her conclusiveness, and exactly as she expected a cute chuckle sounds right next to her ear. Kate turns around, a death glare firm in place and ready to smolder Carol, but finds a warm exasperation gleaming in hazel eyes that throws her off.

    “Kate, I’m an aeronautical engineer. I can easily explain to you how airplanes work so you can go into your flight absolutely unconcerned if you’d like,” and okay, she has a point there. Kate just hadn’t connected the dots between what she and Pietro studied and what it actually meant, she kind of just assumed it was brainiacs stuff and retired herself from the conversation.

    The hands on her shoulders push her gently to the couch, the two of them huddling under the blanket as Carol launches on a very long, very technical explanation of an airplane’s mechanics. The first bit Kate manages to process - and it does make sense and sounds reassuring, really - but the rest is lost to her as she snuggles into the warmth of the other woman’s body and closes her eyes.

    It may not have been the point of it at all, but it turns out to be the best sleep she’s ever gotten on a night before a flight, so Carol definitely considers she succeeds.

    Another thing to despise about traveling by plane is the unhealthily amount of hours you have to be there before your flight is meant to take off. You have to leave way earlier in case some unexpected traffic drama catches you on your way. Checking through security is a hassle in and of itself: you spend what feels like an eternity on the queue and once it’s your turn it seems like everyone’s suddenly in a hurry and you almost fall flat on your ass as you struggle to take off your boots. And then, after all that is over, you always have time to spare because you miscalculated and there’s still an hour and a half left until boarding starts.

    Yeah, Kate absolutely hates planes, don’t mind how they supposedly are the safest means of travel there is (Carol’s words, not hers), she’d very much rather take her car and make the five-hour drive there.

    Surprisingly enough, there seems to be someone in their group who handles the airport way worse than she does.

    Yelena has been on edge since they set foot on the security check line, her face pale and her posture tense. They are at the back of the party - sometimes Kate forgets that there are actually more students in their class other than her friends - and the two of them are closing their group. The brunette thought she had been projecting when she greeted the Russian outside earlier and Yelena barely responded, but something’s definitely off and Kate’s a bit anxious because she doesn’t know how to comfort someone who’s upset about the exact same thing she is.

    “You know airplanes are the safest vehicles there are? Like, comparatively, way more people die in car accidents than in airplane crashes,” she tries to parrot part of the speech she remembers from last night, wincing immediately because mentioning airplane crashes is probably not the best way to ease a worried mind.

    It takes Yelena a second to register her words, her mind clearly on an override. When she does though, her eyes fall to her shoes and her fingers take to twirl her rings, “I’m not scared of airplanes. I just don’t like this part,” she mutters softly, the vulnerability in her voice catching Kate off guard. Yelena looks apprehensively at the security guards conducting a quick search on a middle-aged woman who looks like she had forgotten to take off her belt, and oh -

    How the fuck can she be so damn dense. Of course Yelena would be wary of airports.

    “Bad memories, you know?” the older girl supplies right after Kate’s brain cells make the connection on their own, confirming her train of thought. Russia and the orphanage and the ugly, disgusting people who tried to take her away and her sister and the spoon.

    The conversation feels like a thousand years away now, but Kate remembers vividly the helplessness she felt while Yelena shared that bit of her past with her. She remembers the way Yelena’s voice broke and she remembers the sorrow on her face and the tears in her eyes. She remembers that Yelena trusted her with something that felt sacred - that still does. And so, she decides this falls on her shoulders, too.

    Placing a careful hand on the small of Yelena’s back as to not startle her, the younger girl lets out a quiet breath when she feels the blonde relax under her touch and melt into her side. Kate moves her fingers to rest on the dip of her waist, squeezing lightly and bringing her body closer to hers, “Listen, I won’t pretend to know what’s going on in here -“ she whispers as she gently taps two fingers against the Russian’s temple, “Because I absolutely don’t. But this is different, okay? We’ll place our suitcases on the belt and we’ll have trouble taking off our earings, and then we’ll walk through that arch shoeless and it will be disgusting but we’ll be okay. I’ll be right behind you, I promise.”

    And her babbling must have some sort of effect on the fog of Yelena’s brain because her green eyes look up at Kate earnestly. She doesn’t speak, but her blonde hair tickles Kate’s nose when Yelena nods and she feels an arm sneak around her own waist, and so the younger girl tightens her hold protectively, pushes her suitcase forward with her foot, and keeps her mouth shut.

    As promised, Kate nudges Yelena gently so she can go first. The blonde looks fleetly over her shoulder and back at Kate before she walks through the guards, the younger girl smiling at her from the other side the whole time.

    As expected, it’s Kate the one who forgets to take off her (Yelena’s) necklace and ends up setting off the alarms. They scan her quickly and brush her off, but it still earns her an entertained look from the blonde. She takes it happily though, because the way her eyes begin to glint with mirth now that the worst is over is way better than the worry that clouded them before.

    They steal an empty table for themselves as Yelena and Bucky wander off to buy coffee for the rest while they wait, and it becomes acutely aware then that Wanda is missing. She had to attend to some family business with Pietro and couldn’t make it to the trip, so the rational side of the team is lacking. The redhead’s gentle calm is absent as they gather together and it shows, the overexcitability of the younger members - and Eddie - tipping the scale against Bucky and Yelena’s quietness.

    Even so, she’s honestly glad for their boisterous antics. The louder they talk the quiet the bubbling anxiousness at the back of her mind gets and the least she thinks of their highly impossible but very tangible dooming death.

    Right as Yelena carefully places a steamy latte in front of him, Eddie calls for their attention, “Hey, let’s play a game, shall we? To pass the time.” The blonde hands Kate and Peter their hot chocolates and as she sits next to the brunette, she motions for Eddie to continue, “Okay, so this game is called can you imagine,” a loud snort comes from behind him as Bucky drops down next to M.J., but he zips his lips at the death glare his friend send him, “There are no rules to it, really. One of us will ask a question that starts with can you imagine and the rest has to - well, imagine.”

    Masterfully hiding her lips behind the rim of her cup, Yelena mumbles “Shocking,” loud enough for Bucky and Kate to hear. The younger girl barely manages to stifle a laugh by chewing at the inside of her cheek and Bucky passes a chuckle as a sneeze. The other three members of this mismatched team seem absolutely delighted at the idea though, and it’s then that Kate realizes that, when at disadvantage, she’s the rational’s team reserve member.

    The conspirational smile the Russian sends her way as M.J. begins to clap his hands and Peter hurries to come up with an imagined scenario is so worth it though, so she can cope with being mature for a while.

    “Okay, okay. I’ve got one: Can you guys imagine what kind of superhero you’d be if you got superpowers?” Peter whispers absolutely enthralled at his contribution to the game, and the thrilled sparkle in Eddie’s wide eyes suggests he absolutely agrees. Clearing his throat dramatically, he begins, “Oh, my dear child. This may be the best question you could have ever asked me. I would, of course, be an ancient wizard who’s able to mess with space and time.”

    Their game picks up from there: they imagine what would have happened if neither of them had been late to their first class, what other nationalities they’d be if they couldn’t be theirs, what would be different if they had been born of the opposite sex, and what would they would change if they got to be president. They actually finish their last round on the boarding queue, Eddie winking at her fondly as their friends raise their voices to talk over each other, and the brunette realizes that he must have done some of it on purpose. His soft “You’re welcome, little bird,” only further confirms her suspicions, and the tight hug she gives him right before they check their tickets couldn’t last long enough.

    Then she actually sees the plane through the hallway’s glass walls and she really wishes she could change her early answer about superpowers. She’d really like to be able to teleport so she could avoid this shit.

    A soft hand falls on her the small of her back and gently pushes her forward. “What’s your seat, Kate Bishop?” Yelena asks as she tiptoes over the brunette’s shoulders to catch the number on Kate’s phone. That’s a good damn question, the younger girl thinks as she frowns and glances out of the window again. She finger-counts the number of small rectangles she sees on the airplane. There are about thirty-ish seats on that plane so thirteen means she’ll be somewhere near the wings. That was a good thing, wasn’t it?

    God, she should have paid more attention to Carol’s lecture last night.

    “Eddie, mind switching seats with me?” she hears Yelena say, the blonde giving their friend her seat number - next to Bucky, if the childish high-five they give each other is any indication - right after Eddie agrees. At the younger girl’s questioning stare the Russian simply shrugs, “Now we can sit together and they can continue to fight about the perks of being Cuban over Argentinian.”

    Yelena sticks by her side the whole time, helping her load her suitcase into that stupidly small compartment and yielding Kate the window seat. A warm hand entangles with hers the second they fasten their seatbelts and the brunette could swear she can feel her chest burst open with affection.

    Trusting Yelena to pay attention for the both of them during the safety instructions speech, Kate chooses to focus instead on the way the spaces between their fingers seem to be made for them to fit perfectly together. If she has to listen about evacuation paths, lifejackets, and landing on water she’s definitely going to lose her last bit of cool.

    A distracted thumb begins to sweep gently over Kate’s pulse point on her wrist, lingering protectively over it when she notices the way the brunette’s heart is beating anxiously. The airplane rumbles and comes to life moving forward and Kate has the nerve to whimper.

    Another hand comes to rest on her cheek and forces her face to tilt to the left, “Hey, let’s continue playing, eh? I’ll start,” Yelena speaks in a low voice right next to her ear, partly drowning the noise of the engine and creating a bubble around them. She coaxes Kate’s head to fall on her shoulder, the younger girl burrowing herself on her side and closing her eyes without needing to be told twice.

    “Okay, so. Can you imagine how things would have been if we had met in a different way?”

    the solemnity that colors her tone lets Kate know this is not something that has just come to her. She wonders how often Yelena has thought about this - an alternative universe where they met and things are not like they were now.

    Huffing a breath and throwing her mostly healed arm across the blonde’s lap, she tightens the hold she has on Yelena’s fingers as she mulls over her answer. There are a thousand ways they could meet: in a fancy party as nobility or in preschool as babies or on a rooftop during a fight as superspies or on a baby shower as middle-aged divorced mothers.

    What slips off her lips instead is, “We would meet in New York, on the subway.” She’s not sure why that seems fitting, but at the curious oh that resonates on Yelena’s throat Kate carries on, “Uh-huh. We would take the same line every day - I’d go to Cornell and you’d be studying in Columbia, so we’d be always deep inside our heads or burying our heads in notes and books. The place would be packed, of course. But I’d notice you anyway.” There’s just not a single version of any universe Kate can think of where she wouldn’t notice Yelena, the mere thought feels inconceivable.

    The rhythmic patterns Yelena’s fingers draw on her arm don’t stop even when the plane takes off and Kate clutches at the older girl’s waist almost desperately.

    “I’d be the rich one and you’d be studying on a scholarship and living with your aunt in Brooklyn,” Yelena provides then, the joke an obvious attempt to take Kate’s mind out of the flight and into their pretend game back again. Then, the cadence of her voice softens, “I’d see you get down a few stops before me and I’d catch you looking back at me over your shoulder.” And the brunette only chuckles because it does sound like something she would absolutely do, alternative reality or not, “You wouldn’t see it but I’d be waiting for it and smiling every time.”

    There’s a yearning in her that feels almost nostalgic, as if Yelena craved for that fantasy. It’s a sweet sentiment, but it also makes Kate unbelievably sad. It’s a bit painful, too, to think the other girl would rather have them in a different setting, in a different position than right here right now. Because Kate for one is fucking sure she wouldn’t trade this for a thing, murderous metal bird-wannabe be damned.

    “We’d steal glances at each other back and forth for the longest time, neither of us making a move because we would be different but we’d still be us,” Kate adds with a dotting roll of her eyes, and Yelena giggles and nods. “Yes, yes. But then, when winter break was coming, I’d finally sit next to you and I’d ask you out for coffee -“

    “Hot chocolate,” the younger girl amends, because if they’re going on their first date near Christmas they’d be having a warm cup of chocolate between their hands. “Hot chocolate,”

    Yelena agrees, squeezing her hand affectionately, “I’d ask you out and you’d say yes because clearly, you’d be smitten by me,” Kate can’t see her from where she’s nudging her nose on the blonde’s collarbones, but she feels the smirk in her words. She smiles in spite of herself.

    “As I would be, too,” the other girl continues, resting her cheek on top of Kate’s head, “And after that, we would just keep seeing each other. We’d go dancing, and we’d go to the cinema, and we’d have dinner with your parents and meet each other’s friends. And I’d be brave enough to be with you.”

    Yelena’s jaw quivers as she deposits those words gently on her hair, sounding almost like an apology. Kate’s stomach sinks and something sharp dodges itself on her throat making it impossible to breathe, and she understands then. She understands why Yelena has thought about this before, why there’s longing and aching in her voice.

    Words pile at her mouth, begging to come out and soothe the girl who’s clinging to her as hopelessly as she is, and Kate thinks back to the times when she used to believe the two of them were great at communicating, at sharing their feelings with each other. There was a candid innocence in her then, a great deal of awful, ugly truths lying underneath their feet too.

    Now everything’s out in the open and crawling at them, but they’re still here. They’re still holding on and playing this fucking hard cards that have been dealt to them. They’re still trying and Kate thinks that proves way more than her wholesome, easy perception ever could.

    Things are difficult as hell but Yelena is still here and so is she, and so words aren’t really necessary between them now. They won’t change anything and the two of them know it, so instead, she closes the distance between her lips and the soft skin on the older girl’s neck and leaves a tender kiss right in the juncture of her collarbones.

    The hold on her fingers slackens, Yelena draping her arm around Kate’s shoulders before reaching for her hand again. The brunette makes it to hold it to twelve stuttering breaths before she mumbles, “I think I wanna change - I’d never go to Cornell if I wasn’t filthy rich, so I’ll settle for a nice full ride at NYU.”

    A wet laugh rings right above her head, the loaded moment passing them by softly as they both watch it go.

    They keep hanging onto the other as the clouds leave room for a few rays of sun that disappear as they begin to descend, the rise and fall of Yelena’s chest lulling her to a state of semi-unconsciousness that only lets her be aware of the smell of her perfume and the lilt of her exhales.

    And she swears she dreams of green eyes on a crowded subway train.
