    Yelena.

    Even if Bucky and the blonde were to share - which Kate figures they could even if the idea doesn’t completely sit well with her - that would throw her into a let’s share a bathroom and probably see each other kinda naked situation with Eddie. And while she may love him to pieces, they haven’t reached that level of intimacy yet.

    She also deliberately ignores what it might mean that she doesn’t feel an ounce of reluctance in living that exact same situation with Yelena.

    That’s a problem for future me.

    The two best friends hurry after Peter and M.J. (or rather, Eddie hurries and Bucky begrudgingly follows behind while pushing both their suitcases) to see if their rooms are close together. Kate chuckles at the image of a celebratory dance cut short when they indeed realize they’re on completely different floors, her fellow New Yorkers trying to cheer Eddie up by inviting him to check out their views.

    And then Kate turns around and looks at the girl standing quietly next to her.

    Yelena.

    A tentative smile pulls at the corners of the Russian’s mouth, dubious as she, too, looks over to where their friends stand. It doesn’t take much effort to know she’s been doing the same maths Kate has, and that she’s also reached the same result. “I guess that makes us roomies, huh?” the brunette asks, trying her hardest to keep the nervousness out of her voice as she wholeheartedly returns the smile. She can’t make this weird - for the both of them.

    There’s a brief flash of uncertainty on Yelena’s face that leaves a wake of surprise behind, “You don’t mind?” Her tone is vulnerable, and Kate’s heart squeezes painfully in her chest.

    They had unboarded the plane in silence, hands still tightly clutching at each other’s until they had reached their group again. The older girl’s confession sat heavily on the air between them - not uncomfortable, just as a hard reality they were both aware of but that still hurt them a bit every time it came up.

    Once they had taken the bus to the city they had split up, Eddie stealing Kate away and forcing her to sit with him so she could help him decide which sunrise picture he should post on Instagram after claiming Bucky had been hopeless during the whole flight. Yelena had let her go with a wistful smile and a slow wink.

    Kate knows Yelena well enough by now to understand where this hesitation is coming from.

    The older girl must have come to the (wrong) conclusion that Kate wouldn’t want to share such close quarters with her again so soon after that reminder. Maybe even had Carol in mind while beating herself over it.

    Whatever it was, she was plain wrong. There wasn’t anyone the brunette would rather be with.

    And that’s another problem for future me, too.

    “Don’t be silly. There’s no way I’m letting either of us end up stuck with someone who snores, Yel. We don’t know these people,” she whispers distrustfully as she casts a quick look around the rest of their classmates. The older girl snorts prettily and Kate grins in victory - always deflect to humor to get rid of an emotionally-charged situation, “Plus, we’re even numbers now that Wan’s not here, and seeing the way things are I think you’re going to be my designated partner this whole trip.”

    Green eyes wander to where their friends are sitting with their bags scattered on the floor, no doubt waiting for them to get their own key so they can all go settle, before they skitter back to expectant blue ones. “I think I’ll have to be,” Yelena heaves a dramatic long-suffering sigh that has Kate pushing their shoulders together in retaliation. The Russian smirks and nods decisively then, “Go get our keys, partner.”

    Happiness shines all across her face and Kate absolutely catches the skip of her heartbeat at that. Treacherous as always, that useless thing.

    Instead of speaking (in case she does something embarrassing like spilling to Yelena she thinks she looks beautiful when she smiles) the brunette returns the nod and walks up to where Fury and Banner stand.

    “Bishop, I’m guessing you’re sharing with Belova?” is absolutely not what she was expecting to come out of her professor’s mouth because - what? But there’s not much she can really say because she is in fact sharing with Belova, so she stupidly resorts to squeaking a yes and bobbing her head erratically up and down. The headmaster barely glances her way as he writes down something on his list and hands her a small piece of cardboard with a room key inside.

    It takes her an unnecessary amount of time of staring at the item in her hand - and a pointed, incredulous look from Fury - to get her moving, but when she does there’s a soft hand on her arm, “What’s our number?”

    Mind still trying to catch up with her mouth, she blinks owlishly at the fingers curling on her elbow and then back to their owner. Yelena looks up at her with an arched eyebrow and a funny glint in her eyes, but thankfully the soft tap of her fingers against the key gets Kate’s brain working again.

    “Oh, uhm. We’re on the - 407,” she reads the almost unintelligible scribble on the folded cardboard, twisting it around so Yelena can also see. The blonde hums and shrugs her shoulders, “We’re on the same hallway as Peter and M.J. then,” she nods her chin back to their friends, three excited puppies and a grumpy cat staring back at them, “I’m sure this will be fun.”

    (Turns out she’ll be completely wrong about that. Or right, depending on which side of Kate’s brain you were to ask.)

    When they reach their room there are, surprisingly, two single beds separated by a respectable amount of space. Kate’s sure she could lay horizontally between them and she’d fit, so while a part of her may be a bit disappointed at the ruined excuse for sleeping on the same mattress with the Russian, she guesses it’s for the best. The universe sending her some kind of signal or something.

    But then of course, the universe being the little shit that it is, doesn’t stay quiet for long and decides to mess with her a tad more.

    There’s insistent knocking on their bedroom door literally not a minute after they’ve closed it behind them - really, Kate hadn’t even taken off her boots yet and her feet were absolutely desperate for some air so, imminent. Yelena opens with an exasperated frown that deepens when Bucky’s disgruntled face appears behind it, the older man not bothering to speak as he marches inside their room and swiftly takes a look around the room before nodding resolutely, “We have to switch.”

    He stands there, arms crossed and a stern look on his regularly stern face, and Kate can’t help but cock her head to the side in wonder. What exactly does he mean, switch? Unsurprisingly Yelena seems to be having the exact same thoughts, because the blonde speaks before she can, “What are you talking about, James?”

    Huffing out a breath, Bucky throws his arms in the air in an exasperated way that absolutely does not fit him at all. “We’ve got a queen-size bed. I can’t sleep on the same bed as Eddie, his snores could outmatch the blaring of a locomotive - and he cuddles in his sleep. If there’s no physical distance he’ll be breathing in my ear every two seconds and I won’t be able to sleep for two days. And you know how I can get when I don’t sleep,” he says, pointing a finger accusatorily at Yelena, “You guys have to take the other room, I’m already taking one for the team by sharing space with a man who makes buildings tremble when he sleeps.”

    As if summoned, Eddie appears behind the door Yelena is still holding open with the tip of her shoe, a sheepish grin on his face and his and Bucky’s bags and suitcases dangling from his arms, “I’d act indignant, but he’s right. Cass sleeps like the dead and still complains, so.” He shrugs helplessly and then invites himself in.

    Yelena seems torn between wanting to look completely unimpressed and trying not to seem way too amused. She’s faring way better than Kate is, the snort that scapes her not going unnoticed by anyone in the room. Bucky looks at her pleadingly, Eddie raises his hands in mock apology, and Yelena smiles entertained and questioning at the same time.

    Are you okay with this?

    “Okay, hand that over you children,” the brunette chooses to answer with action, making grabby hands at Bucky who passes the key between his fingers way too willingly, probably worried about them changing their minds. Eddie lets himself fall onto the bed closer to the window then - the one where Kate was just sitting - announcing, “I’m keeping this one, I need a place to charge my phone close by,” and points to the plug next to it.

    They leave the room and the ensuing fight about the possession of the charging station behind, padding quietly to take the elevator to the ninth floor and then making their way to the end of the hallway. Yelena carries their suitcases while Kate jostles her backpack and the blonde’s dufflebag on one shoulder and wrestles with the door with the other.

    And as expected - as previously stated, explicitly cited as the reason why they were asked to sleep here now - there, in the middle of the room, sits a single, queen-sized bed.

    So yeah. Depending on which side she was to listen to, Yelena was either absolutely right or extremely, downright wrong about this going to be fun.

    Kate would not tell anyone, but she’s secretly rooting for Yelena to be right.

    Dropping their bags unceremoniously on the middle of the bed, the brunette proceeds to kick out her boots. A content puff of breath later has her realizing that they’ve unconsciously settled into the sides of the bed each of them took while they shared hers back in New York - Kate on the left, close to the window Yelena on the right, nearer to the door. She’s saved from dwelling too much on the detail (and on the fact that she remembers even if it only happened for like, a couple of nights) when her phone buzzes in her pocket.

    Half expecting it to be a message from their friends with some other drama, Kate is delightfully surprised when she’s greeted with a picture from Lucky and Fanny crowding around one smiling Natasha Romanoff. There’s a text at the bottom that reads the bestest of friends!!!!, and considering the message is from Pietro and the picture looks like it’s been snapped stealthily from around a corner, the younger girl is positive he took it without the redhead knowing and is actually referring to the three of them.

    “Oh my god, Yel. Come see this,” she wheezes out when her full-blown laughter subsides into content giggles while the Russian looks at her as if Kate had lost her mind. The curious smile on Yelena’s face morphs into a mirthful grin that takes over her whole face when she crawls up the space between them to look at the screen. She balances herself with an arm around Kate’s shoulders and rests her temple against her head to add a twist of the knife, the brunette falling silent and breathless at the sight of Yelena moving like that.

    The phone is gently taken from her hands as the Russian inspects the picture and smirks, “I’ll have to thank Maximoff for this one, now she’ll have to admit she likes my Fanny,” and the conspirational look she gives Kate is enough to pull her mind out of the gutter. She grins when another picture pops up on the chat, now of Lucky and Fanny sitting down next to each other obediently and looking straight at the camera.

    “Aw, my babies,” Yelena coos at the screen at the same time Kate rolls her eyes and says “I just know he’s feeding him pizza, there’s no other way Lucky would stay still like that.”Sure enough, the next shot is Pietro’s face squished between the dogs as he holds up a slice of pizza in his hand. Her golden retriever gazes at it adoringly while the akita looks absolutely repulsed by it, and Kate has no choice but to cackle again.

    Slender fingers type swiftly on her phone (a poorly-veiled threat about bodily harm if the Sokovian so much as thought about feeding her dog pizza followed by a request to remind his sister to give Fanny her meds) and Kate just knows she’s going to face an unnecessarily amount of questions from Pietro when he realizes it’s Yelena the one cussing him out. The blonde sends herself the pictures with a soft smile on her face and Kate thinks she doesn’t care at all.

    A loud ding indicates another text, the younger girl perking up at the promise of yet another cute shot of their dogs together, but the way Yelena’s face sobers up and a scowl taints her features lets her know it’s not the case. She’s worried for a minute about Pietro blurting some inopportune thing - which could be like, highly probable - but the Russian doesn’t keep her guessing for too long.

    “Carol.”

    Even if Kate hadn’t caught the muttered name, the utter disdain that drips from Yelena’s voice would have been sufficient to guess. The blonde lets her phone fall onto the mattress and practically evaporates from Kate’s side, choosing instead to open (rather violently, if she may say so) her suitcase to begin unpacking her clothes.

    Sure enough, there’s a how did the flight go text sitting unread at the top of her notifications, her stomach twisting at the realization that she hadn’t even bothered to let the other woman know she’d made it safely to the hotel after last night’s drama. She writes a quick reply with a promise to talk more later and focuses instead on how to make the thick tension disappear from the room.

    Fiddling with the device in her hands, she blurts out the first thing that comes to mind, “She wanted to know if I had made it through the whole airplane panic without throwing a fit.”

    The humorless chuckle that comes from Yelena lets her know her attempts aren’t working at all, but then again, considering everything that had happened between them on the plane, maybe bringing that up wasn’t the smartest move.

    A hanger clatters when it falls from the Russian’s hand on the carpet, the older girl clearing her throat as she bends down to pick it up again. Then, “How come she’s not the one looking after Lucky?” and Kate is really amazed because this woman, who is capable of being unreadable about virtually everything, is helpless at hiding the animosity in her voice when the other blonde is concerned.

    No matter what it may look like from the outside, Kate’s not stupid. She knows why Yelena acts like this, and she also knows that no matter whatever truce the two women had reached while she had been hurt, it had been nothing but an unspoken agreement to ignore the other. The worst thing is she actually feels torn when she knows she shouldn’t.

    She should be chastening Yelena for being mean towards Carol without a reason, but she’s conflicted. She may not be stupid but she is a bit of a masochist obviously because she knows it’s terrible and she hates herself for it but, it feels fucking good to know the blonde’s feelings for her are still there.

    Another dull thunk, another hanger that falls to the floor. Kate looks up at Yelena and notices then the erraticness in her usually fluid movements, catches sight of a slight tremble in her hands. She’s nervous and the way her eyes keep fleeting back to the brunette sitting cross- legged on the bed only further cements the fact. Yelena is anxious about her answer, about being told off for prodding or intruding, about learning something she clearly doesn’t want to know.

    That thought alone melts Kate’s practically inexistent resolve, “Lucky’s not a big fan of hers, gets weirdly grumpy whenever she comes around, doesn’t let her carry the leash, so - I let the golden retriever take care of my golden retriever.” It’s the truth, her dog’s dislike of Carol seems a cosmic joke when compared with the unconditional love he holds for the blonde in this room.

    Her shoulders sag and a smug smile grows on Yelena’s lips without her trying to hold it back. “That sure is a good boy,” and of course, she would agree with her other child. If Kate didn’t know it was technically impossible she’d think the Russian had trained him herself.

    Kate shakes her head resignedly but with an amused half-smirk on her face, and when Yelena looks at her with a mischievous glint in her eyes from beneath her eyelashes she can’t help but chuckle. The older girl smiles again, and thankfully thought moments pass exactly as the sweet ones do.

    Their eyes stay locked on each other though, and that’s exactly how Kate knows a thought has suddenly dawned on the other girl, “Kate Bishop, what time did they say we had to go down to the lobby?”

    And it’s exactly the slightly concerned tone in her voice that lets Kate know they’re most definitely late.

    Hurriedly lacing up her boots again and ignoring the pressure on her feet, she takes both their coats as Yelena nearly stumbles into her when she pulls to free her purse from the straps of her half-unpacked suitcase. Kate hadn’t even bothered to open hers, so she restores to throwing her own wallet into Yelena’s purse while she checks the time again.

    They were all supposed to meet downstairs almost fifteen minutes ago. Fury is going to be a wonder.

    “Why the fuck did no one bother to text us?!” Kate grumbles with affliction at the blatant disregard of their friends about their whereabouts. Yelena is too busy hitting repeatedly the elevator’s button to answer, “And of course we would be stuck on the highest floor.” That was probably why they hadn’t heard any commotion outside from the rest of their peers, the brunette almost sure all of them were staying on the floor their first room had been in.

    The elevator finally arrives and Yelena switches then to pressing the ground floor button like a madwoman. The nerves on Kate’s stomach stop being so important when she notices the worry on the older girl’s stance. “Hey, Yel. It’s okay, we’re not that late anyway. We’ll just say we got caught up changing rooms. I’ll handle Fury, alright? You can scream at our asshole friends.”

    It’s not hard to imagine why the blonde would be apprehensive about backlash from authoritative figures, so the way she quite literally melts into her side when the younger girl passes an arm around her shoulders has Kate taking in a thankful breath at having said the right thing for a change.

    Turns out that they are not the last ones to make it to the hotel foyer, Yelena quickly seeing some of their classmates huddled around their Theory of Law professor. Their friends are nowhere in sight though, so the younger girl shoots them a quick message on their group chat.

    are we the only ones who listened about meeting downstairs at 12?

    “I can’t believe we make it almost twenty minutes late and still we’re the first ones down,” she snickers as Yelena rolls her eyes good-naturedly, positioning herself close to the small circle of students. But then her phone dings signaling a new message and the situation loses its humor.

    Little bird did you bother to read what Peter wrote like, right now?

    We were there almost half an hour ago. Fury decided to split to make things quicker.

    She barely has time to claim the blonde’s attention by shaking her shoulder and practically shoving her phone in her face when Banner speaks up, “Ah, I see the last remainings of our tardy group have made it,” he says merrily, eying her and Yelena with a cheerful smile on his face, “Now, to make our visits more agile we’ve decided to separate you guys into two groups, so welcome to group numero dos!”

    A few forced chuckles fill the room as Bruce Banner claps his hands excitedly and begins to brief them on their itinerary for the day. “I can’t believe they didn’t wait for us,” Kate whispers right next to the Russian’s ear without taking her eyes off the professor, a forced smile on her face to make sure she doesn’t draw attention to herself. She quirks an eyebrow at the shiver that runs through Yelena, but decides not to analyze things further. That’s what got them into this mess, anyway.

    “Come on, Kate Bishop. I’m not that bad of a company,” the blonde tilts her head slightly to the side, her temple colliding with Kate’s shoulders briefly before moving away. She doesn’t turn to look at her, but the younger girl sees the pout anyway.

    Digging her elbow softly on her side, Kate twists her fingers at the bottom of Yelena’s black turtleneck and tugs, “Being a drama queen doesn’t fit you, Yel. Of course I wasn’t talking about you, I meant our traitorous friends for leaving us with them,” she mutters again as she looks around the other students. It seems almost impossible that she knows a couple of these people’s names, tops.

    “Who’s the drama queen now?” Yelena laughs lowly, the sound reverberating on her chest, “It’s only for the visits, it can’t be that bad. They can’t be that bad.” She nods her head as if trying to convince herself as much as Kate, the quick, unsure look she casts around their peers being proof enough that she has her own reservations.

    They silently resolve to stick to each other, following close behind their professor as Banner continues to babble incessantly about the perks of visiting Washington this close to spring. The brunette has a hard time digesting the information though, the cold wind slapping at her cheeks making it difficult to remember they are indeed in March.

    During the subway ride downtown Kate actually makes a short conversation with two of their classmates that manage to grab a seat right in front of Yelena and her. America and Kamala turn out to be rather nice in a very straightforward, borderline rude kind of way that has them both chuckling at the jabs the two girls take at each other. Yelena keeps mostly quiet and lets her handle the talking, but the small smile on her lips lets the younger girl know she agrees with her verdict of them.

    As they climb up the stairs to the International Monetary Fund another girl - Sonya, if this fucking wind didn’t mess with Kate’s hearing - joins the four of them as they wait for their stuff to go through the security belt. This one gets Yelena talking, both of them being Russian providing a comfortable middle ground for their conversation. Still, the blonde keeps her hand carefully wrapped around Kate’s wrist from where she almost lost her earlier (not that anyone could blame her really, that tree was absolutely beautiful and its flowers deserved a picture), the warmth of her skin and the pressure of her hold a nice reminder that she’s there.

    Their new acquaintances move forward without them when the guard decides Yelena’s small penknife keychain is an issue, Kate promising the other girls they’ll find them later as she waits endlessly amused on the other side for the blonde to be let in. Banner is actually cool and offers to keep it on the bag he’s dropping off at the cloakroom, and so the older girl passes through the metal detector grumbling and pouting and the younger girl has to bite her lip to keep from laughing out loud.

    Right as she gets next to her Yelena mutters a warning “Not a word,” with a finger pointed accusingly at Kate that she immediately moves to ram at her ribs when the brunette mimicks zipping up her lips. Giggling, she passes an arm around the older girl’s shoulders and walks them to the last row of empty seats.

    The room they’ve been given looks kind of like an auditorium, the floor lowering progressively towards the front where two very elegant women are waiting to begin their presentation. As they sit down they see the other three girls talking near the front row, Sonya and America catching their eye and smiling at them.

    “See, so much whining and you’ve already made friends,” her tone is mocking and Kate knows Yelena is trying to goad her, but there’s a warmth underneath that betrays her intentions. Her eyes sparkle with mischief but also with awe, and the small curve on the corner of her mouth distracts the younger girl long enough for the Russian to squint her eyes at her in wonder.

    Clearing her throat, Kate throws her hair obnoxiously over her shoulder and smirks, “Well, that’s only because I’m a very friendly person, it would go against my nature not to try and make small talk.” When she gets the expected reaction from the blonde (a snort and a roll of her eyes) she drops her act and smiles softer, her right arm resting across the back of Yelena’s chair, “But yeah, they’re actually nice. Not like - our friends kind of nice, but cool. Also, Sonya is top model material. I don’t understand how we’ve never caught sight of her before, honestly.”

    She feels more than sees the way Yelena’s shoulders tense, the groan that slips past the blonde’s lips only serving to confirm her suspicions. “Ugh, please - don’t. I have way more than enough with Danvers,” and Kate guesses it was meant as a joke but the apprehension in Yelena’s voice shines through, the older girl visibly bristling at the mere idea of Kate thinking about the other Russian like that.

    There are probably a thousand things she could say right now, all of them perfectly fine, friendly answers that would keep the fragile balance they have right now. What comes out of her mouth instead is, “You’re stupid.” Of course, at the sharp glare Yelena throws her way, she elaborates, “Come on, Yel. I have barely registered anyone else but you since that first day in class. I only noticed Sonya existed today, so I’d say you’ve got nothing to worry about.” And that is a loaded statement if there ever was one.

    By now Kate’s become rather comfortable acknowledging her feelings for the blonde, but one thing is accepting them to yourself and another is voicing them into a reality. She knows both Yelena and her know it’s true, but she’s also aware that a lot of other things are also a fact - her thing with Carol being amongst them. Yelena could call her out on it if she wanted - has done so before - but right now the older girl looks almost speechless at the younger girl’s confession, a small, bashful smile growing on her lips as she kisses her teeth and bites the inside of her cheek.

    “Okay,” is the only answer Kate receives, but a hand falls gently on her knee, palm up, and she doesn’t hesitate to rest her own against Yelena’s and close her fingers around it.

    The voice of those classy women fills the room for about an hour and for a brief moment Kate really didn’t think she’d have it in her to remain awake. They end their presentation with a promise to stick around to answer any questions regarding internships at the organization for those interested, and then thankfully they are free.

    Professor Banner had told them before they’d have some free time between this visit and the next to grab something to eat, so with a promise to meet in a couple hours in the same subway station they got here from, he lets them go. Bucky had texted them to let them know they were in fact on the other end of the city, their group having gone to where they were meant to visit next, so they all resign themselves to seeing each other at dinner.

    “Can we go back to where the pretty trees were? I wanna take more pictures,” she knows she’s playing dirty with the puppy eyes and the soft pleading voice, but Kate really needs to get a good shot of those flowers. If she happens to get the blonde in the middle she won’t complain, either.

    A sharp whistle startles them before Yelena gets to answer - which is a shame because Kate knows she was going to say yes -, the two of them turning around to see Sonya signaling at them to wait, Kamala and America walking next to the raven-haired girl and waving as they approach them. “Hey, we were going to grab something to eat with some of the others. You guys coming with?” America phrases it like a question, but she actually pushes at Kate’s shoulder and begins to drag her in the opposite direction she and Yelena were meant to go.

    Looking helplessly over her shoulder at the older girl, she manages to send a rueful pout when she confirms the blonde is walking behind her. Her dramatic frown contorts into squinting eyes and tilted-down lips at the way Sonya links her arm through Yelena’s as she chatters animatedly in Russian. Yelena doesn’t look uncomfortable though, and that’s almost as surprising as this burst of confidence from their new friend to get so touchy-feely with the other girl is.

    There’s something in the way they are speaking so huddled together that bothers Kate, but she guesses it has a lot to do with her not understanding shit about whatever they are talking about. She stomps this weird bewilderment down and forces herself to be glad for the blonde - she knows Yelena feels a bit out of her deep when faced with new people so if she gets a new friend out of this Kate’s going to be proud, never mind that something about particular friend rubs the brunette the wrong way.

    Some of the others ends up being practically the whole lot of group number two. They settle for a cute sandwich parole near Capitol Hill, and the park where they sit down to eat isn’t as pretty as the one they passed in the morning but still Kate won’t complain too much. Kamala and America are almost as talkative as she is (which is a lot) so she barely notices as time slips by and their free period comes to an end.

    What she notices though is the way Sonya won’t leave Yelena’s side, the two of them still deep in a conversation that, according to the way they both keep laughing unnecessarily loud, has to be very fun. Every time Kate happens to look over to the bench where the Russians sit (which is not that often, mind you) there’s a big grin on both their faces. She even catches Yelena holding Sonya’s arm as she throws her head back in a fit of hysterics, the knot on her stomach unpleasantly tightening whenever she watches them.

    “Russians, am I right?” Kamala chuckles when she catches the brunette’s eyes wandering again, “I’m convinced that’s not a real language. They all just make random noises and pretend to understand each other to throw the rest of us off.” Kate forces a snort and a smile that feels plastic on her face, bitterly thinking that if that’s the case then Yelena is definitely not catching the hint that the other girl is making noises with the clear intention to flirt with her.

    The sun is shining low in the sky by the time they all get moving towards their next destination. Kate has honestly no idea where they are supposed to go, but instead of walking after the group that clearly seems to have a direction in mind, she waits back for Yelena to catch up with her. If they’re getting lost, they may as well do it as partners.

    Sonya stays stubbornly by the blonde’s side, although the sting of it is somewhat soothed when Yelena hooks her fingers around Kate’s wrist and pushes both their hands inside the pocket of her jacket. “I told you to bring gloves,” the older girl chides her when she catches the brunette rubbing her hands together and blowing gently onto them. Kate just stammers a pettish “But it’s March already,” that doesn’t hold any force behind it, accompanied with, “And what happened to the Russian blood and not feeling the cold, anyway?”

    A tight squeeze of the hand held prisoner inside her pocket and a delighted, “I’m not cold, malyshka. But you are not Russian,” are the only answer the younger girl gets, but it’s way more than enough to shut her up. Yelena resumes her chattering with her new friend and Kate tries to keep up with America and Kamala’s banter, the feeling of a thumb caressing her knuckles warming her inside out.

    The rest of their day drags out so much Kate can’t help but throw herself face first on their bed the second they step inside the room. She lets out an appreciative moan that doesn’t go unnoticed by her roomie, “Don’t get too comfortable, Kate Bishop. We have to be downstairs for dinner in less than thirty minutes. We’re not going to be late again.” But Yelena, ever the mixed-signals queen, accompanies her reminder by laying down right next to the younger girl, twisting her back around until an audible pop echoes across the walls.

    “That had to hurt,” the brunette mumbles, face still partly burrowed on the pillow she’s snatched from Yelena’s side of the bed. The mattress dips as her bedmate continues to move around, the older girl exhaling loudly through her nose. Kate realizes she probably hasn’t heard her and so she tries again, “Do you want me to give you a rub?”

    Heat crawls as her chest and neck the second she registers what her unreliable brain has decided to sputter, but she figures she might as well own it. Turning her head around she’s met with a curious pair of green eyes, “What? I was on the archery team in college. I know my way around back pains.” Kate breathes that out as casually as she can and attempts to keep her heart somewhere inside her chest cavity.

    That turns out to be a bit tricky when the Russian only nods and shrugs an okay as she scoots to sit down near the edge of the bed, back turned to Kate. She threads her fingers through her hair to tie it into a high ponytail and then proceeds to take off her sweater.

    It shouldn’t be a big deal like, at all. First, because she’s wearing a top underneath. Second, because Kate has actually seen her with way less clothing. Third, because they’re friends. Then Yelena takes off her undershirt too and okay, point one gets thrown off the window. She probably shouldn’t think about number two either, because it kind of invalidates her third one, but then again -

    “Kate?”

    Her name sounds different whenever it comes from Yelena. At first, she thought it had something to do with the girl’s accent, or maybe with how scarcely she uses only her first name. Now though, Kate thinks it sounds different because when the blonde calls her it sounds like she knows her.

    Shaking her head to get rid of those thoughts, the younger girl hurries into action when Yelena shifts self-consciously and looks at her as if she had overstepped some boundary. Kate is so eager to reassure her that she completely forgets to warm her hands first, her cold skin touching Yelena’s heated one and causing the Russian to inhale sharply. She manages to stutter an apology at Yelena’s “Cold!” whine, focusing instead on the goosebumps that erupt all across the blonde’s shoulderblades.

    Slowly but surely Yelena relaxes under her touch, her upper body growing heavy and forcing Kate to apply more pressure with her hands only to keep her standing. As she kneads a particularly sore spot on her spine the older girl lets out a low groan that makes Kate’s finger falter slightly.

    Something hot and heady begins to brew on her lower belly, and even though she resumes her movements before the blonde can notice her hesitation Kate knows her mind is going to reel now, replaying that sound over and over again. She grows hyperaware of everything Yelena.

    She notices the way her elbows rest on top of Kate’s thighs as the Russian leans against her, she catches a strong drift of her perfume, she feels the softness of her skin under her palms, she grows aware of Yelena’s faintly labored breath.

    Later Kate will think hard about what drove her to do it and probably get an answer she’ll rather ignore, but right now she heeds her instinct and places a timid kiss on the juncture of Yelena’s neck and shoulder. Her fingers lay still on the blonde’s lower back, and as she moves softly as to not jostle the other girl she sees Yelena has her eyes tightly closed.

    Then time restarts again and the older girl’s phone buzzes and their moment breaks. Kate clears her throat awkwardly and moves back to give Yelena space, the older girl throwing her clothes back on rapidly as she reaches for the noisy device. A grim look washes across her face and the brunette doesn’t even have to ask before Yelena turns her phone to her, “My mama,” she says simply, hitting the decline button without a second thought.

    Reality comes crashing back in full force and Kate can’t help but ask, “How are things with them, Yel?” She tries not to sound invasive, her question phrased as an invitation rather than a demand. Yelena knows because she only murmurs, “Rough. Not talking much these days,”

    and leaves it at that.

    Kate resents now more than ever the fact that they can’t stay barreled inside this room for a bit longer, but there’s a restlessness to her companion that indicates she’s actually eager to move. She grabs both their jackets again as Yelena fumbles with their key, opening the door for the older girl and bringing her body close with an arm around her shoulders. She squeezes her arm and hopes the other girl gets her message.

    I’m still here.

    Seeing as tomorrow they still have visits scheduled early in the morning, it had been a common agreement to make a quick deal of today’s dinner. It so happens that this trip has brought into life a group chat with all their classmates, so Peter and Eddie (being the self- designated public relations in their circle) had been the ones to inform them of their plans.

    There’s a group of roughly twenty-something people at the hotel’s entrance when they make their way out of the elevator, M.J. being the first one of their friends to spot them. “Look who’s managed to make it on time now,” she drawls out, a shit-eating grin splayed smugly on her lips. Bucky snorts and Eddie and Peter laugh out loud while Yelena lets out a sardonic ha ha and Kate fits in a hard shove to the younger girl’s shoulders before she flips all of them off.

    They continue to tease them mercilessly for actually fucking up the only thing they were instructed to do on their first day and landing themselves in a separate group, but Kate would be lying if she said she hadn’t missed this today. Their other classmates may be nice, but she adores her friends.

    Also, Yelena is still leaning gently against her side as they both endure the mockery they certainly have earned so, it feels a lot like teamwork.

    Introductions are made when America arrives with Sonya hot on her heels, Kate barely having time to ask about the other missing brunette as Kamala emerges from the crowd already gathered there to drag them all into a socializing spree.

    It’s not a surprise to anyone that M.J. and Kate’s companions for the day hit it off immediately, the four of them entering a circle of quips that’s sure to last for the whole night. She sees her boys talking with a couple more of their classmates and then her blood runs cold and scorchingly hot at the same time when she notices Sonya and Yelena chitchatting back together again.

    A low whistle makes her snap her head to the left, “Wow, dude. If looks could kill…”

    whispers M.J. close to her ear to make sure no one else hears her. She links her arm with Kate’s and follows their peers out of the door, “What did that pretty black-haired girl do to offend you so much?”

    Disgruntled, the older brunette hums and twists her head subtly back again. Sure enough, both Russians are walking side by side as Sonya shows Yelena something on her phone. “She didn’t do anything, it’s just a - feeling,” she grumbles as she struggles to put into words her perception of the other girl. She doesn’t know what, but there’s something off, of that she’s sure

    “Uh-hu, a feeling,” the younger girl parrots back to her, disbelief threading her tone, “I know of a word that describes that feeling down to the point, babe. If you ever wanna hear it please, don’t hesitate to ask.” There’s an infuriating knowing smile on M.J.’s face that only serves to exasperate Kate more, so she ignores her patronizing ways and tugs at the arm hooked around hers to make their steps faster.

    Dinner comes and goes and Kate barely enjoys the pizza she orders because there seems to be something tugging at her stomach the whole time. Peter looks at her worriedly when he sees almost half of her food still on her plate. “Are you feeling okay?” he asks rather distressed, and it would be funny if she knew the answer. She doesn’t feel ill exactly, but -

    “Yeah, just - I’m probably a bit weird from the flight still, my body’s not a fan of airplanes,” she lies through her teeth because really, she doesn’t have anything better to offer. Kate catches Yelena’s concerned stare from where she’s sitting a few seats to the right in front of her, nestled between Sonya and Bucky who - of course - also happens to fend for himself in Russian. The younger girl shoots a strained smile in the blonde’s way that feels way too forced before excusing herself to the bathroom.

    Closing the stall door with a heavy sigh, Kate lets her forehead rest against the cool wood and brings a hand up to massage her temples. Am I getting sick for real? It would just be my fucking luck to catch a stomach bug on a trip, she thinks angrily to herself as she tries to calm her agitation with deep, measured breaths.

    Noise comes and goes behind the door, someone leaving the stall to her right, a bolt struggling to twist, another person blowing dry their hands by the entrance. Kate struggles to concentrate on the commotion outside, the rumbling in her insides being louder still.

    With a defeated exhale she resolves to go back before someone decides to come searching for herself and she manages to embarrass herself further. She opens the door when her ears don’t catch any more rustling but her own breath, wanting to keep her little isolation bubble for a while longer.

    Her calculations are proven wrong when she sees her own reflection on the glass wall right next to another figure who’s washing their hands at the sink. Their eyes meet in the mirror and then it’s too late to ignore the other woman.

    Sonya smiles at her cordially, a soft hey and a nod in Kate’s direction when the younger girl pushes herself to stand on the faucet next to hers. Kate nods back politely and for a second she thinks that’s going to be the extent of their exchange, but seconds tick by and the other woman stays put. She dries her hands with paper towels, sets her purse on the wet marble - that has Kate scrunching her nose in distaste at the motion because really, who does that? - and begins to dig around looking for something.

    Waving victoriously a tube of lipstick up in the air, the Russian lets out a whooping aha! and smiles at her again before she busies herself with reapplying her makeup. Kate walks back to the stall to grab some toilet paper to pat her hands dry and then Sonya decides to speak, “She’s way too gorgeous, isn’t she? It seems impossible for someone to be that pretty.”

    It’s really a miracle that Kate happens to have her back turned on the other girl, otherwise the snarling grimace that blooms on her face would have been impossible to cover up. The same unsettling feeling roars back again at the pit of her stomach, her throat dry and a pulsing sensation behind her eyelids as she feels her hackles rise.

    There’s no need to ask to know who they’re talking about.

    “She is. Inside and out,” Kate shoots back easily, trying to ease the bite on her tone. It is a fair statement. True, too. So she convinces herself to settle this quickly and make a run for the door as fast as her feet will carry her.

    A murmur of acknowledgment resonates behind her, “Ah yes. She’s got the looks and the brains and the personality,” the raven-haired girl breathes with a disgustingly dreamy voice, “You’re a lucky one, Kate.” That last part lacks its previous airiness, her dark eyes sobering up when they find her blue ones. And yet, Sonya doesn’t move.

    Her jaw aches from how hard she’s working her teeth and biting her tongue. She knows the other woman is fishing for something, is waiting for her to either confirm or deny her suspicions, probably. She doesn’t want to give her the satisfaction of knowing there’s nothing between her and Yelena, but shame tickles at the back of her neck at the memory of a very serious conversation with an even more stern M.J. when she learned Pietro knew about the two of them.

    (“You can’t go around spilling people’s secrets, Kate!” — “I didn’t! I just didn’t deny it when he correctly guessed Yelena and I indeed were more than friends” — “Outing by omission is still wrong, Bishop.”)

    It had been a mistake she’d made enough already to know better by now. No matter how much she’d like to leave Sonya in the dark about their relationship, she owes Yelena this much. Shallowing thickly she forces a smile and wills her voice not to waver as she responds, “Oh, no. You must have been confused. We’re not together, her and I.”

    She had been expecting a fake gasp, a confused tilt of an eyebrow, a poorly contained grin. What she gets instead though is a curt nod and a bemused “I see,” that surprisingly isn’t a prelude to a deeper interrogatory.

    Or perhaps it would have been, had Yelena not chosen that exact same moment to almost throw the door out of its hinges with the force of her palm. The noise startles the three of them and Kate takes a step back, her heels colliding with the wall behind her. She feels as if she had been caught doing something she shouldn’t and she hates it.

    Both Sonya and her look at Yelena, waiting for the blonde to speak. Yelena’s eyes stay focused on Kate, “Sorry. You had been gone for a long time so I thought maybe you weren’t feeling well.” She gifts Sonya a fleeting smile when she tears her eyes from Kate’s, the younger girl noticing her perplexion at the scene she had just walked into.

    Decided to put an end to this already too long situation, she takes it on herself, “I am, actually. Not feeling well, that is,” she really should practice her lying because this stammering is getting ridiculous, “I think I’m just going to call it a night, I’m sure sleep will make it better. So - I’ll see you back in our room.”

    Maybe she could have kept that last bit for herself, okay. She’s still a competitive child at heart, what can she say.

    Concern is written all across her face when Yelena looks at her as if she had lost her mind, “No, no. I’m leaving with you. You’d probably end up getting lost on your own,” the blonde tries to make a joke out of it, to lighten up the tension that still cuts across the bathroom, but the apprehension in her voice makes Kate realize she’s worried she will push her away.

    That’s the only reason that stops the younger girl from telling her to stay and enjoy the rest of the night.

    (That and the very possible crestfallen look on Sonya’s face.)

    “Have a lovely night, ladies. I hope you feel better soon, Kate!” is the other Russian’s cheery reply. She jumps deftly from where she had seated herself during their brief encounter -

    which, again, who the fuck sits on the wet tiles of a random restroom - and walks briskly to the exit, giving them a short salute before leaving them alone.

    What the hell is this girl’s deal?

    Kate’s so caught up in her own thoughts she realizes she waves back when the older girl is no longer in sight. Yelena seems torn between wanting to be amused and actually being concerned, so she settles for a soft squeeze of the brunette’s arm and a gentle, “I’ll go pick up our things, give me a minute.” With that, she disappears behind the door and Kate has to close her eyes to keep from feeling dizzy.

    The walk back to the hotel is actually refreshing in the sense that she finally gets to stop thinking. There’s a cold breeze flushing their cheek, and the comforting sound of cars rushing past them, and their clasped hands are hidden back again inside the pocket of Yelena’s jacket. There’s a blissful quietness inside her head that feels unreal.

    Until they get to their room and Yelena decides to break it.

    “So,” the older girl drawls out the word, eyes skittering between Kate and literally everything else in the room, “I thought you liked Sonya?” She busies herself with the clothes she hadn’t gotten time to unpack earlier, keeps her back to the brunette, but Kate doesn’t need to see her to know she’s equally curious as she is tickled.

    When the only answer she receives from Kate is a nonchalant, grumpy grunt Yelena turns around, amusement slipping from her face as she studies the girl lying on the bed. “I’m sorry, do you want to go to sleep? I can finish this tomorrow, we can turn off the lights now,” she offers tentatively, still unsure about the extent of Kate’s unwellness.

    To say her uneasiness makes her feel like shit would be an understatement, so the younger girl hurries to sort herself into a seated position and smiles reassuringly, “No, no. I’m fine, I promise. My mind’s a bit fuzzy, that’s all.” It’s a lie, honestly, but it wipes the frown off Yelena’s face so she can deal with it.

    And then, because she just can’t keep things quiet and to herself, she blurts, “Plus, I’m allowed to change my mind, aren’t I?” and at the Russian’s confused squint she elaborates, “I mean - Sonya.” She’s conscious of the way she nearly spits the name, tries to contain it but fails miserably, so instead of digging herself deeper she just waves a hand around as if it explained anything and nods.

    Of course, Yelena doesn’t follow at all, “No, I mean - yes, of course, you can change your mind. It’s just - you were gushing about how pretty she was right this morning, and you two barely talked, so…” so it’s completely unjustified goes unsaid, but Kate hears it nonetheless.

    Turns out I wasn’t the only one gushing about how pretty someone was this morning, the younger girl thinks bitterly, recalling the bizarre encounter with the other Russian only a few minutes ago. It’s enough to bring back all the aggravation she has felt toward the woman at once.

    They could leave it at that. Yelena is eyeing her curiously but Kate knows she won’t push the subject if she chooses to divert it. It could be swept under the carpet and Kate could pretend any of it happened. Until tomorrow comes along and Sonya glues herself back to the blonde’s side and she has to navigate all these stupid feelings again, so -

    “I just didn’t like the way she was with you.” There, short and concise and truthful. Now it’s on Yelena to make sense of it, see if she has any more luck with that.

    Neatly folding a shirt and pushing it inside a drawer, the older girl doesn’t turn around when she asks, “What do you mean, the way she was with me?” She delivers it with such a carefully neutral expression Kate has trouble figuring if she’s really clueless or if she’s choosing to play dumb to coax a further explanation.

    There’s an alarm going off at the back of her head warning that she’s about to walk into something messy, but her disbelief at the other girl’s ignorance pushes her onwards anyway, “C’mon, Yelena. You know. She was all touchy and insistent and - and there all the time. She just rubs me the wrong way.”

    Her main clue to read Yelena has always been her eyes, so when the Russian turns around with a mildly wounded look on her face she knows she’s not playing thickhead, “Is it really so surprising for you that someone would actively seek out my company?” And it may not be the best way to phrase it but Kate knows she means it in a friendly way.

    Which is exactly why her heart shrinks in her chest at the idea of Yelena thinking she views her as someone incapable of making friends on her own.

    “No! No, no. God, Yel - no. That’s not what I meant at all,” she gasps frantically, “Of course people love to be around you, she’d have to be fucking stupid not to. I just think she liked it a little too much.”

    Hurt morphs into doubt which switches into curiosity that finally settles into incredulity.“Kate Bishop, are you jealous?” Yelena’s voice is an octave higher when she finally gathers her wits, and Kate has to bite her tongue because she thinks that’s probably the word M.J. promised to unveil to her earlier if she so much asked.

    Now she’s got two options: confirm or deny. The former implies an explanation she’s not sure she can put into words, the latter entails a lie she’s absolutely incapable to come up with.

    She must spend too long deliberating because Yelena ends up making the choice for her, “So you’re telling me I have to handle your girlfriend being ever-present around you - around us - but you’re going to, what? Be petty because another woman spoke to me?” Yelena looks downright aggravated with this new piece of information, but as she walks up to face the brunette Kate realizes there’s something else in her eyes. A challenge.

    There are a lot of truths to acknowledge in the older girl’s accusation. The one Kate settles for, the one that feels more important to clarify when she rises to her feet in a bolt of rage turns out to be, “We’re not dating.” She didn’t mean to spat it out like that, to sound so scandalized by the idea, but she’s lost the little self-restraint she had in her because -

    Because they’re both breathing heavily, their faces barely inches apart, and Kate realizes without an ounce of surprise as she digs her nails in her palms that she’s dying to kiss her.

    And for a short, fleeting breath Kate’s sure they’re going to. Yelena’s tongue pokes out to wet her lower lip devastatingly slow, the brunette’s eyes following every move. When blue eyes swipe back up she catches the older girl doing exactly the same, and so she takes a step forward, their bodies pushing closer still as their chests press together.

    But then Yelena exhales through her teeth and walks back. She looks dubious and tired all of the sudden. “Does Carol know that?” the blonde asks quietly, her voice softer and her glare nonexistent.

    Fair question. Kate wants to believe she does, but then she thinks back to how she’s spent this whole day avoiding answering the other blonde’s texts while Yelena was nearby. She feels like she’s cheating, somehow. On whom, she’s simply not sure.

    (Probably both.)

    The silence stretches across the room for far longer than it would be deemed comfortable, Yelena resting the back of her head against the closet door and Kate sitting precariously at the edge of their bed. Their eyes stay locked together and neither of them dares to move.

    In the end, Kate doesn’t answer and Yelena concedes to her the small mercy of not bringing back the issue that drove them to this moment, Sonya being but a distant memory on a way more difficult subject.

    If the hard set of Yelena’s shoulders or the thick tension that threads her quietness is any indication, Kate’s certain the blonde is harboring plenty of unpleasant feelings regarding her earlier semi-confession. They get ready for bed in silence and the Russian buries herself under the covers, her back stubbornly facing Kate.

    Still, as they lay side by side on their shared bed, Kate can’t help but be selfish again when short nails scrap the side of her palm, Yelena’s fingers searching blindly for hers. A peace offering. The younger girl takes her hand greedily, tightening her hold just in case the blonde was to change her mind.

    They don’t move closer nor do they shift away. They stay there, hands clasped between them, and as Kate drifts out to sleep it only takes her five deep, steady breaths to resolve herself to make this right.

    The blaring sound of Yelena’s alarm had startled the two of them into consciousness about ten minutes ago. That, of course, had implied that they had become aware of the…

    compromised position they were in. The blonde laid face down with her cheek resting against Kate’s forearm, her own arm thrown carelessly across the younger girl’s stomach. Their feet were touching and so were their knees where Yelena’s thigh sat heavily on top of the brunette’s.

    Kate felt warm all over.

    They must have shuffled in their sleep to end up that way, but it wasn’t the first time they’d found themselves in a similar position and frankly, it was way too comfy to break it up over technicalities like it maybe being a bit too intimate state for two supposed friends to be in.

    Plus, friends hugged each other all the time, right?

    At least that had been the younger girl’s reasoning. The Russian had hurried to scoot out of their bed with sleep still clinging to her eyelids, so she had clearly disagreed. Kate had innocently (read: stupidly) thought that perhaps a good rest would dim Yelena’s annoyance at her (rather absurd now that she’s had time to think about it cooly) display of jealousy.

    Obviously, it wasn’t the case but well, sometimes you win, sometimes you lose.

    Still, Kate had used the spare time she had to contemplate what she was to do next. She’d come up with a full list of all the things she has to tackle to smooth things over - a glaring example of it being her hey can we talk when I get back text to Carol that was sitting yet unread on her phone - but the girl in front of her is the priority out of all of them.

    An unimpressed chuckle raises goosebumps across her nearly numb arm and breaks Kate out of her stupor. “Why, you want to ditch?” Yelena asks with a raspy voice, hoarse from the lack of use that triggers a new round of shivers all across her skin.

    “Yes, that’s exactly what I want us to do.” No need to beat around the bush, she has given it a lot of thought and the first step to being rid of this tension between them is to get some alone time with the other girl. She’s sure no one will miss them much if they decide to skip a visit to some random institution.

    Yelena eyes her weary but doesn’t shut down her proposition right away, so the younger girl takes it as an open to press, “We could go to see the pretty trees from yesterday, take a walk around the city - we can even have some hot chocolate if you want!” She’s going to use every single trick on her sleeve to get the reluctance out of the blonde’s face.

    The promise of sweet treats and her infallible kicked-puppy eyes are enough to crack the other girl’s resistance, a slow smile spreading across Yelena’s face as she considers her proposition. “Okay, we can do that. But -“ she raises her voice as well as her index finger to point threatening at Kate, trying to maintain some of her precarious stern facade, “If Fury finds out you’re dealing with him. Also, you’re paying.”

    Grinning like an idiot, Kate nods her head as she utters a soft “Deal,” while her eyes track the blonde’s movements. Yelena ruffles around the wardrobe gathering her clothes and aims for the bathroom, but stops short of the doorway to look over her shoulder back at the girl still on the bed. She seems to hesitate for a second, but then, “Come on, Kate Bishop. You don’t want to keep me waiting for too long.” After that, she disappears behind the door, and the room gets quiet again.

    There are a lot of interpretations to that statement. Kate knows all of them are true.

    “I won’t,” she whispers softly, mostly to herself, and then gets ready to move.

    After making sure all of their classmates have already left in their corresponding groups (yes, she checks in with both Peter and Kamala to make sure her plan goes off without a hitch) they arm themselves with a pocket city map and take off in the direction Kate is almost sure she remembers those gorgeous blooming trees being.

    Almost being the keyword.

    “I’m sure we’ve walked in front of that bakery twice already,” Yelena drones exasperated as she follows after Kate, the younger girl squinting her eyes around as if she knew what she was looking for. She doesn’t, truly, but there’s just no way she’s admitting it to the Russian after she had adamantly refused to ask their hotel receptionist for directions.

    I’m my own Google Maps, she had puffed out proudly. Never before a sentence had aged so poorly so fast.

    Right as they walk past the same bakery for the third time and Kate’s about to accept defeat, she catches sight out of the corner of her eye of a telltale of a familiar pink a few streets down. She only squeaks a delighted noise out of her mouth and links eagerly her hand with Yelena’s, practically dragging the other girl behind.

    And then, after eleven wrong turns and a handful of complaints from her partner in crime, they finally find their way to the beautiful flowering trees of the Jefferson Memorial. “I told you I knew where we were going,” she smugly gestures with her hands around as if to prove her point as she skips a few steps ahead of the older girl.

    It speaks volumes of Yelena’s self-control that she only shoots her a callous look paired with an amused tilt of her eyebrow. She’s trying very hard not to laugh, but then Kate almost trips on a bench as she continues to walk backward, so the brunette can’t be too insulted at the snickering that ensues at her expense.

    There are rather a lot of people milling around considering it’s a mid-week day, but Kate supposes tourists never rest and so she takes out her own phone and begins to snap pictures of everything pretty her eyes see.

    (Later, as she revises her photos, she’ll realize most of them feature Yelena in some sort of way. It only further reinforces her point, really.)

    “Move a tad to the left… No! Wait - take a step back. Right there, now, twist a bit your left leg and stay still,” she instructs as she observes Yelena through the screen, the Russian’s antagonizing glare softened by the grin that’s threatening to bloom on her lips. She’s standing in front of a mass of cherry blossoms that bring out the sparkling gold in her emerald eyes. The revealing laugh lines that form around the corner of her mouth and the way her blond hair whips at her face make her look devastatingly attractive, and Kate spends an embarrassing amount of time just staring at the image before hitting the capture button.

    Perfume flares at Kate’s nostrils when Yelena drifts back to her side to curiously snoop at the picture, “You always get me looking nice,” she acknowledges, an appreciative smile drawing on her face as she darts her finger around to swipe at the device to see the rest of the shots.

    Making a disbelieving face, Kate narrows her eyes at her as if the other girl had suddenly gone blind, “No. You always look beautiful, I only happen to be paying attention.” She shrugs her shoulders mindlessly, but at the warm, slightly flustered glint on Yelena’s face, she realizes her words can be read with a deeper meaning. The younger girl doesn’t bother to elaborate though, because it applies either way - she’s always been drawn to Yelena, always aware of the beauty she holds.

    Just then a senior gentleman taps cautiously at the blonde’s shoulder, an analogic camera held shyly between his fingers as he asks them if they could please take a picture of his wife and him by the water. Sure enough, a short distance behind there’s a lovely looking lady dressed to the nines and smiling kindly at them.

    Yelena steps back and lets Kate do the picture-taking while she assures the elderly couple they’ve landed themselves the best photographer in the country. She smirks proudly as their new friends enthuse about their luck, and the amusing woman forces her husband to pose in a handful of different ways while he indulges her with a content smile. Kate is so focused on the feel of the blonde’s eyes gazing softly at her that she barely gets the opportunity to throw her own instructions to the pair.

    The results end up being up to the couple’s standard anyway, the two of them thanking the brunette purposefully as they coo at the enchanting (their words, not hers) pictures Kate has taken.

    “Come on, love. Go stand next to your girlfriend, we’ll take some of you two,” the older woman orders as she shoos Kate in the blonde’s direction. She stammers, unsure of what to tackle first - that they don’t really need their pictures taken because selfies work just fine, or that they are not girlfriends - but Yelena wraps her fingers around the cuff of her jacket and tugs, walking them to spot where the married couple was just a few seconds ago.

    Looking at her questioning, Kate can only smile flusteredly when the other girl winks and says, “I don’t think we really have a say in this with her. And I do want to have a picture with you,” before she sneaks an arm around the taller girl’s waist to bring her closer to her side. Kate throws her own arm over Yelena’s shoulder and hides her grin in golden tresses for a breath before she turns her face to the camera with a toothy smile.

    The older woman is as bossy with them as she was with her husband, but it does prove to be useful when they end up with almost fifteen pictures that look spectacular considering the trembling pulse of her husband (because of course, he’s the one actually snapping the shots).

    While they are revising the pictures together the sweet older man grins proudly at his achievement, “Now you girls have to come back when you’re our age to recreate these ones.

    It’s what we just did!” and he produces an ancient-looking piece of paper out of his pocket that indeed turns out to be a polaroid. It’s black and white, the edges of the thick material worn out by the pass of time, but the two silhouettes on the picture remain clear and intact.
