    Kate knows they’ve made it before the sign even appears on the road because Yelena turns off the music altogether, the only sound on the car being their almost synched breathing. The brunette’s familiar enough by now with the pattern of Yelena’s intakes of air to know she’s breathing raggedly and trying very, very hard not to show it.

    “Have you told them we’re dropping by today?” Kate asks tentatively into the silence, trying to gauge the other girl’s reaction to the question while simultaneously keeping her eyes on the road.

    There’s a sharply drawn breath that has Yelena clenching her jaw and twirling her thumbs together in her lap, “Yes. They said they would make themselves scarce, but… I don’t know.”

    “You don’t know if you believe them, or you don’t know if that’s better or worse?” Kate prods again, lowering her voice as she notices the way Yelena winces at her words.

    “Both,” the Russian answers tiredly, and then nods sharply to her right, “There, take that turn. When you see a house with blue window panes you can park.”

    The neighborhood is strikingly ordinary, and the realization has Kate shaking her head. She wasn’t exactly sure of what she was expecting, but somehow seeing a big, two-story house with blue windows and a large garden didn’t fit the narrative of a couple who had all but disowned their own children.

    Maybe Kate had stupidly been expecting a lair. A creepy, dark place. But that was exactly the problem, wasn’t it? That this doesn’t exclusively happen with villains in movies. This is something that can happen in any and every household, no matter how familiar it may seem.

    The copilot’s door slamming shut snaps Kate out of her inner thoughts, and as she hurries to unbuckle her seatbelt she remembers Natasha’s words: the worst thing won’t be the kicking or the screaming, it will be the unsettling quietness. The nothingness.

    As she slants against her car next to Yelena and takes a look around the empty street, Kate thinks she’s beginning to get it.

    “Papa said to close once we’re done and leave my copy of the key under the rug by the door,” Yelena musses out loud, her eyes never once leaving the building in front of them. She lets part of her weight fall onto Kate’s side though, so the younger girl doesn’t think twice to drop an arm across Yelena’s shoulders and squeeze her arm.

    It’s such a fucked up thing to say, to just ask her to drop her key before leaving once she’s packed all of her stuff because you don’t want to see her again.

    There are so many things Kate could say right now, thousands of things she’s dying to blurt out - that she knows are well deserved - but today it’s not about her, so she clears her throat and nods, “Sure thing. Ready when you are, babe.”

    Yelena takes one last long look at the house before turning around to face Kate, her eyes slightly red-rimmed and her smile wavery, and reaches up a hand to link her fingers through the ones resting against her shoulders, “Let’s do this.”

    Considering this has been Yelena’s home for as long as she’s lived here in the United States, it makes a lot of sense that the place is filled to the brim with knick-knacks of the blonde’s life. There are pictures and diplomas, medals and trophies, boxes full of drawings and school notebooks and diaries.

    The clothes are easy to pack, it takes them about an hour of folding item after item and throwing it inside one of the suitcases.

    The memories are another whole different thing altogether.

    Kate busies herself with getting things out of the drawers and shelves, leaving them on the table for Yelena to sort through them and decide what she wants to keep and what she prefers to leave behind. And once she’s done she resorts to assembling the boxes they had bought on their way here, fighting with the stubborn roll of duck tape and pretending not to notice how the Russian wiped her thumb under her eyes every once in a while.

    “Ha! Look, Katyushka. See? I told you I could teach you, I was an excellent rider even since I was little,” Yelena exclaims, breaking the heavy silence that had settled over them. She’s sporting a watery smirk when Kate turns around with an arched eyebrow and a question on her lips. Her question dies the second her eyes fall upon the picture Yelena is holding up proudly though, a loving grin of her own growing across her face.

    The image shows a cute little girl with braids and a mischievously delighted smile on her face sitting on top of a pony that definitely looks way much bigger than it probably was considering how small the kid appears next to it. The girl is sitting upright with her hands masterfully holding onto the reins, and were it not for the tall man standing behind her with his hands carefully hovering by her shoulders and back, one would actually think that the little kid was doing it all on her own.

    Deliberately choosing not to ask about the man who Kate clearly recognizes as Yelena’s father, she decides to focus her efforts on not swooning when she realizes the blonde is recalling the conversation they once had about Kate’s own dad promising to buy her a horse. Yelena had offered to take her horse riding sometime then, and Kate had taken it as a nice attempt to try and make her feel better.

    The fact that the older girl not only remembered their conversation but also her promise made something twist pleasingly on her belly.

    “So you were just a pro at everything ever since you were born then?” Kate asks teasingly when she regains her ability to talk, her eyes fitting from baby Yelena on the picture to the grown-up version standing right next to her.

    Same hair, same lips, same nose, same cheekbones, same gorgeous smile. She had always been effortlessly beautiful too, it seemed.

    “Yes,” the blonde replied easily, her own eyes fixed on her younger self while she tried to stiffen a self-satisfied grin, “In fact, you should know by now that I -“

    And then the front door opens and whatever teasing remark Yelena was meaning to say never makes it out of her throat. They both snap their heads sharply towards the foyer, and Kate’s glad to be the one holding up the picture frame because she’s positive it would be shattered on the floor beneath them had it been on Yelena’s hands.

    Kate’s familiar enough with Alexei Shostakov’s face by now to know who the bulky man hovering by the entrance is, but even if his features had been a complete mystery to her there would be no mistaking his identity. Yelena’s rigid posture and her angry, pained frown leave no room for questions.

    “We’re not done yet,” is what the Russian chooses as a greeting, and honestly? It’s way more than what Kate considers they could ever deserve.

    There’s discomfort written all across Alexei’s face as he crosses and uncrosses her arms and clears his throat. “I see,” he mumbles, and it’s plainly obvious he’s trying his best to look anywhere but towards them. His eyes finally fall upon Yelena, and then his whole demeanor changes. His shoulders sag and he takes a hand up to run his fingers through his beard. He’s nervous. “Can we talk?”

    There’s a beat of silence where Yelena holds her father’s gaze, but when seconds pass and the older girl doesn’t answer Kate subtly turns her body towards her, shielding her from her father’s view, and softly asks, “Do you want me to give you guys a moment?”

    She knows Yelena will understand what Kate’s trying to say, knows the blonde will see that there’s a very valid option to say no, and that Kate will stay rooted on the spot if that’s what she wishes.

    “Yes, please,” Alexei answers back from a few feet away, but Kate doesn’t move and she doesn’t turn around to face him because she’s not asking him. She doesn’t care about what he wants. In fact, if she gets her way Kate will leave this place without acknowledging the man’s presence altogether.

    Yelena looks fleetly over Kate’s shoulder back at her father before returning her warm green eyes back to the taller girl’s face, resolve shinning across her features, “Yes, Kate. I’ll be fine. It’ll be a minute.”

    With one last, warning look to Alexei, Kate takes a few of the boxes they’ve already taped close and aims for her car. The last thing she hears as she closes the door behind her is a curious, “So that’s Kate Bishop?” that has her debating whether she should smile or walk back and smack that man in the head.

    As Kate makes her way down the driveway she notices another car parked right next to hers. It’s a rather beaten-up pick-up truck, bright red with a white roof, and Kate’s so distracted trying to read the stickers that hang around the back that she only realizes there’s someone sitting inside the second she closes her own trunk.

    Stoic posture, sleek black hair, pretty fair skin, almond-shaped eyes with deep dark circles around them. If recognizing Alexei had been easy, knowing this woman was Yelena’s mother was instinctual. Melina looked as regal and imposing as she had in every single picture Kate had seen of her. Inaccessible.

    And she also looked terribly exhausted.

    There’s no time for Kate to decide if she should pretend she didn’t notice the other woman or if she should acknowledge her because the second their eyes meet Melina rolls down the window and asks, “Are you my daughter’s girlfriend?”

    Realistically, Kate had been expecting either fake politeness or a rude reproach. Out of every single thing the older woman could have said, that question hadn’t really been on Kate’s list.

    And it also made Kate waver because was she Yelena’s girlfriend? They had agreed to talk once the chaos around them settled, but Kate had a very specific idea of how that conversation was going to go down, so she steadily answers, “Yes, I am,” while forcing herself to meet Melina’s challenging stare. Her firm, slightly angry tone surprises them both, the dark-haired woman arching an eyebrow almost imperceptibly.

    Melina doesn’t speak again, only hums in poorly-hidden contempt and turns to look straight ahead. But she doesn’t roll her window back up, so Kate takes it as an opportunity to say what she’s been dying to scream to these people since she knew of their existence, “I think you’re making a damn huge mistake.”

    Her words don’t make the Russian face her, but Kate does catch the slight, curious look Yelena’s mom tries very hard to conceal, so she pushes on, “I also think you’re both ridiculously lucky to have such awesome, loving, forgiving daughters. You could still stop this.”

    For once in her life, Kate’s very mindful of what’s coming out of her mouth. She knows she’s threading a dangerous line between calling out something that’s wrong and meddling in matters that don’t directly concern her.

    Kate won’t say they could make it okay, or that their daughters would be willing to forgive them. She won’t because she doesn’t know if it’s true, couldn’t speak on Yelena’s behalf (and much less on Natasha’s), but she does feel like there would still be a chance to at least not severe their relationship completely. Then, as an afterthought, she blurts, “And, full disclosure here: I’m not saying this because I pity you, or because I feel bad about you losing them. I think you deserve to lose them after what you’ve done. But I also know that Yelena loves you, so what I think or what I want doesn’t matter. I’m saying this for her.”

    So much for not meddling, Kate scolds herself then, but refuses to show any kind of remorse for her words. She doesn’t regret them, so she won’t apologize for them.

    Something akin to recognition flashes on Melina’s eyes but it’s gone after a blink, and they don’t get to continue their (one-way) conversation any further because the main door opens and Alexei comes out with his hands stuffed in his pockets. He jumps into the driver’s seat with a fleeting glance towards Kate starting the engine right away, and Kate’s so sure that’s her cue to leave she almost misses the way Melina mumbles, “Goodbye, Miss Bishop,”

    before rolling up her window while her husband drives them away.

    It’s a bit embarrassing to admit that she needs a few seconds to break out of her stupor, but once she gets over her shock - both for being acknowledged at all and for Melina and Alexei immediately knowing about her - Kate pretty much makes a run for the door. She’s not sure what she was waiting to find because she has absolutely no idea of what could have transpired between father and daughter, but when she enters the living room and finds Yelena carefully packing her boots with a worryingly even look on her face Kate only asks, “Everything okay?” and patiently accepts Yelena’s hum and sharp nod as an answer.

    They work quietly through every single item that has to be packed, and while Kate struggles to fit the last boxes inside the back of her car she watches Yelena gently close the door behind her and kneel to hide the keys under the mat at the entrance.

    If it takes the blonde a few minutes to stand back up, Kate won’t mention it. Neither will she mention the redness on her nose nor the smudge of mascara under her left eye. She’s determined to keep her mouth shut and just get them out and away, but Kate doesn’t start the car right away either, waiting for a cue that they’re good to go.

    The car door slams shut and Yelena barely glances at her as she reaches out for the seatbelt and struggles to buckle it. It’s at the third exasperated huff that Kate’s resolve breaks, so she gently places her fingers over Yelena’s shaky hands and fastens it herself.

    “It’s okay,” she mumbles at Yelena’s helpless look, and really that’s all it takes. The older girl’s lip wobble and her nose wrinkles and she crumbles. Kate sees the tears even before Yelena has the chance to cover her face with her palms, and even if the Russian doesn’t make a damn sound the constant tremor of her shoulders would be telling enough.

    Yelena cries in the car while they’re parked right in front of the childhood house she’s been kicked out of - right after having faced her father while her mother refused to see her altogether - and Kate feels powerless because the only thing she can do is try to gather Yelena’s shaking body up in her arms and rub lovingly back and forth on her back while whispering whatever sweet-nothings come to mind.

    As Yelena’s nails dig at her back, Kate can’t help but think of her own mother, can’t help but compare Eleanor to Melina. Kate remembers how scared she was when she told her mom that she thought she liked girls just like she liked boys, how terrified she felt about letting her mother down, about Eleanor being disappointed in her. She thinks about the guilt and the shame and the fear, about how her mom made it all disappear when she wrapped her arms around her and told her she’d always love her no matter what.

    Kate thinks about how awfully Melina has failed her daughter.

    Then thinking about Eleanor brings the fact that her mother is actually very sick back to the front of her own mind. As Kate reflects on how scared she feels now that there’s a risk of losing her mother, she can’t begin to comprehend what Yelena must be feeling today, having lost hers when she’s right there, probably a walking distance away.

    The fact that Yelena has kept it together for so long is miraculous enough. Kate can’t help but marvel at the blonde’s strength while simultaneously despising that she’s ever had to need it, so she resorts to biting down her lip hard enough to draw blood and keeping her arms tightly around Yelena for as long as she can.

    Unsurprisingly, the Russian manages to calm herself down way faster than Kate ever could, wiping furiously at her cheeks with the back of her hands while breathing sharply through her nose. Kate places a hand resting softly on the other girl’s knee, drawing circles across the material of Yelena’s jeans, and waits.

    It only takes Yelena six breaths to say, “Get us out of here, malyshka,” and even though her voice is still scratchy from crying there’s unmistakable resolve pouring from her voice, so Kate only nods, reaches over to leave a loving kiss on her temple, and smiles when Yelena adds, “I think I’m ready to go home.”

    and now i'll stop the rambling and i'll leave you guys to it. i love you you're all truly the best xxx XXV - The End of Love Chapter Notes The End of Love - Florence + The Machine See the end of the chapter for more notes After what Kate had lovingly branded as the long weekend from hell, finals barely seemed like a challenge at all.

    At least that’s what she had told Yelena - and herself (mostly herself) - once they made it back to New Haven on the Thursday of what was meant to be their last week of classes. The blonde had only chuckled at her while they waited for Natasha and Wanda to come help them carry everything they’d brought back with them inside, so Kate had innocently taken it as an agreement.

    Then she repeated the same to Wanda after Natasha had taken her sister away demanding a complete narration of their trip, and the way the redhead had arched an eyebrow and shaken her head pityingly at her had made Kate second-guess herself.

    Of course. Of course Kate had been terribly wrong.

    When classes end and they’re granted a week and a half of grace before their exams start, Kate literally loses sight of Yelena. And she doesn’t mean a ‘we barely get to see each other a few minutes a day’ kind of thing. No, she means Yelena and Wanda barricade themselves inside their shared apartment and don’t allow anyone who’s remotely distracting inside.

    Kate, obviously, is on top of that list.

    It sort of works in the brunette’s favor though, because she’s then forced to focus herself. And once she does focus and begins to thread through her notes, she deeply regrets her words. She is deeply, terribly, amazingly fucked.

    “You’re one lucky bitch, you know?” M.J. hollers at her from a few feet away, earning herself so many dirty looks Kate’s sure they’re about to be kicked out of the library on the very first day they even try to study here. Next to her Kate hears Peter sigh and she doesn’t dare turn around to check if he’s looking at his girlfriend like a lovesick puppy or if he’s staring at her with dread.

    Kate would rather not know for sure if she’s going to get banned from ever setting foot in a library ever again. It’s funnier if some things keep their mystery.

    A stack of papers big enough to be considered a hazard to public safety lands itself right on top of Kate’s closed laptop. She tries very hard not to wince because she thinks she’s got an idea of what those papers could be and if they are, then M.J. is absolutely right and she’s fucking blessed.

    “There you go, girl. Every single page of my notes photocopied, just for you. I did them on black and white though, ‘cause you know, not all of us can be rich heiress to a security empire - ooof,” M.J. staggers a few feet back and nearly lands herself (and, by inertia, Kate) on the floor. The curly-haired girl saves the fall at the last minute by holding onto a nearby table (while knocking down a few books) and this time there’s no mistaking Peter’s grunt for anything other than pure exhaustion. There’s a sneaky hint of affection tough, so Kate’s not worried at all.

    “Oh my god, you’re literally saving my life right now,” Kate breathes into the other girl’s neck, trying to keep her voice as low as her excitement will allow. M.J. pats her back a couple of times in a very dad-like style and snorts, “Trust me, I know. I’ve seen the disgrace you dare to call notes,” and then, pushing softly at Kate’s shoulders she adds, “Now the way to pay me back is not strangling me to death, babe.”

    Someone clears their throat purposely next to them, and it’s all the cue Kate needs to know they’re very close to pushing their luck far too much. She also knows M.J. will probably snark back to anyone who - rightfully - asks them to shut the hell up, so the taller girl rushes to let go of her friend and drags her back to the table she was previously occupying with Peter.

    She’s so absorbed admiring the beauty of her new notes that Kate actually passes the opportunity to make gagging sounds at the kiss the two lovebirds share once M.J. finally sits down. Kate, however, doesn’t miss the chance of poking fun at the embarrassing heart-eyes her friend and savior makes at her boyfriend while Peter tries to explain to them the practically incomprehensible mechanisms of the International Criminal Court.

    The wiggly eyebrows and the knowing smirk Kate not-so-subtly sends M.J. earn her a kick on the shin that has her groaning louder than it’s definitely allowed.

    “Are either of you listening to a word I’m saying?” Peter asks exasperated, to what M.J.

    offendedly answers, “Of course! You were talking about… Rome?”

    That draws a snort out of Kate and a fond headshake from Peter, who mutters ruefully, “The Statute of Rome,” while M.J. ignores him in favor of targeting Kate. Pointing a finger accusatorially in Kate’s way, M.J. retorts, “You’re literally the last person on earth that should be making fun of me, Miss ‘I pout every single time my girlfriend tells me she doesn’t want to study with me because I’m a pain in the ass.”

    “Hey! The word she used was a nuisance. And I don’t pout!” Kate calls back in a high-

    pitched tone, her bottom lip jutting out treacherously as she continues, “And she’s not my girlfriend.”

    “Officially,” M.J. replies without missing a beat, closing her notebook and leaning her chin on top of her open palm, “And you do pout, babe, but it’s endearing so I’ve got to commend to Lena - that girl has a will of steel. God knows I would bend for that wobbly lip.”

    “A will the three of us clearly lack. Maybe I should try join her,” Peter whispers defeatedly as he too turns off his laptop, probably sensing they’re as well as done for the day now that his girlfriend has decided she wants to meddle with Kate’s love life.

    M.J. squints her eyes at him in a mockingly disapproving way and snorts, “Yeah, you wouldn’t last an hour with Wan and Lena. Those two are like, geniuses that probably read a page a second and translate everything into Russian just for the fun of it.”

    “Are you implying I’m not a genius?” Peter asks faking offense as he places a hand over his chest, “I’ll let you know I was doing fine until you two got here.”

    “Ah, but you love us,” Kate murmurs tauntingly at her friend, enjoying the way Peter rolls his eyes at her antics and M.J. snickers next to him, and as the two of them continue to bicker back and forth with each other she can’t help but feel absolutely grateful. She may be on the verge of failing most of her master’s classes, but she would redo this whole year all over again if it meant getting to know these people.

    “Now, I say you’ve done enough geniusing before Kate got here, and Katie has my notes so she’s as well as saved. I say we call it a day and head down to the cafeteria so lover-girl here can update us on her impromptu trip to New York and Ohio,” M.J. decides, already standing up and dragging her chair obnoxiously loud. Peter hastily throws his stuff inside his bag then, and Kate tilts her head to the side at his sudden compliance.

    Until, of course, out of the corner of her eye Kate catches sight of the librarian making her way down to their table with a clearly angry scowl on her face. Then she’s also gathering all her notes between her arms and scurrying after M.J. towards the door.

    “I’m so going to fail,” Kate whines for what’s probably the eleventh time in the last five minutes as her head hangs upside down from Yelena’s bed. She hits her forehead with the block of pages just for the hell of it - and maybe with a shred of hope that perhaps somehow the words will get stuck there, if not by memory at least by physical force.

    The same snickering that has been answering every single time Kate has stated her precarious academical future echoes again from somewhere over the headboard. “No one has ever failed with Myriad, Katyushka,” Yelena attempts to comfort her even though it’s transparent that the blonde is having a hard time trying not to laugh at Kate’s distress.

    “Watch me be the first,” Kate shoots back easily, letting M.J.’s notes fall to the ground as she moves her now free hand up and down her forehead, “I’ve beaten stranger odds, Yel, nothing’s really a challenge for me anymore.”

    “Except International Law, apparently,” the Russian points out in feigned innocence, but it only takes Kate a second (and an almost broken neck) to verify that Yelena is in fact covering her endlessly amused grin with the back of her hand. The crinkles around her green eyes and the scrunch of her nose give her away, and it takes a lot of resolve on Kate’s part to keep from breaking out in a smile herself.

    She doesn’t though, because if she’s going to fail and possibly have to resit an exam while the rest of her peers enjoy their summer then she’s going to make a drama about it, and not even a gorgeous girl is going to make her break character.

    Groaning in misery, Kate lets her body fall back to dangle from the bed again and focuses on the feeling of blood rushing down to her head. Here’s to hoping it either makes her brain catch up faster, or collapse altogether.

    Whatever comes first works for her at this point, honestly.

    The mattress sinks and rustles as Yelena shuffles to lie next to her, her blonde hair tips falling on top of Kate’s brown locks. The Russian snatches one of Kate’s hands away from her face to link their fingers together, and when the younger girl reticently turns her head to the side she’s met with a fond smile.

    “It’s not funny,” Kate remarks again, mostly to remind herself that she should remain strong against Yelena’s tender look and remember she’s merely days away from facing her impending doom.

    “It’s not,” the older girl concedes, going as far as to draw a cross over her chest while she attempts to keep a straight face. Kate doesn’t point out the way Yelena is clearly sucking in the inside of her cheeks to keep from grinning, too busy enjoying the calming sensation of a comforting thumb running up and down her knuckles. “But you’re not going to fail, Kate Bishop, you’ve studied more in the last week than Wanda and I combined. Things are not coming to you right now because you need to sleep the knowledge off. Let your brain store every piece of information away,” Yelena softly nudges, squeezing the brunette’s hand encouragingly to further prove her point.

    “You really think so?” Kate asks sheepishly. Under any other circumstances, Kate wouldn’t consider herself someone who doubts their own capacities. In fact, her tendency to overestimate her abilities has more often than not landed her in hazardous situations, but there’s something about academic life that makes her second-guess herself every single step of the way.

    Maybe it’s a bit stupid to hang on so much to some (probably - absolutely, definitely - biased) third-party opinion, but if that opinion comes from someone as brilliant as Yelena then Kate is not above letting it console her for a little while.

    Moving to sit upright and tugging at Kate’s hand to bring the brunette up with her, Yelena only smiles patiently again before cupping Kate’s cheek to draw her into a kiss. The Russian goes as far as to allow Kate to drag her teeth down Yelena’s bottom lip without retaliation (not that Kate doesn’t enjoy what said retaliation entails) to grant the younger girl a way to force silence into her mind. And then, after what feels like seconds but is probably more than a handful of minutes, Yelena pulls back gently to mumble, “Of course I do,” while keeping their foreheads resting together.

    Kate basks into the reassurance that washes over her body then, into the quietness around them and the feeling of Yelena’s fingers stroking her cheek and her hand clutching at her waist. She closes her eyes and focuses on matching Yelena’s inhales and exhales, letting the familiar motion calm the remaining bits of her uncertainty.

    They end up laying down with their legs tangled and their arms around each other, Kate’s eyes blissfully closed as Yelena runs her fingers through her hair. Kate’s on the brink of sleep when she mumbles, “You’re the best for believing in me,” not even ashamed of how awfully corny those words sound out loud.

    If M.J. could hear her now she wouldn’t stop gagging out loud for as long as she lived.

    The chest under her cheek rumbles with silent laughter as deft fingers tug at her soft curls playfully before they resume their soothing motion. For a second Kate’s almost sure that’s going to be Yelena’s answer altogether, but after a beat, “Always, Katyushka,” resonates sweetly, sincerely, onto the brunette’s hair.

    And then mischief returns to Yelena’s raspy voice, “Besides, you’re studying M.J.’s notes. Had they been yours and then maybe I wouldn’t have been such a believer.”

    “You’re such a bitch!” Kate startled laughs as she pushes herself off Yelena’s chest to unleash a tickle attack onto the blonde, grinning at Yelena’s squeals and pleas of forgiveness while the Russian squirms under her fingertips.

    Neither of them gets any studying done after that but frankly, Kate’s absolutely willing to sacrifice a whole summer studying the Neorealistic theory of International Relations if she gets to keep this moment engrained in her brain forever.

    Finals come and go and by some sort of divine intervention, Kate survives them. And not only does she survive them, she totally aces them. Or, you know, she thinks she does well enough to actually be sure she’s passed all of them, some even with high marks.

    “You’re welcome,” M.J. salutes haughtily when Kate says as much, giggling like a little kid. Kate only pushes at her shoulder before dragging her in for a long, obnoxious hug that has the shorter girl struggling to squirm away.

    “Thank you, M.J. If not for you, I would have had to listen to her whine and complain about her terribly lacking notes forever,” Yelena calls from the seat next to Kate, leaning over the table they’re all sharing to exchange an amused look with their friend. M.J. raises her beer and clinks it with Yelena’s cup of wine while winking complicitly at the blonde, and at Kate’s rebuttal that she does not whine, Yelena simply states, “Yes you do, malyshka. You whine.

    And pout.”

    “See! I told you, you’re a pouter!” M.J. exclaims elatedly, hitting her palms on the table excitedly as Yelena snorts and Peter tries his best to hide a grin behind his drink. The rest of their friends are mercifully not there yet, so Kate tries to count her wins as she groans.

    “I’m not enjoying this alliance against me,” she mumbles pitifully, biting the inside of her cheek to keep from smiling and spoiling her dramatic act. Truth is, she kind of loves the fact that Yelena seems to be way more comfortable around all of their friends now.

    It probably has everything to do with the fact that a huge weight has been lifted off her shoulders now that the blonde doesn’t feel like she has to keep hiding who she is anymore.

    Obviously, it doesn’t automatically mean that Yelena is fine and ready to openly discuss every single aspect of her life, but once again Kate can’t help but feel like the luckiest woman on earth because she’s absolutely certain that neither Peter nor M.J. (or any of their other friends, really) would ever bring anything up on their own accord. And Yelena must feel it, too, because there’s no mistaking the comfortable way she speaks with them while leaning subtly against Kate’s side without a second thought.

    Kate would take a lifetime of teasing if it meant seeing her like this forever.

    “I don’t know what’s this alliance we’re talking about, but if its goal is to annoy Katie then I want in,” a voice calls from behind Kate, the empty chair next to her sliding back as Pietro slumps down draping an arm around the brunette’s shoulders. Kate moans and drops her forehead to the open palm of her hand as M.J. fist-bumps the Sokovian from across the table and Peter rises his beer and nods at him.

    Yelena barks out an amused laugh while she pats Kate’s back sympathetically, the younger girl shooting her a betrayed look while Yelena tries to stifle a grin. “This is unfair. When’s Eddie coming? I need someone on my side,” Kate grunts grumpily, making a show to look over to the door in hopes of magically materializing their other friend with the sheer power of her mind.

    “Cass said they’d drop by after picking up Bucky,” M.J. replies swiftly, Yelena immediately piping in with a furrowed brow, “Wait, Wanda told me she and Natasha were going to pick him up before coming here.”

    That gets M.J. to pull out her phone to scroll through her texts, but her mission is cut short when Bucky himself walks up to their table with a couple of beers precariously balanced on one hand while the other carries a jacket and a bike’s helmet. He pulls out a chair with his foot and sits down carefully, setting everything on their table before looking up at them and offering a confused smile. “What’s up?” he innocently asks right as Sam appears through the door.

    It doesn’t take a lot to figure out that they’ve driven here together on Sam’s bike, and Pietro snorts when Bucky’s confusion deepens as his phone begins ringing, Eddie’s name flashing on the screen.

    Disbelief and mirth shine in M.J.’s voice when she questions, “Dude, did you really arrange a ride with three different people and forgot all about it?” and Bucky’s face shifts from bewilderment to realization so quick even Sam, who probably has no idea of what’s going on, huffs out a laugh.

    “Pick up, I’ll let Wanda know you’re an idiot,” Yelena resignedly instructs Bucky, shaking her head at her friend while searching her bag for her phone. Kate slides hers across the table in a swift motion, the blonde smiling gratefully at her and dropping a quick kiss on her cheek before calling the redhead.

    “Alright Katie, whenever you’re done being whipped you feel like taking it up on the darts?” Pietro asks unimpressed, poking the brunette’s shoulders and standing up to head towards the bar. Kate gapes after him for a beat processing his words and immediately shout-whispering, “Hey! I’m not whipped!” as she trips over herself to follow after him.

    The second a handful of darts finds its way into her hand, Kate realizes that had been Pietro’s intention all along: to goad her into leaving the table and join him for a game. She can’t even bring herself to stop grinning when she sees her friend’s self-satisfied smile though, and after a couple of rounds she even concedes, “I may regret saying this out loud, but I’m going to miss you.” And then, as if suddenly registering how true that statement is, Kate hastily adds, “You better not disappear on me now that classes are ending.”

    She had tried for a lighthearted tone, but there’s an eagerness there that the brunette’s sure Pietro definitely catches on. He must, because he turns around and with his most serious face he simply states, “Come on, Katie. You’re dating my sister-in-law’s sister, that practically makes us blood-related. That means you’re stuck with me forever.”

    “Oh, no,” Kate whispers in fake horror, “I’m already regretting this. Stop. I want to take it back.”

    But of course that only further fuels Pietro on, “Come on Katie Kate, picture it! Birthdays and Christmases and Thanksgivings. And then weddings and baptisms and -“

    “Shut up, oh god,” she moans out loud, mortified for real this time, “Will you cut it? No one’s getting married. We’re not even dating,” Kate grits the last bit between her teeth, her eyes frantically looking around to make sure no one’s paying attention to them. The rest of their friends are chatting animatedly at their table, Yelena somehow sensing Kate’s gaze on her and twisting around to send her a soft smile.

    Smiling back is an instinctual act, one that Pietro doesn’t let go unnoticed. “Please, you two have been dating even before you guys were really dating -“

    “That actually makes no sense like, at all,” Kate cuts him, crossing her arms over her chest and arching an eyebrow up at him.

    “- And besides, I wasn’t talking about your wedding,” he finishes, grinning mischievously and enjoying the way Kate squirms under his knowing stare, “It’s quiet telling you thought I was though, I think it sums up pretty much everything there is to say about the subject. It also highlights the fact that you should stop being a wimp and ask the girl out already so all of us can be put out of the misery of having to watch you two dance around each other -“

    “Wait, wait, wait,” Kate interrupts him again, waving her hand dismissively and paying no mind to the way he grouchily mumbles rude, “What do you mean you weren’t talking about Yel and I? Who were you talking about?”

    Honestly, if given a few more seconds to mull over it, Kate wouldn’t have needed any extra help to figure it out on her own. It just so happens to be that Natasha and Wanda walk through the door right then - hand in hand and so disgustingly in love that Kate inwardly winces at the idea of having that same look on her face when being around Yelena - and Pietro’s startled gaze shifts to panic once he realizes what he’s let slip.

    “Kate,” Pietro begins, a pleading tone in her voice, right as a soft hand falls on the small of her back and huskier voice calls, “Katyushka. Maximoff.” Yelena falls into the open stool right next to them then, handing Kate a drink as her eyes dance between them, and the brunette has to bite the inside of her cheek to keep from laughing out loud at the Sokovian’s horrified stare.

    “What’s gotten into him?” Yelena stage-whispers to Kate, her green orbs never leaving Pietro’s gaping mouth, but right as Kate debates about how to defuse this situation, Pietro breaks, “I may have mentioned the secret.”

    It’s then that Kate understands he isn’t nervous about Yelena knowing - or at least, that’s not his main concern.

    “Oh my god, is Natasha going to propose?” Kate can’t help but blurt out then, probably louder than she should but still lowly enough for her words to get drowned by the pub’s noise. Still, both Pietro and Yelena shush her hurriedly, the Russian clamping a hand over Kate’s mouth while the silver-haired boy grabs her by the shoulders and desperately says, “Kate, if you care about my wellbeing at all you’ve got to keep this as the best secret you’ve ever kept, got it?”

    Chuckling, Yelena decides to add to Pietro’s panic and seriously states, “Yes, my sister won’t hesitate to gut you if she learns you’ve gone around spilling her secret. She learned a few things during our time back in Russia.”

    “See?!” Pietro screeches in a high-pitched voice, his worried frown morphing into a painfully awkward smile when they see Wanda and Natasha looking over at them. The three of them wave at the two redheads then, Yelena probably being the only one who manages to look relatively normal. Wanda tilts her head to the side while Natasha squints her eyes at them suspiciously, but miraculously Cassie and Eddie join their table just in time to stop them from coming over.

    “Stop moving your hand like a string-puppet, Maximoff,” Yelena scolds Pietro then, reaching over to smack the hand Pietro still had waving up in the air with her own, “It will be alright. Kate won’t say anything. Right, malyshka?”

    “I’ll be a tomb, I swear. My lips are sealed,” Kate eagerly nods, mimicking a zipper motion over her mouth to further prove her point.

    Both Pietro and Yelena look at her rather unconvinced, and at Pietro’s desperate look Yelena just sighs, “Natasha is going to pop the question this weekend, Maximoff. There’s no need to panic, I’ll make sure Kate Bishop here doesn’t run her pretty mouth. Now if you’ll excuse me, I’ll be right back.” And just like that the blonde squeezes Kate’s waist affectionately and reaches up on her tippy toes to leave yet another kiss on the younger girl’s cheek before walking over to her sister.

    A flush spreads through Kate’s neck up to the tip of her ears then, both at Yelena’s words and at Pietro’s sudden change of demeanor. He grins triumphantly at Kate with a devilish glint in his eyes and easily states, “Yeah, I’m sure there are plenty of ways for her to keep your pretty mouth busy.”

    “Shut up,” Kate whines then, slapping her hands repeatedly over Pietro’s chest and trying to ignore the Sokovian’s self-satisfied laughter and the sneaky hand that curls around her shoulders and drags her body next to his in an uncomfortable half-sided hug.

    Their moment is (mercifully) cut short when Eddie, Cassie, and Peter join them by the bar and ask to be let in on the game. And even though Pietro keeps wiggling his eyebrows obnoxiously at her every time they exchange glances, Kate will admit (to herself only) that it’s one of the best nights they share.

    Admittedly, thinking about the prospect of everything that’s going to come for them surely has a lot to do with it. Her cheeks hurt from smiling so hard every time she sees Natasha eyeing Wanda lovingly even when the shorter redhead misses her shot and causes them to lose against Yelena and Kate on their round of darts, her chest constricting at the unadulterated joy she feels at knowing the two of them are so close to being even happier than they are now.

    (And maybe, just maybe, the fact that Yelena kisses her square on the mouth to celebrate their victory while all of their friends cheer and holler around them has something to do with it, too.)

    “You could move in with me.”

    “What?” Yelena startedly asks, turning her body carefully around while holding onto the ladder she’s on.

    They are the last ones left at the apartment, Kate having volunteered to stay behind and help the blonde clean up the remains of the party they had thrown to celebrate Natasha and Wanda’s engagement. Kate had very deliberately ignored Pietro’s knowing look when she’d told the rest of their friends they were free to go and that she’d be giving Yelena a hand at tidying everything up.

    (If she’d told university-newbie-Kate that she’d be freely offering herself to clean up after a party for a girl, she’s sure her younger self would have been way more mockingly than the older Maximoff twin).

    Emotions had been running high because the party had also served as a sort of farewell for their group. Eddie and Cassie were set to leave for Los Angeles in about two days, Bucky would be traveling to Chicago with Sam the day after, M.J. was heading to Washington on Monday, and Peter would follow close behind. Kate knew for certain they would see each other again, but she couldn’t help but feel terribly nostalgic at the thought of not seeing this makeshift little family practically every day.

    And even though Yelena had spent the evening playing the role of the perfect host like a dream, Kate had noticed the way Yelena’s knuckles turn whiter as the Russian strengthens her grasp around the metal nervously and the way her feet can’t seem to stay still. The older girl is as emotional as Kate herself is feeling, and for a second she wonders if perhaps this was not the best time to bring this up.

    To avoid losing her nerve, Kate focuses on the warmth of the green eyes looking questioningly down at her instead.

    “Yeah, I mean - We’re both going to be staying in New York for the summer. You’ve got your internship and I’ve got to be there to help mom with the company and stuff. I just thought it would make sense, you know? If we’re going to be living in the same city, we should just stick together. Besides, I’ve already got my apartment so you wouldn’t have to worry about paying rent, and that way we could see each other more easily because, you know, work will be chaotic and -“

    “Kate Bishop,” Yelena gently breathes out then, and when did she climb down the ladder and walk up to her? “Has someone ever told you you speak way too much?”

    Chuckling nervously but grinning at the familiar question, Kate barely blinks when Yelena’s fingers thread with hers and states, “Yeah, there’s been a few mentions here and there.”

    The two of them share a smile that leaves Yelena’s lips slowly. “Do you really want to live with me?” the blonde asks seriously, her eyes searching Kate’s for an answer the younger girl doesn’t have to think twice to give, “Of course I do, Yel. I think it would be awesome. But don’t feel like you have to say yes just because I want to -“

    “Yes,” Yelena stops her with a tug of their joint hands, closing the distance between them as their chests fall against each other, “Yes, I do want to live with you. It’s not that.”

    Raising a hand to carefully cup her cheek, Kate forces Yelena’s eyes to meet hers as she swipes a thumb across the Russian’s jawline, “What is it then? You know you can tell me everything.”

    “I’ve never done this,” Yelena states simply, drawing a sharp breath through her nose as her fingers tighten around Kate’s, “I don’t… What if it doesn’t work out?”

    And just like that, Kate knows they’re not just talking about living together anymore.

    There are a lot of things Kate could say now. She could brush Yelena’s worry off by saying that she’s confident in their chance at making it work. She could take the joking route and say that moving in together for a few months doesn’t equate to a marriage proposal. She could tell her that Kate hasn’t done this before, either - not like this, at least. Not so serious. Not with someone whom she cares about so much.

    It all seems like way too little or a bit too much though, so the brunette settles for something else altogether. The truth.

    “It could. Not work, I mean. It could happen. It has happened before to like, millions of thousands of people before us. I won’t promise you it will work out because I can’t. But I can promise you this - I would honestly like us to try,” Kate speaks softly, the quiver in her voice betraying the unmistakable earnestness she’s feeling.

    For three short, deep breaths Kate truly has no idea what Yelena’s answer will be. Honestly, Kate would take whatever the blonde decided to do - whether be it sharing a room or living on opposite sides of town. She’s certain Yelena knows this, too, so there’s not an ounce of nervousness in her body while she waits for the older girl to speak again.

    Not that Kate needs her to speak, though. There’s an impasse between Yelena biting the corner of her mouth and the blonde pushing their lips together, just a second when Kate catches a glimpse of something in those green eyes of hers that lets her know what the answer will be.

    She used to wonder what that glimmer was, way back. Now Kate feels like she could discern it anywhere.

    (Love. It’s definitely love.)

    When Kate walks through the door the last thing she’s waiting to find is Yelena sitting on the sofa with her knees drawn up to her chest and her face pale as if she’d seen a ghost.

    She had only been gone for around twenty minutes, the time it took Fabricio to make their delicious dinner and return to their apartment.

    Carefully leaving the pizza inside the oven to make sure Lucky wouldn’t find his way around the counter, Kate walks over to sit down next to the Russian. She doesn’t ask what has happened, only places a hand tenderly on top of Yelena’s knee and waits.

    Yelena doesn’t answer either. At least, not speaking. What she does instead is look toward Kate’s direction and then shift her gaze to her right, moving to reach around her body for something. Yelena offers Kate her phone then, unlocked, and once it lands on the brunette’s outstretched hand she sort of understands.

    There, on the way too bright screen, lies a written but unsent message that reads They’re playing Mr. and Mrs. Smith on Channel 4. The contact on top of the text is listed as Papa, so it doesn’t take a lot for Kate to add two plus two and get the full picture.

    “I didn’t even think about it,” Yelena hoarsely begins, her voice strained as if she had cried her weight in tears while Kate had been gone, “It’s his favorite movie. I saw and I only thought, I have to tell papa. I didn’t remember.”

    I didn’t remember I couldn’t speak to him anymore. I didn’t remember he didn’t want to speak to me ever again, is what Yelena doesn’t say even if Kate understands anyway.

    Her green eyes shine wetly under the TV light when Yelena finally looks at Kate, and it’s all the consent the younger girl needs to gather her girl up into her arms. “I’m so sorry, baby,” is the only thing she can whisper, depositing her words reverently in the skin of Yelena’s neck as the Russian curls her arms around Kate’s shoulders.

    The telltale feeling of helplessness that invades Kate whenever the topic of Yelena’s parents comes up begins to stir in her stomach. The truth is that the older girl has been dealing with this situation way better than Kate ever would. This is the first time since they left Ohio that she’s seen Yelena cry about it actually, and the brunette guesses that if anything the weird thing would be this not happening earlier on.

    That still doesn’t mean she has any damn clue about how to proceed because just like it happened back in Yelena’s hometown, there aren’t good enough words to say. Never will be, probably.

    “This feels even worse, you know?” Yelena mumbles into the crook of Kate’s neck, her eyelashes tickling the skin there, “The day I told them I was so overwhelmed I could barely focus on a sole emotion, so I barely registered the hurt. It’s been the after that’s been painful. I didn’t expect that.”

    There’s a tick about herself that Kate normally despises. Whenever someone is sharing something personal, she gets the urge to pinch in with an experience of her own. She hates it because she sometimes feels like the other person might understand her camaraderie as an attempt to one-up them, but it’s really the only way she knows how to genuinely relate with people. By being vulnerable too.

    Somehow Kate thinks Yelena will appreciate it though - she always has, so far - so clearing her throat she ventures, “My dad had always been big on holidays. The Fourth of July, Thanksgivings, Christmas, New Years, you name it. He truly enjoyed gathering all his friends and family together, chatting them up, dancing, singing, making toasts… but he would always find me at some point during the party to take me up into our roof, just the two of us. Maybe because he kind of knew I used to get a bit overwhelmed, or because he actually also needed a break, I don’t know.”

    A soft tap against her wrist brings Kate out of her own head. She links her fingers with Yelena’s and swallows to try to sound a bit less choked up as she continues, “The thing is that the first Christmas after he passed, mom did every single thing possible to make it seem like everything was as it used to. She got everyone together and did the decorations just like he used to and bought the presents and all that jazz. And I had fun, I did. But then there was just this moment when I was mindlessly looking around that I realized I was waiting for him to come get me, and I think that was the first time it really hit me, you know? That he was truly gone and that the small things I used to share with him were gone with him, too.”

    There’s wetness in her cheeks and there’s wetness in her collarbones, and Kate doesn’t need Yelena to say anything to know the other girl is listening. To know she gets it.

    “Does it ever get better?” Yelena tentatively asks. There’s apprehension in her voice, as if she was honestly scared to know the answer, so Kate kisses gently her temple and tightens her arms around her as she answers, “It does. Generally, when I think about him now it’s always a happy feeling that comes with it. But I won’t say it ever really goes away because that would be a lie. There are still random moments every now and then, years later, that I feel his absence so acutely it hurts. And that’s normal, babe. Grief is not linear, it’s ups and downs that we just learn to deal with.”

    Silence stretches on and Kate’s almost positive Yelena must be either on the brink of sleep or already there, her breathing a steady pattern of inhales and exhales hitting the skin of her chest. The brunette is about to reach around to hoist her up and take Yelena to bed when the other girl mumbles, “You know I love you, right?”

    For a second Kate wonders if the older girl is already asleep and talking deliriously, but when she tilts her head down she finds deep green eyes staring right back at her, clear and still a bit shiny from her tears.

    “Of course I do, babe. And I love you too,” Kate swiftly responds, not an ounce of hesitation in her tone. She lets her shoulders drop when Yelena nods against her shoulder, the blonde’s cheek resting against Kate’s sweatshirt.

    “It’s just - you remember when we went over to my parent’s house and my father asked to speak with me?” Yelena pauses to look over at Kate, waiting for the younger girl’s shake of her head before continuing, “He said a lot of the same stuff about how I had always been easily influenced by Natasha and how he used to be proud of me and how he loved me and so he couldn’t understand why I had chosen not to fight this. He seemed so upset, Kate, as if he truly couldn’t wrap his head around it. So I just told him I loved you. He chuckled and said that it wasn’t love. But I really think it is. I know it is,” the Russian moves to face Kate, her rosy cheeks a sharp contrast with the fierceness shining in her eyes, “I guess I just wanted you to know, too. That I don’t regret this, and I love you.”

    How had they started their afternoon with a marathon of Sex and the City and ended up having a breakdown while their dinner got cold in the kitchen Kate had no idea. She just knew she wouldn’t trade this for the word.

    “You’re amazing,” Kate replies when she realizes she’s been silent for too long, “I know, babe. And I love you too, I do. Just like your sister does, and Wanda does, and our friends and my family. Anyone with a single functioning brain cell would be privileged to love you, Yel.”

    Noticing the way Yelena tries to blink away the stinging in her eyes, Kate decides it’s time to diffuse the heaviness around them. She reaches over to grasp Yelena’s phone again and grins, “And speaking of, you know who else really loves Mr. and Mrs. Smith?”

    “My sister’s crush on Angelina Jolie is embarrassing,” Yelena mutters as she takes the device from Kate’s hand, but there’s a smirk dancing on her lips as she scrolls through her contacts to change the recipient of the still written message.

    Kate smiles at the tug of affection she feels inside her chest at the sight, but quickly rearranges her features in disbelief, “Excuse me? No crush on miss Jolie could ever be anything but normal.”

    “Aw, you too,” Yelena coos sweetly as she pats Kate’s cheek twice smirking, “Don’t worry malyshka, I won’t tell.”

    “What even do you have against Angelina Jolie?” Kate grumbles as she crosses her arms petulantly, rolling her eyes when Yelena snickers and mumbles something about being a cliché lesbian. She can’t keep her own smile away though, because they’re out of the deep for now, so if Yelena poking fun at her is the price she has to pay Kate will happily oblige.

    “You have to twist it to the right,” Wanda instructs somewhere in between the third and fourth floor, her voice echoing through the flight of stairs while Yelena retorts from below, “No, no. Try to tilt the bottom half towards the wall, Natasha. Turn it left.”

    Neither Kate nor Natasha can see them, but according to the older redhead’s fed-up eye roll, Kate’s positive she’s not the only one absolutely done with this.

    They’ve been trying to get a new sofa inside Kate’s - and now, Yelena’s - apartment for the best part of the last hour. Or rather, Kate and Natasha had been struggling under the furniture’s weight while Yelena and Wanda self-appointed themselves as overseers of the whole ordeal.

    Kate’s pretty sure Natasha is just one more impossible command away from snapping and honestly, the brunette’s rooting for it. God knows Kate’s way too soft for them to do it herself so really, their physical integrity lies entirely on Natasha’s notoriously short temper.

    “Why couldn’t we get this up with the elevator again?” Natasha ruefully mumbles as she attempts to shift her hold of the rear legs of the cursed furniture. The movement almost makes Kate stumble down, the younger girl struggling to keep her nails buried into the soft material as she answers, “Because your sister insisted she wanted the three-seat sofa, and obviously this thing wouldn’t fit inside the elevator unless we took off the roof or something.”

    For a beat, Natasha seems to contemplate the idea of finding a way to strip away the roof of her building’s elevator. “That’s not the left,” Yelena singsongs just as the pair manage to climb three steps in a row, and then Kate begins to understand the appeal of disassembling a whole lift just to put an end to their misery.

    Miraculously they make it to Kate’s floor without either rolling down the stairs or crossing paths with a bewildered fellow resident, and when Natasha realizes they won’t have to take the door out of its hinges just to push the thing inside Kate swears she sees a tear rolling down her cheek.

    There’s also a high chance of it being a droplet of sweat though, so she doesn’t mention it at all. She gets both, honestly.

    Wanda and Yelena begin to gush about the lovely way their new piece fits inside the living room, heatedly discussing the merits of buying matching red cushions to combine with the dark maroon leather of the sofa. Kate’s pretty sure the words blend and burgundy and sparkly get thrown into the conversation, but she’s too busy rummaging through the cabinets in search of her beloved vodka to care all that much.

    “You know we’re going to get dragged into a shopping spree for house stuff just because you couldn’t convince Lena to keep the old sofa you guys had, right?” Natasha complains as she drops next to Kate into an empty stool. Her eyes shine with gratitude when the younger girl presents a shot glass full to the brim though, so the grumbling is half-heartedly at best.

    Frowning, Kate drowns down her own liquor and tries to keep a straight face - because there wouldn’t be anything more embarrassing than grimacing while drinking vodka with her sister-in-law who also happens to be very cool and very Russian - and simply asks, “What?”

    “See that?” Natasha says as she points her thumb over her shoulder towards Yelena and Wanda. The two of them are eyeing the living room as Yelena animatedly gestures with her hands and Wanda nods effusively, her eyes wide and her grin as big as the blonde’s as the Russian reaches over to take a hold of the curtains. Kate can’t help but smile affectionately at the sight, but her bubble bursts immediately when Natasha continues, “They’re already plotting. Now Lena will want to buy more stuff to decorate and Wanda will help and she’ll get ideas for our apartment. And that will land us all on a five-hour trip to Ikea with them going wild over literally everything while we drag carts full of shit behind them.”

    If Natasha didn’t look so downright worried (and if she wasn’t still a bit imposing) Kate would have surely laughed and thought it was a joke.

    But just as she’s debating what to say next, Wanda calls from behind the living room table, “Baby, we’ve got to find some of these covers for our chairs!” Then, Yelena snaps her fingers and adds, “And we need a new rug, Katyushka. The puppies have destroyed the old one.”

    That’s the extent of their acknowledgment of Natasha and Kate before they burst into a chorus of dates and places they should try to visit. The older redhead turns her head slowly towards Kate with an arched eyebrow, the younger girl shrugging her shoulders lamely as she lifts the half-empty vodka bottle as a solution.

    “Hit me, sister,” Natasha sighs as she wiggles the glass, clinking Kate’s empty one with hers before throwing back the shot.

    Barely managing to conceal her squeal of joy behind a carefully faked cough, Kate mentally thanks the strong drink for giving her an excuse to avoid looking like a pathetic schoolgirl just because she’s commiserating with the one and only Natasha Romanoff.

    Natasha reaches over to pour more drinks as Wanda walks over to her side and Yelena moves to wrap her arms around Kate’s middle, and the brunette can’t help but think that a full day roaming inside a gigantic furniture store with them doesn’t actually sound that bad anyways.

    Living together proves to be way less of a challenge than either of them could have ever dared to hope. They work around each other easily, knowing when to give the other some space and when to seek the other’s company, and not even sharing roof with two very messy, very hairy dogs brings up irreconcilable brawls.

    Of course, Yelena always yells at her when Kate leaves a trail of little drops of water when she leaves the shower, or when she leaves in a rush and forgets to take out the trash for like, two days in a row, or when she systematically works through all her cutlery instead of using and washing what she uses, or when she plays ball inside with Lucky and Fanny and they end up smashing two picture frames and a lamp to the ground, or when -

    Anyways, the point is that they always find a way to make it work, and all in all, sharing an apartment in the city with Yelena turns out to be the dream Kate had never known she had until she was living it.

    There was something about coming home after a dreadfully long day at the office, attempting to make sense of every single duty her mom had upon herself while trying her best (and pretty regularly failing, too) to fit in Eleanor’s shoes, only to find the other girl messing around the kitchen, a lovely scent filling the room and hitting her square on the nose. Or about waking up to a warm body resting on her side, a heavy arm draped across her stomach and a mess of blonde hair filling her view. Or about spending Sundays cooped up on their sofa watching old movies and sharing ice cream. Or about going grocery shopping together and feeling disgustingly domestic while struggling to fit the stuff on the cabinets afterward.

    When Yelena’s internship gets extended into a one-year deal it wasn’t a question where she would be staying.

    No, living together under the same roof hadn’t been an issue. Being together on the outside though, that had proven to be a bit trickier than Kate had anticipated. Because Yelena had overcome a lot of the shame and the guilt she used to carry back when they had met, and they’d held hands and hugged and even at some point kissed in front of strangers, but there was something about them being more serious - about their relationship being more serious - that sometimes, irretrievably, put the older girl on edge.

    Today had turned out to be one of those days.

    It was a Saturday. Summer had bled into autumn but the heat refused to leave. They had been strolling through Central Park, hand in hand while their puppies raced ahead of them. Kate balanced their leashes on the crook of her elbow while she licked the remains of her ice cream cone (and yes it would have been way easier to let go of Yelena’s hand for a bit like the blonde so wittily had pointed out but Kate could manage like this so there was no need, thank you very much) when they had stumbled upon a group that seemed to be taken straight out of a cult.

    The men were wearing shirts and ties even under the humid heat of New York, and the ladies struggled to hold onto their children while the kids attempted to rush away to the swings.

    No one said anything to them directly, but there really was no need. Kate had seen that look of disgust and disapproval a bunch of times before, knew pretty well what those people were thinking when they eyed their linked fingers with revulsion.

They wait until they lose sight of blonde hair and a red backpack. They wait until the panel above their heads signals that Yelena’s plane has departed.

    And once there’s no more reason to wait, Kate finally cries.

    She cries all through the drive back to her apartment. She cries while Wanda makes her dinner and Natasha walks Lucky and Fanny. And she cries when she burrows under the covers that still carry a scent that’s irrevocably Yelena’s. She cries until she falls asleep.

    Then the blonde texts her somewhere around nine in the morning, a simple I’m here with a picture of her beautiful, tired face attached to the text, her smile so big and blinding as it has ever been, and Kate smiles, a burst of full-chest laughter suddenly erupting from her throat when Yelena sends yet another picture making a cross-eyed face back at her.

    Kate shoots back another text with a picture of her still-sleepy face with an affectionate Showstopper, breathtaking, heartbreaker, reply to Yelena’s picture, and gets out of bed. She works methodically around the bedframe, pulling out the sheets and carrying them to the washing machine before pulling out a fresh bedspread from the closet and laying it on top of the mattress.

    Sitting down and closing her eyes, she leans back on her palms and focuses on breathing.

    Kate counts each inhale and exhale, and once she makes it to four-thousand and thirty-eight she clicks her tongue and begins to move.

    And that’s their ending.
