    anyways i won't ramble anymore, thank you everyone who's stick around to see this conclusion, i absolutely adore you xxxx XXVI - On the Radio Chapter Notes On the Radio - Chip Taylor See the end of the chapter for more notes Sometimes, if Kate really allows herself to stop to think about it, it surprises her how true the fact that life always carries on is. There are certain moments in life where it seems like the world has stopped turning and time has stopped still and the mere idea of things ever being okay again feels almost like a betrayal. But then you wake up one day and the pressure in your chest feels a little less heavy, the urge to cry only hits you once or twice a day, and you find yourself actually laughing out loud and meaning it.

    Some people would be quick to state that someone like Kate Bishop, an only daughter to a millionaire family, wouldn’t know shit about sorrow. Then again, Kate’s always been one to defy cliches.

    The first time she ever had to grieve was when she was thirteen and she’d lost her father to a brutal three-year battle with cancer, so she’s quite confident that grants her enough credit to state that she knows her way around pain.

    Maybe that’s precisely why what actually surprises her about the end of her relationship with Yelena is how much it actually hurts and not the fact that she does get her life together and going in the end. Because the latter she expected, knew would happen because she has overcome way worse. The former, though, caught her completely off guard.

    The two of them had wordlessly agreed that it was realistically impossible to maintain a relationship while living on literal sides of the word for (at least) two years, but they hadn’t truly broken up either. They had left things in this weird nowhere ground that drove Kate to wonder where they would stand were they ever to be in the same timezone again.

    It was a thought Kate tried not to entertain too much, however, because the possibility of actually meeting again while still being on the same page was one that the brunette was mostly sure would be a one-in-ten-thousand-lifetimes type of deal probably, so it was better not to dwell on that.

    They had begun texting every day at first, mostly to keep up with Yelena’s settling-in process and because the Russian was still very new (and very intimidated, whether she would admit it or not) to her new life. Then life inevitably began to pick up for the two of them, and one day turned into two and then bleed into three to finally morph into weekly and later monthly checkups. They spoke about work and Kate mentioned their friends every once in a while even though Yelena still kept in touch with most of them.

    And Yelena always sent pictures of every single European city she visited. Postcards, too.

    (Kate wasn’t ashamed to admit she had cleared a whole drawer in her bedside table to store each of them. Whenever she was feeling especially emotional she’d pull them out and picture Yelena walking the streets of Barcelona or dancing somewhere in Santorini, and she’d feel confident in her decision of letting go again.)

    There was never a conversation about seeing other people, not once and not prompted by either of them. It was something that was wound to happen, realistically, but no matter how much Kate wanted to remain a part of Yelena’s life there would always be things she would simply prefer not to know. It was one thing accepting it abstractly and a whole other having to hear about it textually.

    (This truth most likely went both ways, too.)

    So yeah, Kate never thought she’d die once Yelena left because she sadly knew from experience that no one ever truly dies for somebody else. But there were moments nonetheless, small, stupidly simple moments where she’d see something or hear something or smell something and she’d have this strong urge to share it with the blonde.

    It was precisely those moments where she was reminded that heartbreak was a very real thing, no matter how non-physically lethal it may be. And it was during those moments that she was reminded of how cruel grief was, because the worst day wasn’t the first or the second or even the tenth. The worst day was a regular day when something inconsequential happens and your first instinct is to tell the other person only to remember that’s not a thing you can - or should - do anymore.

    Nevertheless, as she knew would happen, those moments started fading too. Sure, it may have taken her almost half a year to even consider dating again - because somewhere in her mind there was a deeply-rooted belief that it would still be a betrayal anyway -, but once she did Kate’s reminded that she’s actually a people’s person.

    She thrives in new spaces and in knowing new people, so even if at least two-thirds of the women she sets up dates with end up being only very good acquaintances she can’t really complain. Besides, there’s the other one-third that do end up being really nice companions even if nothing more serious happens, so there’s that too.

    All in all, a year goes by and Kate feels like her life is pretty much together even if there’s still a Yelena-shaped hole in her heart. She pushes forward nevertheless because she’s pretty much confident that it will never fade anyway, so there’s no point in waiting around.

    Eleanor gets better and then worse and then better again, and eventually she’s discharged even if she’s going to be on meds that could easily knock out a horse for at least another year.

    That same night they go out to celebrate and her mom waters down her pills with a dry martini and a blinding, all-teeth grin on her face, and Kate’s reminded of how much she loves this woman yet again.

    Her mom comes back to the office and for a hot minute Kate worries that things will change, that she’ll be stripped out of her duties, or that Eleanor and her will end up clashing over different points of view.

    And don’t get her wrong, it does happen. Sort of. They disagree and they fight and they challenge each other, but Eleanor respects her and treats her as an equal, as someone who’s set to take the lead once she’s out of the company for good, and that’s frankly everything Kate could ever ask of her mother.

    Eleanor is as fierce as she ever was, almost as if this past year and a half hasn’t touched her. Kate feels a little more invincible with her mom by her side, knowing there’s a battle that they’ve won.

    At some point in between Christmas and the end of March Kate ends up singing for classes, too, because she’s found out she actually enjoys learning when there’s not a huge pressure behind it. She takes up archery again just for the fun of it, and she sings up for French lessons at NYU too, because she’s absolutely done with Carol poking fun at her by sending memes in foreign languages only to laugh at her when she doesn’t instantly get them.

    The older blonde had moved down to New York at some point in between Christmas and the end of March too, thanks to a job promotion, and that first update text had sparked the beginning of a true friendship between the two.

    (There had also been a slip-up - or four, if Kate was completely honest - before they mutually agreed that they worked way better off as friends. Plus, shortly after, that enigmatic ex from Carol’s past that she had once set free had come back too, so now when they met sometimes Maria tagged along and Kate got the chance to tease Carol for being absolutely wiped.

    She’d never mention that seeing them together triggered something raw inside her chest, choosing instead to smile wistfully at them while trying not to think about a set of deep emerald eyes blinking back at her.)

    All in all, life for Kate was going surprisingly well. She’d had dinner with Natasha and Wanda, hang up with M.J. and Peter whenever they came down on a family visit, take a drive up to New Haven to bother Pietro for a while, call her godfather at least once a week, discuss work stuff with her mom during dinner under Jack’s reproving - but adoring - stare, and meet new people along the way. There are new relationships, some that last a week and others that make it up to a couple of months, and even if nothing feels like a long-term deal Kate tries to enjoy each and every one.

    That’s actually how, without even noticing, a whole semester goes by and she’s actually stuck trying to memorize the conjugation form of French verbs, reciting the endings up in her head while struggling not to mess up the pronunciation. She’s riding the subway on a late afternoon in early December, the city beginning to gear up for Christmas, and she has trouble believing it’s been almost two years since she moved here again.

    Some days it feels like she never left. On others, it’s like she’ll perpetually be a stranger.

    After a short while, Kate begins to doubt she’s even chanting her lesson correctly, so with an exasperated huff she moves her woolen scarf to the side to take a look at her notes. It’s then that out of the corner of her eye she catches sight of a neatly folded piece of paper dancing right in front of her.

    She’d love to say she would never fold her notes but something she’s never been able to improve has been her messiness while taking notes, so with a resigned sigh she places her folder and laptop on the empty seat next to hers and bends down to pick the stray paper.

    Knowing her luck it will be exactly the one she’s been looking for, too.

    Right as she makes to unfold the piece of paper the next stop gets announced and the doors to the wagon slide open, a bunch of tourists forcefully pushing their way in while an elderly couple waits patiently on the platform. Kate’s so busy side-eying the rude newcomers after smiling politely at the elders that she almost misses what’s written on the note altogether.

    Almost.

    Her heart stops and her blood runs burning hot before a freezing sensation takes over her body from head to toe. She feels the palm of her hands grow damp as she wets her lips, blinking furiously at the words glaring back at her.

    Coup the foudre, (French), (noun) - A sudden unforeseen event, in particular an instance of love at first sight.

    It can’t be right. It can’t be true. What are the fucking odds?

    Kate closes her eyes anxiously, rubbing a hand down her face as she concentrates on her breathing. This must be her mind playing tricks on her. It has to. Chances are that when she opens her eyes again the words that will greet her will be a bunch of poorly-scribbled French vocabulary and she’ll have to chuckle at the way her mind is treacherously betraying her.

    But then she blinks twice, three, four times, and the lines on the paper refuse to shift as she gulps and cautiously runs her thumb across the elegant scrawl. Her brain is still trying to catch up, trying to make sure she’s not conjuring this up in her head, when suddenly another thought hits her right on the chest.

    This handwriting… she’s seen it before. She knows it. By heart.

    Turning around frantically, she forces down the urgent need to run that’s suddenly burning at the sole of her feet and the tip of her fingers. She clings to the note with a death grip and stands, takes a few steps towards the other back of the wagon, and gives herself a second to hope.

    Then she reaches the end and the only blonde hair she sees belongs to that lovely old lady who got on the subway next to her husband a couple of stops ago. Kate kind of wants to slap herself because of course Yelena is not here. Of course, this was just a wicked coincidence the universe is throwing at her and now she’s most likely gotten her laptop and notes stolen because who on their right mind leaves their stuff unattended on the subway just to follow a stupid hunch -

    “I figured your easily distracted brain may have forgotten to look it up, so I thought I’d write it down for you,” a husky voice calls tentatively from behind, and Kate’s a big enough person to admit she actually needs to clasp her hand around handrail to keep her knees from giving up under her own weight.

    Whipping her head around, Kate’s immediately met with a pair of warm green eyes already staring at her. It’s no surprise at all that they’re as beautiful as she remembers them.

    They look at each other for what’s probably an inappropriate amount of time considering they’re in a very public space, and Kate debates whether to laugh or cry or kiss her or slap her for not telling her she was coming here while trying to get a hold of her emotions and a good read on her whirlwind of feelings.

    Instead, the brunette settles for an astonished, “I’m actually studying french now, you know.”

    Her mind stops reeling the second she hears Yelena burst out into surprised laughter, the sound so familiar - so goddamned comforting - a very special kind of calmness settles over her. It feels terribly like finally coming home.

    Then Yelena stops giggling, still looking at Kate with that same glint in her eyes from all those years ago, that glint it took Kate so long to understand, and when the Russian takes an uncertain step forward Kate doesn’t hesitate to meet her halfway.

    They throw their arms around each other almost desperately, Kate clinging to the older girl still half convinced that she’ll suddenly wake up in her bed all alone. Yelena mumbles fondly, “Kate Bishop,” and the taller girl squeezes her so hard she’s almost sure the tips of Yelena’s shoes leave the ground, but if the delighted squeal from the blonde is anything to go by she’s as thrilled as Kate herself feels.

    “Are you real?” Kate can’t help but whisper as she hides her nose on the Russian’s hair, inhaling the musky scent that’s always been so characteristically Yelena’s. She gets her answer when the blonde barely chuckles and twists her arm around to pinch the skin on Kate’s wrist tenderly.

    “Ow!” the younger girl whines, but her pout is completely overridden by the elated grin she lets out when she sees Yelena’s self-satisfied smirk and the unmistakable fondness in her eyes. “I fucking missed you, Yel,” Kate admits then, a fact so true it slips past her lips without a second thought.

    She doesn’t get the chance to feel self-conscious about the eagerness in her voice because the blonde mumbles, “So did I,” with such conviction it erases any kind of doubt left in Kate’s mind about being alone in this.

    Pulling apart reluctantly, Kate circles her fingers around Yelena’s wrist and drags her back to the seat she was occupying before, miraculously still holding her stuff. The blonde moves her fingers down to link them with Kate’s and as she sits down, she tries not to dwell too much on how natural the mere act of holding hands and touching skin feels even years later.

    “What -“ Kate begins then, stammering over her own words as she attempts to phrase her thoughts. What are you doing here, why are you here, when did you get here, how long have you been here, how did you find me, how long will you be staying - And most importantly: “Why wouldn’t you tell me you were coming to New York?”

    A shadow of something that reads a lot like doubt washes over Yelena’s face, the older girl biting relentlessly at her bottom lip as her eyes wander around the wagon before finally settling over Kate’s face again. “I thought about it,” Yelena carefully explains, “But I just - I didn’t want to put any pressure on you.”

    “Yel, oh my god. What kind of pressure would you ever be putting on me?” Kate practically yells, her brain trying - and failing - to follow the other girl’s reasoning process. Unable to fully comprehend she’s there, within reach from her.

    “Katyushka,” Yelena breathes out that nickname so reverently it forces goosebumps to burst all across Kate’s skin. The Russian places her free hand on top of the one that’s already holding Kate’s, bringing them over to her lap as she runs her thumb tenderly over the brunette’s pulse point. It’s then that Kate realizes for the first time that Yelena looks choked up, a nervousness so unusual for someone so kept together oozing off her body in waves, her demeanor crumbling as the seconds tick by.

    It takes Yelena eight deep, even breaths to get her resolve together and speak again, “Kate, last time… Our relationship was complicated. Difficult. I made it difficult because I was not in a good place. But you waited for me. You were patient and encouraging and you were there. Until I had to leave. Again,” Yelena rushes out, and her voice would be swallowed by the incessant noise of the railways was not Kate hanging onto every word, “I was the one to make the decision to leave, and you supported me because you wanted what was best for me.

    Now I’m back, but I couldn’t - I wouldn’t come here expecting you to make room for me in your life again after almost two years, Kate. I didn’t want you to change anything you’ve done in your life during the time we’ve been apart just so I could fit in there again. I want to do things right this time.”

    Infuriating. That’s the first word that comes up in Kate’s mind to describe the woman sitting next to her. Yelena is infuriating. Infuriatingly frustrating, infuriatingly beautiful, infuriatingly smart. Infuriatingly good, too. Infuriatingly selfless.

    The worst thing is Kate can see where she’s coming from. She can, from an objective point of view, understand the way Yelena has thought about this.

    Yelena is worried Kate may have already moved, meet someone new - or be in the process -, and she doesn’t want that to stop just because they’re sharing a city again. She doesn’t want to dislodge Kate’s life again after she voluntarily took herself out of it when she decided to leave.

    Maybe, Kate thinks ruefully, maybe I would do the same, where things reversed. Maybe I already did, back then, when I decided to give her the time she needed to fully come to me when she was ready. If she ever was.

    So with that last thought in mind, Kate opts for the only reasonable answer: “Does that mean you’re back in the city then?”

    An astounded chuckle forces its way past the blonde’s lips as Yelena eyes her wondering if Kate’s really going to downright ignore the clearly heartfelt speech. Still, trying to keep her disbelief at bay, the older girl answers, “Yes. They moved my position back to the New York headquarters so I could do my doctorate here at Columbia.”

    A delighted squeal echoes across the wagon as Kate throws her arms around Yelena excitedly, pulling the blonde’s weight over to her own seat, “God, Yel, that’s so - it’s huge, babe. You’re going to be a fucking doctor!”

    There’s a tiny hint of bashfulness glinting on Yelena’s prideful grin, just the smallest amount that lets Kate know the other girl is actually preening under her the brunette’s praises.

    Just the small amount that actually encourages Kate to go all the way and add, “And you’re going to be here.”

    That’s all it takes for Yelena’s face to shift into a tentative smile, all her distinctive bravado gone as she unconsciously tightens the hold on Kate’s fingers. “Yes. Here,” Yelena mumbles, almost as if she had trouble believing it herself.

    They stay transfixed in each other’s eyes for a short breath, the two of them smiling softly as their hands stay clasped together. Then Kate blinks and looks around, realizes they’re way past the stop she’s meant to take to get to her French lessons, and decides that she wasn’t going to pass anyway.

    There will surely be another chance to resit the exam, the younger girl reasons easily with herself as she turns to Yelena again, this is a once-in-ten-thousand-lifetimes situation.

    “You know, there’s a cafe near the next station that serves one of the best hot chocolates in town. Are you doing anything right now?” Kate suggests confidently, trying not to dwell on the fact that Yelena had clearly intended to meet her there, right on this subway.

    Tilting her head to the side, the blonde answers, “I’ll have to check my schedule.”

    “Don’t be an ass,” Kate complains as she shoves Yelena on the shoulder playfully. And then, because she can give as good as she gets, the brunette grins mirthfully and asks, “Speaking of schedules -“

    “Oh, nyet,” Yelena groans as she realizes just what Kate’s about to say next.

    “- How did you know I’d be on this exact same line, at this exact same time? I’d like to know how you managed to plan such a romantic second first meeting,” Kate teases, standing up and pulling Yelena to her feet by their still joint hands. The taller girl only lets go of Yelena’s fingers in favor of draping a protective arm around the blonde’s shoulders to make sure they don’t get lost while getting down at the platform.

    If she mindlessly leaves it there after - and if Yelena reaches a hand up to interwoven their fingers together again - well, that’s just a cause and effect sort of thing.

    “Wanda told me you take this line to get to NYU, she told me you two sometimes meet before your lesson to have a drink,” Yelena admits demurely, “The rest was just patience and luck, I guess.”

    Clicking her tongue against the back of her teeth, Kate can’t help but smile as she recalls a now distant conversation. Words like Columbia, Brooklyn, chocolate, or bravery flash through her mind as the cold winter wind hits her cheeks. Most of the Christmas decorations are already on, their lights casting shadows across Yelena’s rosy cheeks.

    “So, Columbia,” Kate muses out loud, wondering if the other girl will understand. If Yelena remembers, too.

    “So, New York University,” the Russian shoots back easily, a knowing, tender smile on her face.

    “So, hot chocolate,” Kate continues, her smile already a full-out grin at the elation coursing through her veins right now. She’s debating whether or not to ask Yelena to pinch her again, just to make sure this is real and happening when the blonde skips on her tiptoes to leave a chaste, affectionate kiss on her cheek.

    It works just as wonderfully as a pinch would, so Kate doesn’t need to use her words.

    Humming, Yelena takes a step ahead and says, “And after we can go dancing. Or to the cinema. And we’ll go see your parents, and meet with my sister and Wanda.” And then she turns around, looks at Kate thoughtfully and with full intent, her emerald orbs glimmering with conviction as she speaks again, “We can do anything you want to. I told you, I’d be brave to be with you when we meet again.”

    Now it’s Kate’s turn to be rendered absolutely speechless. Her feet halt, her throat closes up, her mouth dries and her heart tugs at her ribcage, and the only thing she can’t come up with is that same damn word.

    Infuriating.

    Yelena is infuriatingly perfect. Infuriatingly meant for her.

    Tilting her head to the side, Yelena eyes her curiously and asks, “Are you coming?” as she tightens her hold around Kate’s fingers with her own, and Kate can only answer, “Yeah.

    Always,” before squeezing back and placing a closed-eyed kiss on top of silk-soft blonde hair.

    They continue walking then, Kate’s arm around Yelena’s shoulders. Hands clasped together as they make their way down a busy avenue illuminated by colorful Christmas lights, their bodies pressed close together as they warm each other up.

    There are probably thousands of things that will have to be said and conversations to be had. For now, though, Kate chooses to focus on the feeling of Yelena’s weight against her chest when the blonde boldly reaches up to tenderly graze their lips together after looking bashfully up and mumbling, “Mistletoe.”

    Everything else can wait then, as their lips met again after years of being apart but still feeling as if they’ve known each other for more than one lifetime. Their eyes meet, and they smile.

    And that’s their beginning.
